ABOUT THIS REPORT
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 1
REPORTING PERIOD 
 
The reporting period covers 1 January 2025 to 31 December 
2025 (“2025”).  
 
REPORTING CYCLE 
 
Annually. 
 
REPORTING STANDARDS AND FRAMEWORKS 
 
This report has been prepared in accordance with applicable 
Malaysian regulatory requirements and ﬁnancial reporting 
standards, and with reference to relevant governance, 
sustainability and integrated reporting frameworks. 
 
These include: 
 
• Main Market Listing Requirements of Bursa Malaysia 
Securities Berhad;  
• Malaysian Financial Reporting Standards;  
• International Financial Reporting Standards (“IFRS”);  
• Malaysian Code on Corporate Governance 2021;  
• FTSE4Good Bursa Malaysia Index criteria;  
• Global Reporting Initiative Standards 2021;   
• United Nations Sustainable Development Goals; 
• Bursa Malaysia’s  Enhanced  Sustainability  Reporting 
Framework; 
• ISO 26000:2010 Guidance on Social Responsibility; 
• Sustainability Accounting Standards Board; and 
• National Sustainability Reporting Framework. 
 
The National Sustainability Reporting Framework requires 
companies listed on Bursa Malaysia to adopt IFRS S1 and IFRS 
S2. IFRS S1 sets out the general requirements for 
sustainability related ﬁnancial disclosures. IFRS S2 focuses on 
climate-related disclosures. Together, they build on the 
principles of the Task Force on Climate-related Financial 
Disclosures and relevant industry guidelines, aimed at 
strengthening the connection between sustainability, 
strategy, risk management and ﬁnancial performance. 
 
Genting Berhad has adopted the IFRS Sustainability 
Disclosure Standards for the ﬁrst time in its reporting. The 
International Sustainability Standards Board ("ISSB") 
Sustainability Statement is available in the Integrated Annual 
Report 2025. The disclosures are aligned with the mandatory 
implementation timeline set by Bursa Malaysia, while utilising 
the available transition reliefs. 
REPORTING SCOPE AND BOUNDARY 
 
This report covers Genting Berhad (“Company”) and its 
portfolio of companies, collectively referred to as the Genting 
Group (“Group”). 
 
The portfolio comprises the Group’s principal operating 
companies in which the Company has direct ﬁnancial control 
or holds an equity interest of 50% or more and are listed as 
follows: 
 
Listed principal operating subsidiaries: 
• Genting Singapore Limited (“Genting Singapore”) 
• Genting Malaysia Berhad (“Genting Malaysia”) 
• Genting Plantations Berhad (“Genting Plantations”) 
 
Unlisted principal operating subsidiaries: 
• Genting Energy Limited (“Genting Energy”) 
• Resorts World Las Vegas LLC (“Resorts World Las Vegas”) 
 
The content of this report is aligned with the published 
annual reports of Genting Malaysia, Genting Singapore and 
Genting Plantations and includes the reporting of Genting 
Energy and Resorts World Las Vegas. 
 
This report provides an update of the sustainability 
performance,  commitments  and  progress of the Group 
covering environmental, social and governance (“ESG”) in 
2025. 
 
The sustainability data disclosed covers the Group, unless 
stated otherwise. 
 
“Our Feature” sections in this report highlight the key ESG 
initiatives  and  strategies  implemented  by  the  Group’s 
operating subsidiaries in 2025. 
 
The Group measures Greenhouse Gas (“GHG“) emissions 
using the GHG Protocol: A Corporate Accounting  and 
Reporting Standard (2004), applying the ﬁnancial control 
approach to deﬁne its organisational boundary. The 
operational boundary and related disclosures are provided 
in the Climate Change, Energy and Emissions Monitoring 
section.
Genting Berhad presents its tenth standalone Sustainability Report, which provides updates on 
the progress made on sustainability issues that are material to its stakeholders. This 2025 annual 
edition reaffirms Genting Berhad’s enduring commitment to sustainability, a principle embedded 
in its core values since the founding of the Genting Group in 1965. 
 
The Company’s dedication to sustainability disclosures is reﬂected in its reporting efforts since 
1982. This report supplements the Sustainability Statement in the Integrated Annual Report 2025 
of Genting Berhad. 

=== PAGE 4 ===

BOARD APPROVAL 
 
This report was reviewed and approved by the Executive 
Committee and Board of Directors of Genting Berhad, with 
endorsement by resolution on 24 March 2026. 
 
ASSURANCE 
 
Selected ﬁnancial data in this report can be 
veriﬁed  against the audited ﬁnancial statements 
in the  Integrated Annual Report 2025 of Genting 
Berhad,  which have been assured by 
independent external  auditors. 
 
Internal Review 
 
To ensure the accuracy and integrity of sustainability 
disclosures, this report was reviewed by the Company’s 
sustainability working teams and the Executive Committee. 
Selected sustainability matters and indicators of this report 
have been subjected to an internal review by the Company’s 
internal auditors. 
 
The scope of the internal audit was expanded in 2025 to cover 
additional sustainability matters and indicators.  
 
Please refer to the Statement of Assurance by Internal 
Auditors on page 131 for the detailed scope and subject 
matters of the audit review.  
 
External Assurance  
 
Within the Group, Genting Singapore has engaged an 
independent external veriﬁer to provide limited external 
assurance on GHG emissions across Scopes 1, 2 and selected 
Scope 3 categories. 
 
This exercise provides a basis for the Group to assess its 
readiness in relation to the proposed mandatory requirement 
for external reasonable assurance on Scope 1 and Scope 2 
GHG emissions, targeted for annual reporting period 
beginning on 1 January 2027.  
 
 
 
 
 
MATERIALITY 
 
Genting Berhad actively undertakes an annual materiality 
assessment to identify and analyse matters that are important 
to its businesses and stakeholders. This exercise ensures the 
continued relevance of existing material matters in response 
to dynamic changes in the operating landscape. 
 
For details on the Company’s materiality assessment, 
please refer to page 11 of this report. 
 
Inputs from various stakeholders including Bursa Malaysia, 
ESG rating agencies, bond and share investors were 
considered in the preparation of this report. 
 
 
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS & DISCLAIMER 
 
This report includes forward-looking statements relating to 
Genting Berhad’s strategies, initiatives and performance. Such 
statements are based on reasonable assumptions and 
expectations. However , they may be subject to risks and 
uncertainties beyond our control that may cause actual 
outcomes to differ materially. Readers are cautioned not to 
place undue reliance on these forward-looking statements. 
 
ACCESSIBILITY & FEEDBACK 
 
This report is available in digital format on the 
Company’s corporate website at 
www.genting.com. By prioritising digital access, 
the Company aims to reduce paper consumption 
and minimise its environmental footprint. The Company 
remains committed to improving the quality and relevance of 
its reporting. Stakeholders are encouraged to share their 
feedback via gbinfo@genting.com.  
 
For further information, including the Sustainability Policy and 
related disclosures of Genting Berhad, please visit the 
Company’s corporate website at www.genting.com. 
 
The sustainability reports for the listed subsidiaries of Genting 
Berhad are accessible via their respective websites:  
 
Genting Singapore Limited    www.gentingsingapore.com 
Genting Malaysia Berhad       www.gentingmalaysia.com 
Genting Plantations Berhad   www.gentingplantations.com

=== PAGE 5 ===

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 3
DEAR STAKEHOLDERS,  
 
We are pleased to present the 2025 Sustainability Report of 
Genting Berhad, which highlights our ongoing commitment 
to advancing sustainability and creating long-term value for 
the communities and industries in which we operate. 
 
During the year under review, we improved our sustainability 
reporting capabilities by leveraging an enhanced 
sustainability data monitoring tool, which strengthened data 
accuracy and supported alignment with the IFRS S2 climate 
reporting standards. These efforts reinforce our commitment 
to transparent and reliable disclosures while ensuring that our 
sustainability framework remains closely aligned with the 
Group’s vision, mission and values. 
 
We are also pleased to inform that Genting Berhad was 
included for the ﬁrst time in three FTSE4Good indices: the 
FTSE4Good Bursa Malaysia Index, FTSE4Good ASEAN 5 Index 
and FTSE4Good Emerging Index, effective 22 December 2025. 
 
In addition, Genting Berhad received a 4-star ESG rating, the 
highest rating category awarded by Bursa Malaysia and a 4-
star recognition under the 2025 National Corporate 
Governance and Sustainability Awards. These recognitions 
reﬂect our strong governance practices and proactive 
sustainability efforts. Furthermore, Genting Malaysia, Genting 
Singapore and Genting Plantations have remained  
constituents of the FTSE4Good Index, underscoring the wider 
Group’s commitment to long-term value creation.  
 
CHAMPIONING SUSTAINABLE TOURISM 
 
Genting Singapore continues to lead in sustainable  tourism, 
maintaining an “AA” MSCI ESG rating in 2025. For  four 
consecutive years since 2021, the company has  maintained 
as the ﬁrst in the world certiﬁed for both the  Global 
Sustainable Tourism Council’s Destinations Criteria  and 
Industry Criteria for Hotels. 
 
Hotel Ora further  strengthened this performance by receiving 
two notable  accolades: the regional ASEAN Green Hotel 
Award and the  Singapore Hotel Sustainability Award. 
Together , these  achievements underscore the company’s 
excellence in  environmental performance and sustainable 
hotel operations  within Singapore. 
 
UPHOLDING GOOD GOVERNANCE 
 
Governance remains a core pillar of Genting Berhad’s 
sustainability framework, reﬂecting our commitment to 
transparency, accountability and long-term resilience. Our 
directors proactively engaged on sustainability matters, 
ensuring their effective integration into our business strategy. 
 
We uphold a strict zero-tolerance approach against bribery 
and corruption, consistently communicating this stance to 
stakeholders. The Group reported zero major incidents of 
bribery and corruption in 2025. 
 
 
ADVANCING DECARBONISATION 
 
As an investment holding company, Genting Berhad does not 
directly cause signiﬁcant environmental impact. However , the 
environmental performance of our operating subsidiaries can 
inﬂuence the overall economic performance of the Group. 
 
We continue to reinforce our commitment to environmental 
stewardship, aiming for net zero emissions by 2050 and 
aligning climate targets with national objectives.  
 
In conjunction with the Genting Group’s 60th anniversary,  we 
convened a group-wide sustainability webinar in September 
2025 to strengthen climate preparedness and reinforce future 
readiness. 
 
Our climate ambitions are supported by our operating teams’ 
green technology strategies, which aim to reduce carbon 
emissions. These strategies focus on the adoption of 
renewable energy, the deployment of advanced technologies, 
system upgrades and the implementation of sustainable 
practices, while fostering energy-saving practices among our 
workforce. 
 
The alignment with IFRS S2 climate-related disclosures has 
further enhanced transparency and strengthened our 
approach to managing climate-related risks and 
opportunities, ensuring robust governance and accountability 
in our sustainability strategy. 
 
The integrated resorts of tomorrow will be deﬁned by the 
impact they create. This conviction continues to guide the 
ongoing transformation of our leisure and hospitality 
operations. 
 
Genting Singapore continues to improve its energy efficiency 
through infrastructure upgrades and performance 
optimisation. Despite higher visitor arrivals, the resort’s 
carbon emissions intensity in 2025 remained 24% below its 
2015 baseline, demonstrating meaningful progress in its long-
term decarbonisation strategy. 
 
Genting Singapore remains ﬁrmly committed to advancing its 
2030 Sustainability Master Plan and is on track to achieve a 
30% reduction in total emissions intensity across Scopes 1, 2 
and 3 by 2030, while continuing to deliver positive socio-
economic value. 
 
The solar network at Resorts World Sentosa was expanded to 
18 sites in 2025, resulting in an increased generation capacity. 
Facilities for staff transportation and electric-vehicles charging 
were enhanced across the resort, supporting lower-emission 
mobility for both operations and guests. 
 
The launches of WEAVE, The Laurus and Singapore 
Oceanarium in 2025 marked major milestones in advancing 
our sustainability journey, demonstrating that guest 
experience, commercial performance and environmental 
stewardship can go hand in hand. 
MESSAGE FROM CHIEF EXECUTIVE,  
PRESIDENT AND EXECUTIVE DIRECTOR
OVERVIEW

=== PAGE 6 ===

MESSAGE FROM CHIEF EXECUTIVE,  
PRESIDENT AND EXECUTIVE DIRECTOR
OVERVIEW
GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 20254
Resorts World Genting is steadily progressing toward its 
target of a 12% reduction in electricity consumption by 2028, 
achieving a cumulative reduction of approximately 10.7% in 
2025 compared with its 2018 baseline. 
 
Genting Malaysia remains steadfast in upholding 
internationally recognised environmental management 
standards, maintaining ISO 14001:2015 certiﬁcation across all 
engineering functions at Resorts World Genting and the entire 
operation at Hilton Miami Downtown. 
 
Building on this foundation, Resorts World Genting 
strengthened its resource efficiency in 2025, achieving  
reductions of more than 35% in food waste and around 48% 
in plastic waste through recycling and waste-reduction 
initiatives. These efforts will continue to expand in scale, 
enhance operational efficiency and reinforce the Group’s 
commitment to environmental stewardship. 
 
Water stewardship remains a priority for the Group. Resorts 
World Las Vegas, located in Southern Nevada, achieved a 3% 
year-on-year reduction in overall water consumption and a 
waste diversion rate of nearly 40% in 2025. It has also  
strengthened its decarbonisation progress by operating on 
100% renewable energy. Biodiversity and natural landscape 
preservation efforts are expanding as well, supported by 
community-focused initiatives such as the annual Earth Day 
celebration and ladybug release. 
 
Genting Plantations continues to uphold sustainable palm oil 
production, adhering to the standards set by leading 
certiﬁcation bodies, including the Roundtable on Sustainable 
Palm Oil, Malaysian Sustainable Palm Oil and Indonesian 
Sustainable Palm Oil. These standards promote responsible 
land use, minimise environmental impact and ensure full 
compliance with No Deforestation, No Peat, No Exploitation 
principles. All certifications were maintained in 2025, 
reinforcing our commitment to sustainable practices. 
 
Genting Plantations’ ﬁrst biogas power generation facility at 
Genting Ayer Item Oil Mill  was commissioned in November 
2025, marking an important step in reducing carbon 
emissions and supporting the use of clean energy across the 
Group. 
 
Genting Energy successfully handed over its 2 x 393-megawatt 
subcritical coal-ﬁred power plant to the Fujian local 
government upon the expiry of the 21.5-year power purchase 
agreement on 30 June 2025, marking a strategic step towards 
building a cleaner and more sustainable energy portfolio. 
 
This commitment is further demonstrated by the commercial 
operation of the Dongwucha 120-megawatt-peak solar power  
plant and the ongoing development of a hydrogen-ready gas-
ﬁred power plant in Zhoushan, China. These initiatives reﬂect 
our focus on building a resilient and future-ready energy 
portfolio. 
  
 
FOSTERING POSITIVE IMPACT 
 
Our Group remains committed to uplifting local communities, 
contributing over RM49 million in donations and in-kind 
support in 2025. 
 
In life sciences, TauRx Pharmaceuticals Ltd has made good 
progress as its UK marketing authorisation application for 
hydromethylthionine mesylate, an oral anti-tau therapy for 
Alzheimer’s disease, has been accepted by the UK Medicines 
and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency and is under 
review. 
 
Genting TauRx Diagnostics Centre Sdn Bhd continues to 
advance cognitive health. Building on the launch of HiPAL and 
HiPAL Pro in 2023, the company is developing a broader suite 
of early diagnostic and monitoring tools for dementia 
patients. 
 
The development of Fontaine Vitale, our Group’s ﬁrst stem cell 
facility  established  in  partnership  with  Celularity  Inc., is 
progressing  in the Sanur Special Economic Zone in Bali, 
Indonesia. The construction of the stem cell facility is ongoing 
and targeted for completion by the end of 2026. This facility 
is expected to be the ﬁrst in ASEAN to offer placenta-derived 
allogeneic stem cell therapy. 
 
We prioritise employee well-being and a safe work 
environment, aiming for a balanced workplace culture. The 
Group recorded zero substantiated complaints related to 
human rights violations or labour standard non-compliance 
in 2025. We will continue to strengthen governance, 
monitoring and employee engagement practices to uphold 
ethical conduct. 
 
While there were no work-related fatalities in most divisions 
of our Group, ﬁve fatalities occurred in the plantations division 
in 2025. We remain committed to achieving zero fatalities 
across all divisions. 
 
MOVING FORWARD 
 
Our sustainability journey continues to advance through the 
adoption of new technologies, strategic partnerships and a 
shared commitment to responsible growth. By aligning with 
global standards and focusing on practical, high-impact 
initiatives, we remain dedicated to creating meaningful value 
for our stakeholders. 
 
I extend my heartfelt gratitude to our valued stakeholders and 
dedicated workforce for their continued support as we work  
together to shape a greener , more inclusive and sustainable 
future. 
 
 
DATO’ SRI TAN KONG HAN 
Chief Executive, President and Executive Director

OUR PROFILE 
  
Founded in 1965 by the late Tan Sri Lim Goh Tong when he 
brought his vision of building a temperate mountaintop resort 
in tropical Malaysia to life, Genting Berhad has evolved from 
its entrepreneurship roots to an investment holding and 
management company with sustainable value creation at its 
core. 
 
Since its incorporation in 1968 and subsequent listing in 1971, 
Genting Berhad has grown into one of Asia’s leading and best-
managed multinationals with investments in the leisure and 
hospitality, oil palm plantations, power generation, oil and 
gas, property development, life sciences and biotechnology 
industries. 
 
Our portfolio spans nine countries around the world, 
managed by our listed subsidiaries Genting Singapore 
Limited, Genting Malaysia Berhad and Genting Plantations 
Berhad, as well as our principal unlisted subsidiaries Genting 
Energy Limited and Resorts World Las Vegas LLC. 
 
Genting Berhad is led by Tan Sri Lim Kok Thay, who is a son of 
our beloved Founder . The family heritage provides Genting 
Berhad with a unique multi-generational perspective in value 
creation. This has translated into a long-term investment 
approach in stewarding our portfolio towards delivering 
strong ﬁnancial returns to shareholders while preserving the 
ecosystem of our planet and supporting our local 
communities. 
  
 
Genting Berhad and its portfolio companies are also known 
as the Genting Group. In its core leisure and hospitality 
business, the Genting Group markets and offers a suite of 
leisure products under a number of premier brands including 
Genting, Resorts World, Genting Grand, Genting Club, 
Crockfords and Maxims. 
 
The Genting Group has tie-ups with established names such 
as Universal Studios, Premium Outlets, Zouk, Hilton and other 
renowned international brand partners. 
 
The intellectual property rights of Genting Berhad, in 
particular “Genting” and “Resorts World” trademarks and 
brand names are valuable company assets that have grown 
in line with the expansion of the Group’s businesses. 
 
The market capitalisation of Genting Berhad was RM11.6 
billion as at 31 December 2025. The Company is listed on the 
Main Market of Bursa Malaysia Securities Berhad under the 
Consumer Products and Services sector . The Company’s 
corporate offices are based in Wisma Genting in Kuala 
Lumpur , Malaysia. 
 
Genting Berhad is committed to continue managing its global 
business investments in a sustainable and responsible 
manner .

OUR ESG RECOGNITIONS
We have received numerous sustainability awards and accolades across our businesses, recognising our long-standing 
efforts to drive sustainability and create positive socio-economic impact.
GENTING SINGAPORE
• Achieved GBAC STAR accreditation, demonstrating 
the implementation of a comprehensive cleaning and 
maintenance programme for the built environment; 
 
• Sharecare Health Security veriﬁed with Forbes Travel 
Guide, ensuring compliance with public health 
protocols and best practices for the safety of guests 
and employees; and 
 
• Crockfords Las Vegas, LXR Hotels & Resorts earned a 
VERIFIED Responsible Hospitality badge, as issued by 
Forbes Travel Guide.
• Achieved 100% certiﬁcation under RSPO, ISCC and 
MSPO in Malaysia; 
• Achieved overall score of 79.8% in the SPOTT 
assessment; 
• Achieved A rating in the MSCI ESG Index; and 
• Achieved  FTSE4Good score of 3.4.
 • Jangi wind farm is registered with the United Nations 
Framework Convention on Climate Change under 
Clean Development Mechanism. 
 
• The Banten power plant has received the Programme 
for Pollution Control, Evaluation and Rating 
(“PROPER”) award (rated as blue) for seven 
consecutive years since 2019 from the Ministry 
Environment and Forestry, Indonesia. 
 
• The Banten power plant has successfully maintained 
all existing ISO certiﬁcations: 
 ISO 9001:2015 Quality Management System; 
 ISO 45001:2018 Occupational Health and Safety 
Management System; 
 ISO 14001:2015 Environmental Management 
System; 
 ISO 50001:2018 Energy Management System; 
and 
 ISO/IEC 17025:2017 General Requirements for 
the Competence of Testing and Calibration 
Laboratories. 
 
• Kasuri block has received the following ISO 
certiﬁcations: 
 ISO 9001:2015 Quality Management System; 
 ISO 45001:2018 Occupational Health and Safety 
Management System; and 
 ISO 14001:2015 Environmental Management 
System.

GENTING MALAYSIA
• Genting Malaysia has maintained inclusion in the 
FTSE4Good Bursa Malaysia Index, the FTSE4Good 
ASEAN 5 Index and the FTSE4Good Emerging Index, 
achieving a score of 4.1 out of 5.0 in 2025. 
• Genting Malaysia has awarded Graduate Employer 
of the Year and ranked among the Top 20 Most 
Popular Graduate Recruiters 2025. 
• Resorts World Catskills has maintained the LEED 
certiﬁcation for sustainable building design and 
operations. 
• Resorts World Genting has maintained the RG Check 
accreditation by the Responsible Gambling Council. 
• Genting Casinos UK received recognition as the Top 
50 Inspiring Workplace 2025. 
• Resorts World Birmingham achieved Bronze 
accreditation under Green Tourism. 
• Resorts World Awana received the TDM Travel Trade 
 Excellence Awards 2025. 
• The Engineering function of Resorts World Genting, 
as well as the entire operation of Hilton Miami 
Downtown, are ISO 14001:2015 certiﬁed.

We deﬁne stakeholders as parties who are impacted by our organisation’s business decisions and activities and as groups 
whose actions and decisions will inﬂuence our business growth. It is important to understand our stakeholders’ needs so that 
we can work together along the value chain, ﬁnd opportunities and address risks. We have mapped our stakeholders’ concerns 
and our responses against our sustainability material matters, allowing us to identify any associated risks and opportunities 
arising from these concerns.

OUR GOALS  
 
Prioritise sustainability considerations in 
all Board deliberations, including strategy 
development, business plans, major 
initiatives and enterprise risk 
management, ensuring long-term value 
creation and resilience.  
 
 
Monitor the ﬁnancial and ESG 
performance of our portfolio 
companies to ensure indirect impacts 
are  effectively managed, supporting 
long-term sustainable growth. 
 
Maintain active and transparent  
engagement with the government  and 
regulatory bodies, through the  
submission of statutory reporting  and 
timely responses to queries,  ensuring 
full compliance with all  applicable laws 
and regulation, and  safeguarding 
operating licenses. 
 
 
 
Provide timely updates on our business 
performance and strategies to maintain 
open and regular communications with 
the investment and media communities. 
 
 
 
 
 
Build good working relationships with 
our lenders and protect our strong 
credit standing to ensure continuity of 
our operations, ensuring clear terms, 
timely repayment and compliance with 
loan conditions are key priorities. 
 
Foster an engaged, committed and 
highly competent workforce by 
empowering our employees to reach 
their full potential, contributing to the 
creation of a high-performing organisation.

As part of its stakeholder engagement, Genting Berhad held 123 meetings with lenders (bankers, bondholders and rating 
agencies) and investors (shareholders, equity analysts and potential investors) in 2025. 
 
Our Annual General Meeting was held physically in 2025, in compliance with the Securities Commission Malaysia’s guidelines. 
We engage regularly with lenders and investors to ensure that we remain in a strong ﬁnancial position to address potential 
risks and opportunities. 
 
All material company announcements are published through Bursa Malaysia. Stakeholders and the public can also access 
these company announcements and other corporate information about the Company and the Group on Genting Berhad’s 
official corporate website at www.genting.com. 
 
Genting Berhad also keeps abreast of regulatory developments and shareholders’ concerns through its membership in 
associations such as the Federation of Public Listed Companies and the Minority Shareholders Watch Group. 
Genting Berhad’s official corporate website: www.genting.com

OUR MATERIAL MATTERS
Genting Berhad actively undertakes an annual materiality assessment to determine and analyse sustainability matters that 
are important to its businesses and stakeholders. This approach aims to reﬂect the evolving priorities within its operational 
landscape, enabling the Group to identify opportunities and mitigate risks.  
 
Genting Berhad conducted its materiality assessment survey in 2025 to determine the relevance of the Company’s 17 material 
matters prioritised in the previous year , engaging with both internal and external stakeholders. The internal stakeholders 
included Genting Berhad’s Board of Directors, heads of departments and portfolio companies, while the external stakeholders 
invited to participate, included Bursa Malaysia as well as the Company’s investors and suppliers.
Materiality Assessment Survey 
 
The online survey, conducted from September to October 2025, invited 66 
stakeholder respondents to provide feedback by ranking and rating the 17 material 
topics from their perspectives, while considering the interest and importance of 
these topics to Genting Berhad’s other stakeholders. Of the 66 stakeholder invitees, 
42 respondents provided their feedback, resulting in a response rate of 64%.
01
Validation and Endorsement of Materiality Matrix 
 
The materiality matrix and the ﬁndings from the assessment survey were reviewed 
and deliberated at Exco. They were later submitted to the Board for ﬁnal validation 
and approval.
03
Materiality Analysis and Prioritisation 
 
The survey feedback on the topics was compiled and analysed using statistical 
methods. To enhance the validity of the results, varying weightings based on best 
practices were applied when assessing core feedback from different stakeholder 
groups. This analysis provided valuable insights that informed the identiﬁcation of 
key material matters. These material matters were subsequently mapped onto a 
materiality matrix to determine their priority for Genting Berhad’s sustainability 
efforts.

Referencing the disclosures of speciﬁc topics to the portfolio companies
OUR MATERIAL MATTERS
While Genting Berhad’s portfolio companies are given the autonomy to manage their unique sustainability priorities based 
on their respective operations, the Company supports them through ongoing engagement and communication on the 
alignment of Group-wide sustainability objectives. 
 
Each portfolio company is encouraged to drive the broader sustainability agenda through its own strategies and initiatives. 
Genting Berhad’s Sustainability Policy serves as a guiding framework for the Board and Management, outlining the 
commitment to managing global business investments sustainably and responsibly. The Sustainability Policy complements 
the Company’s Sustainability Framework, ensuring alignment with its sustainability objectives. The Sustainability Policy is 
available  on the corporate website at www.genting.com. 
 
Referencing Genting Berhad’s material matters to its portfolio companies 
The Company’s 17 material matters were mapped to the relevant topics of its portfolio companies, in accordance with Genting 
Berhad’s Five Sustainability or ESG pillars: Governance, Economic, Environment, Community and Workplace. Based on the 
2025 materiality assessment, all 17 previously identiﬁed topics remained material and relevant. No new material topics were 
identiﬁed in 2025. Ethical Business Conduct, Cybersecurity and Data Protection, and Corporate Governance and Risk 
Management emerged as the top three material matters.

VISION
MISSION
We are a leading multinational corporation committed to enhancing shareholder value and maintaining long-term sustainable 
growth in our core businesses.
We will: 
be responsive to the changing demands of our customers and excel in providing quality products and services; 
be committed to innovation and the adoption of new technology to achieve competitive advantage; 
pursue personnel policies which recognise and reward performance and contributions of employees and provide 
proper training, development and opportunities for career development; 
generate a fair return to shareholders; and 
be a responsible corporate citizen, committed to enhancing corporate governance and transparency, including 
undertaking social responsibility for the enhancement of the standard of living of the country.

WHY IT 
MATTERS
 Effective corporate governance and robust risk management are essential to safeguarding the Group’s global 
investments and ensuring long-term value creation. Without these, the Group risks exposure to heightened 
vulnerability, regulatory breaches, diminished stakeholder trust and potential social and environmental harm. 
 
In response, the Group focuses on embedding robust governance practices, ensuring compliance with regulatory 
requirements, maintaining a safe workplace and actively supporting the communities and countries in which it operates. 
These measures are integral to the Group’s responsibility in managing its investments and delivering sustainable value.
   SUSTAINABILITY GOVERNANCE 
 
At Genting Berhad, sustainability is a fundamental aspect of the Company’s corporate governance. It is governed using a top-
down approach, led by the Company’s Board of Directors. The sustainability framework is designed to embed sustainability 
considerations into the Company’s operations and decision-making processes. It is integrated into its corporate governance 
and risk management functions, supported by its audit and risk management processes. 
 
Board of Directors 
 
The Board takes an active role in prioritising sustainability considerations when executing their duties, which encompass the 
development of company strategies, business plans, major initiatives and risk management. The Board attends ongoing 
training on sustainability-related issues, including climate-related topics. The Board is supported in its oversight role by th e 
Executive Committee  (“Company Exco“) and the sustainability working team. 
 
Sustainability considerations are incorporated in all decision-making processes at Board level, including across all Board 
Committees, comprising the Audit Committee, Risk Management Committee, Nomination Committee and Remuneration 
Committee. The Audit Committee and Risk Management Committee comprise independent directors with appropriate ﬁnancial 
and accounting expertise, enabling effective oversight of ﬁnancial reporting, internal controls, risk management, external 
audit, and sustainability-related ﬁnancial disclosures. Details of all directors are disclosed in Genting Berhad’s Integrated Annual 
Report 2025, in the Directors' Proﬁle section. 
 
The Board has seven members, comprising three non-independent executive directors and four independent non-executive 
directors. The Board comprises one female director (14%) and six male directors (86%). The ethnicity of the Board is 14% Indian 
and 86% Chinese. One director (14%) aged between 30 and 50 years, while the remaining six directors (86%) are aged above 
50 years. 

=== PAGE 21 ===

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE AND RISK MANAGEMENT
EMPOWERING GOOD GOVERNANCE
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 19
  SUSTAINABILITY GOVERNANCE (cont’d) 
 
Management 
 
The Company Exco drives the strategic management of the material sustainability matters, including climate-related topics of 
Genting Berhad. Speciﬁcally, the Company Exco reviews and makes executive decisions on material issues and business 
strategies, including sustainability-related matters in monthly meetings with the heads of departments and business units of 
the Company. The Company Exco reports all relevant matters to the Board of Directors for deliberation. 
 
As a member of the Group Executive Committee ("Group Exco"), the Company Exco engages in discussions and decision-
making on material issues and strategies including sustainability-related matters during its monthly meetings. The Group Exco 
also includes executive leadership an d senior management from Genting portfolio companies. 
 
The Risk and Business Continuity Management Committee (‘RBCMC”) takes a proactive role in managing the Company’s 
sustainability-related risks. Consisting of the Company’s senior management, the RBCMC convenes quarterly to ensure the 
continual effectiveness, adequacy and integrity of the risk management system. Any key risk matters, including sustainability-
related risks, are escalated to the Board’s Risk Management Committee and the Company’s Board of Directors for deliberation 
and approval. 
 
The Chief Executive and President, who also serves as an Executive Director of the Company, chairs both the monthly Company 
Exco and Group Exco meetings, as well as the quarterly RBCMC meetings. He is also the acting Chief Sustainability Officer for 
Genting Berhad, focusing on the strategic management of material sustainability matters and integrating sustainability 
considerations across the Company’s operations. 
 
Sustainability working team 
 
The sustainability working team of the Company manages the day-to-day implementation and coordination of material 
sustainability matters. The team comprises representatives from all departments of the Company involved in the management 
and reporting of ESG-related data and information and is coordinated by the Corporate Sustainability and Communications 
department, which oversees the preparation of the Company’s annual sustainability report.  Together with the heads of 
departments at Genting Berhad, the sustainability working team plays a key role in executing the Company’s sustainability 
initiatives. 
 
To ensure comprehensive and consistent group-wide reporting, the Corporate Sustainability and Communications department 
engages and coordinates with the subsidiary sustainability working teams to collate and consolidate ESG data, identify material 
ﬁndings and propose recommended actions to the Company Exco for deliberation and guidance.   
 
The subsidiary sustainability working teams are responsible for managing and implementing their respective sustainability 
initiatives, including initiatives that are tailored to the speciﬁc nature and risks of their operations, while aligning with t he 
Group’s overarching sustainability framework. 
 
Sustainability-linked remuneration 
 
Genting Berhad has embedded climate-related considerations within its Sustainability Policy, which governs the identiﬁcation 
and management of material sustainability risks and opportunities. The Company is currently developing its approach to 
strengthen the linkage between remuneration outcomes and sustainability-related performance measures at senior executive 
level. This reﬂects a phased and governance-led approach, subject to data availability, measurability of metrics and appropriate 
internal approvals.  
 
At operating level, selected subsidiaries have incorporated ESG-related key performance indicators (“KPIs”) into their 
remuneration frameworks for senior management and, in certain cases, executives. These KPIs are aligned to material 
sustainability priorities and support accountability for sustainability performance across the Group’s operations.

Berhad.
• Comprises all departments of Genting Berhad that handle ESG 
data and information and is managed by the Corporate 
Sustainability and Communications department to prepare the 
annual sustainability report. 
• The Corporate Sustainability and Communications department 
engages with the sustainability working teams of the Company’s 
subsidiaries to collate and consolidate ESG data, present material 
ﬁndings and propose sustainability-related actions to the 
Executive Committee for deliberation and guidance. 
• Sustainability working teams from subsidiaries manage their 
respective sustainability initiatives, some of which are unique to 
their operations.  
• Institutionalises the risk management practices in the 
respective business units of Genting Berhad. 
• Ensures the effectiveness of the risk management policies and 
processes. 
• Identiﬁes and reviews relevant material risks including 
sustainability risks and ensures appropriate actions are taken.
• Execute and integrate sustainability initiatives as part of the 
daily operations. 
• Monitor and communicate feedback to the Executive 
Committee and the sustainability working team of Genting 
Berhad.

WHY IT 
MATTERS
Upholding high standards of ethical business conduct is crucial in building and maintaining stakeholder trust while 
mitigating systemic risks. It enables us to establish partnerships and collaborations across our investment activities. 
 
As a responsible investment holding company, Genting Berhad is committed to practising the highest standards of 
ethics and integrity, across our investments and stakeholder engagement. By fostering a culture rooted in trust, 
accountability and compliance, we ensure that Genting Berhad continues to create sustainable value for our 
stakeholders. 
OUR APPROACH 
 
Genting Berhad remains guided by its Code of Conduct and Ethics for Employees and Directors (“the Code”). The Code, which 
is available on the Company’s corporate website at www.genting.com, outlines the expected behaviour of all its employees 
and directors, including those of its unlisted subsidiaries, such as:
A safe and fair workplace 
 
 
Protection of company information,  
records and assets 
 
 
Duties of good faith, diligence and integrity, 
including declaring conﬂicts of interest, avoidance 
of bribery and corruption, insider trading, money 
laundering, fraud and unfair competition practices 
Interaction on social media and with members  
of the traditional media 
 
Expected conduct with internal and  
external parties 
 
Security responsibility 
 
Whistleblower procedures 
Furthermore, all directors and employees of Genting Berhad are mandated to sign an Integrity Pledge and declare their 
conﬂicts of interests. Directors of the Company are also required to comply with the Company Directors’ Code of Ethics by the 
Companies Commission of Malaysia. 
 
To formally engage third parties, the Company has established a comprehensive engagement process with due diligence. 
Third parties who intend to conduct businesses with the Company are required to complete a Due Diligence Questionnaire as 
part of the on-boarding process. They are also required to acknowledge and comply with the Company’s Code of Business 
Conduct for Third Parties and will report any acts of bribery and corruption through the Company’s Whistleblower channels.

   ANTI-BRIBERY AND CORRUPTION SYSTEM (“ABCS”) 
 
Genting Berhad enforces a strict zero-tolerance policy towards bribery and corruption by complying with Malaysian anti-
corruption laws and regulations. Since its establishment on 1 June 2020, the ABCS Manual has served as a key reference for all 
employees and individuals associated with the Company in ensuring compliance with anti-bribery and corruption laws. The 
ABCS Manual addresses all forms of bribery and corruption, covering actions such as offering, promising, giving, accepting or 
soliciting any undue advantage or gratiﬁcation. This includes both ﬁnancial and non-ﬁnancial beneﬁts, whether conducted 
directly or indirectly and regardless of location. The ABCS manual is available on our corporate website at www.genting.com. 
 
The ABCS is overseen by the Risk and Business Continuity Management Committee, comprising the Company’s senior 
management, and the Board’s Risk Management Committee. 
 
The Company adopts a proactive approach by creating a high-level of awareness of the ABCS among its employees. This is 
done through providing training on ABCS annually and incorporating ABCS awareness into the induction programme for new 
hires. Additionally, e-posters on key policies of the ABCS Manual are sent to all employees quarterly.

   WHISTLEBLOWING POLICY AND PROCEDURES 
 
Genting Berhad’s Whistleblower Policy outlines a comprehensive whistleblowing mechanism, allowing employees and other 
stakeholders to report concerns about improper conduct or detrimental actions without fear of reprisal or intimidation. The 
Whistleblower Policy is available to all employees via the Company’s intranet and accessible by the public through the corporate 
website at www.genting.com. 
 
Reports on any improper conduct or detrimental action received through our various whistleblower channels are reviewed by 
the Whistleblower Committee comprising Genting Berhad’s Senior Independent Director and Chief Financial Officer . If the 
report requires further investigation, an investigation team may be formed to conduct an inquiry. 
 
Employees and stakeholders are welcome to report any potential misconduct via the following channels, which are directed 
to the Whistleblower Committee: 
 
• by completing the forms and submitting them in a sealed envelope marked “Private & Conﬁdential” to the Secretariat of 
the Whistleblower Committee; 
• by ﬁling complaints directly to any Whistleblower Committee member or the Secretariat; or 
• by sending an email to whistle@genting.com. 
 
For detailed information on the scope, disclosure channels and the conﬁdentiality as well as protection of whistleblower , visit  
https://www.genting.com/whistleblower/. 
 
 
   ANTI-COMPETITIVE BEHAVIOUR 
 
Genting Berhad remains fully compliant with the Malaysian Competition Act 2010, which prohibits anti-competitive agreements 
and the abuse of a dominant position in the Malaysian market. We are committed to complying with all applicable laws and 
upholding the principles of fair competition in all business dealings, as outlined in Genting Berhad’s Code of Business Conduct 
and Ethics.  
 
We enforce a strict zero tolerance stance against anti-competitive behaviour , including price ﬁxing, bid rigging, abuse of 
dominant  position or any practices that distort fair market conditions. Employees and external stakeholders may report 
suspected  misconduct through a conﬁdential whistleblowing mechanism, ensuring protection from retaliation and 
contributing to transparent  conduct oversight. Genting Berhad recorded zero pending or completed legal actions related to 
anti-competitive behaviour and  zero violations of anti-trust and monopoly legislation. 
 
 
  LEGAL AND REGULATORY COMPLIANCE MATTERS 
 
Genting Berhad remains committed to upholding the highest standards of legal and regulatory compliance as part of its 
broader  governance responsibilities. At the company level, Genting Berhad recorded no cases of litigation, penalties or 
regulatory  enforcement actions in 2025. 
 
At the Group level, two isolated matters were identiﬁed in 2025. Both matters are not expected to have any material impact 
on the  Group’s ﬁnancial performance or operations. The ﬁrst relates to a litigation matter involving Genting Americas Inc., a 
subsidiary of  Genting Malaysia, in which a motion to dismiss an amended complaint was ﬁled on 12 September 2025. Based 
on external legal  advice and the status of the proceedings, Genting Malaysia maintains that the allegations are without merit. 
 
The other matter was an administrative ﬁne issued to PT Susantri Permai, a subsidiary of Genting Plantations for forestry land-
use  compliance issues. The matter has been fully resolved and does not affect Genting Plantations’ climate  transition strategy 
or its broader sustainability commitments. As of the reporting date, there are no other pending material litigation  cases 
involving the Group.

SUPPLY CHAIN – ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT 
 
Genting Berhad remains committed to responsible sourcing 
practices that drive energy efficiency, reduce environmental 
impact and support the transition to a low-carbon economy. 
 
As a conglomerate with a diverse range of businesses, we 
recognise the inﬂuence we have across our value chain and 
expect our suppliers and partners to align with our 
sustainability principles, particularly in addressing climate 
change, promoting responsible resource use and conserving 
the environment. These principles include: 
 
• GHG emissions reduction: suppliers are encouraged to 
disclose any proactive measures taken to monitor , measure 
and reduce their GHG emissions, aligning with global 
climate goals and industry best practices; 
 
• sustainable energy use: suppliers are encouraged to 
transition to renewable energy sources, improve energy 
efficiency and minimise reliance on fossil fuels in their 
operations; 
 
• water conservation: suppliers are encouraged to disclose 
any water-efficient technologies and practices implemented 
to reduce water consumption and prevent water pollution; 
 
• biodiversity protection: suppliers must ensure that their 
sourcing practices do not contribute to deforestation, 
habitat destruction or loss of biodiversity; 
 
• waste reduction and resource use optimisation: 
suppliers are encouraged to adopt circular economy 
principles to ensure sustainable use of raw materials, 
minimise waste generation or reduce excessive resource 
consumption and implement eco-friendly production 
methods to maximise efficiency; 
 
• pollution  prevention:  suppliers  are  encouraged to 
disclose any measures taken to prevent pollution, including 
responsible waste disposal, emissions control and the 
reduction of hazardous substances; and 
 
• compliance with environmental regulations:  all 
suppliers must adhere to national and international  
environmental regulations and standards, ensuring 
responsible sourcing practices that align with Genting 
Berhad’s sustainability commitments. 
 
 
These environmental principles targets to integrate with 
suppliers through communication initiatives and training 
programmes if needed, to ensure relevant parties  
understand and align with our sustainability principles. 
 
Group-wide, Genting Berhad targets to ensure that the 
environmental supply chain policies of its portfolio companies 
are aligned under a uniﬁed framework while addressing 
industry-speciﬁc considerations. This integration includes: 
 
• Leisure & Hospitality (Genting Singapore, Genting 
Malaysia and Resorts World Las Vegas):  
 
Commitment to sustainable tourism practices, green 
procurement, water conservation, waste reduction and 
energy-efficient resort operations. 
 
• Plantations (Genting Plantations): 
 
Commitment  to  responsible  sourcing  of  palm oil, 
deforestation-free supply chains and sustainable land 
management. 
 
• Energy (Genting Energy): 
 
Commitment to cleaner energy solution investments, 
carbon reduction strategies and responsible resource 
management. 
 
By establishing a consistent environmental policy across  
its portfolio companies, Genting Berhad ensures that 
sustainability principles are embedded throughout its global 
supply chain while allowing for tailored strategies that align 
with each industry’s unique challenges and opportunities. 
 
 
WHY IT 
MATTERS
Supply chain management is critical to Genting Berhad as it directly impacts the Company’s sustainability commitments, 
operational efficiency and regulatory compliance. Effective supply chain management is essential for ensuring 
environmental sustainability, operational efficiency and regulatory compliance across all subsidiaries. A well-managed 
supply chain helps reduce environmental risks, ensures responsible sourcing and aligns with the Company’s climate 
and biodiversity goals.

SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT
Genting Berhad upholds transparency and accountability as essential elements of effective environmental stewardship. We 
encourage and support our suppliers in monitoring and reporting any environmental impact reduction through audits, 
benchmarking and incentives programmes that promote transparency. 
 
Environmental supply chain policies are integrated across the supplier network through communication and, where necessary, 
training of relevant supplier staff to ensure they understand and uphold responsible sourcing standards. 
 
Environmental reporting results provided by suppliers are evaluated and monitored. Any environmental violations must be 
reported promptly. Upon identiﬁcation of non-compliance, corrective actions are enforced, including a remediation plan with 
clear timelines, participation in mandatory sustainability training to address gaps and enhanced monitoring for persistent 
issues. Contractual consequences include suspension or termination for severe or repeated non-compliance. 
 
The Group actively participates in various industry initiatives, global sustainability frameworks and collaboration efforts to 
promote responsible supply chain management practices and address environmental impacts within their supply chains. 
 
Notable examples include: 
• Responsible Sourcing and Supply Chain 
 
Genting Singapore is committed to integrating 
environmental, social and corporate governance 
principles into its supply chain management. The 
company ensures that all suppliers adhere to its 
Supplier Code of Conduct, guidelines and social 
practices, including:  
 
➢ sustainable procurement: prioritising suppliers 
who meet environmental, labour and ethical 
standards and adopt responsible practices that 
reduce negative impacts across their operations; 
 
➢ advocacy: motivating suppliers to adopt  
renewable energy, energy-efficient technologies  
and emissions reduction strategies; 
 
➢ environmental compliance: adhering to 
environmental laws and practices, and at all times 
be committed to environmentally-friendly practices; 
 
➢ supplier training and engagement: conducting 
workshops and training programs to educate 
suppliers on best sustainability practices and 
performance improvement; and 
 
➢ ongoing monitoring and evaluation: conducting 
periodic audits to ensure suppliers meet 
sustainability targets and corrective measures are 
taken when necessary. 
 
Supply Chain Inﬂuence •
 
As the founding member of the Sentosa Carbon Neutral 
Network, Genting Singapore partners with Sentosa 
Development Corporation and other industry players on 
sharing resources and expertise in developing large-scale 
sustainability solutions.  
 
These efforts include joint initiatives in waste 
management and energy efficiency, aimed at achieving 
carbon neutrality for Sentosa by 2030. 
 
Genting ProcureBiz 2025 •
 
Genting Malaysia hosted this event at Resorts World 
Genting in October 2025, reinforcing Resorts World 
Genting’s commitment to supplier diversity, sustainable 
sourcing and long-term partnerships. 
 
The event strengthened collaboration between Resorts 
World Genting and the business community by connecting 
internal procurement teams with new and potential 
vendors.  
 
It attracted over 500 visitors and 26 exhibitors, including  
24  local  and  two  international  suppliers, showcasing  a 
range of sustainable, eco-friendly products  and  
innovations  across  Food and Beverage ("F&B") packaging, 
technology and hospitality services.

SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT
Supplier Engagement and Green Building •
 
Resorts World Las Vegas maintains robust supplier 
standards to ensure ethical and responsible practices 
throughout its supply chain. All suppliers must meet the 
standards outlined within the integrated resort’s Supplier 
Code of Conduct. Wherever feasible, the integrated resort 
seeks to provide opportunities for small and medium size 
enterprises ("SMEs") and local businesses. Sustainability 
criteria for all products materials and services are 
incorporated into procurement frameworks and 
sustainable development standards for design and 
construction. 
 
Resorts World Las Vegas collaborates with organisations 
such as Clean the World and US Oil Solutions to implement 
recycling programmes, including recycling streams across 
the property and minimising waste. Continuous 
partnership with the local utility provider , NV Energy to 
ensure 100% renewable energy for resort operations. 
Following its LEED Gold Certiﬁcation, Resorts World Las 
Vegas ensures that all remodels, renovations and new 
buildouts adhere to third-party sustainability certiﬁcation 
standards, with a focus on green building design.  
 
Enhanced indoor air quality is emphasised as a main 
component of green building design, requiring new design 
and development to prioritise enhanced indoor air quality 
strategies. All new development – including the integrated 
resort’s new Team Member Tunnel, set to open in May 2026 
– will be powered by 100% renewable energy. 
 
• Green Energy Projects 
 
Genting Energy is committed to supporting clean energy 
initiatives and actively participates in various renewable 
energy projects. Genting Energy’s ﬁrst renewable 
investment was the Jangi wind farm in India, which 
commenced operations in December 2011.  
 
Following this, Genting Energy expanded its renewable 
portfolio with the 120MWp Dongwucha solar power plant 
in China, developed in collaboration with SDIC Power  
Holdings  Co., Ltd  which  commenced  commercial 
operations on 31 May 2025. 
 
• Sustainable Palm Oil Certiﬁcations 
 
Genting Plantations holds certiﬁcations from the 
Roundtable on Sustainable Palm Oil (and is a member since 
2006), International Sustainability and Carbon Certiﬁcation, 
Malaysian Sustainable Palm Oil and Indonesian 
Sustainable Palm Oil, demonstrating a commitment to 
sustainable palm oil production and supply chain practices.

SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT
SUPPLY CHAIN – SOCIAL IMPACT 
 
As a leading global conglomerate, Genting Berhad remains committed to ethical and responsible business practices, ensuring 
that all suppliers uphold fundamental labour rights, including the prevention of child labour and forced labour , non- 
discrimination, the right to freedom of association and the right to collective bargaining. 
 
Our supply chain management principles incorporate considerations of our portfolio companies and align with international 
sustainability disclosures standards relevant to Genting Berhad. Our approach integrates sustainability principles covering 
environmental, social and governance to promote ethical procurement, operational efficiency and long-term business 
resilience. By fostering responsible sourcing and supplier partnerships, we aim to minimise risks, enhance transparency and 
contribute to a more sustainable future.  
 
The proportion of spending on local suppliers in 2025 was 88% (2024: 52%) for Genting Berhad and 79% (2024:65%) for Group.  
The higher spending on local suppliers was mainly due to the increased engagement of local-based service providers,  reﬂecting 
our efforts and commitments in supporting local community and businesses. 
Compliance with Laws and Standards 
 
Suppliers must adhere to: 
• The United Nations Universal Declaration of Human 
Rights 
• The United Nations Guiding Principles on Business 
and Human Rights 
• The International Labour Organisation Conventions 
• National and local labour laws governing fair 
employment practices 
• Company’s Supplier Code of Conduct 
• Company’s Code of Business Conduct for Third 
Parties 
 
 
 
Prevent Child Labour 
 
Suppliers must ensure that: 
• no individual under the legal minimum working age 
(as deﬁned by local law or 15 years old, whichever 
higher) is employed; 
• no individual under 18 years old is assigned to 
hazardous work that may jeopardise their health, 
safety or moral development; and 
• proper documentation verifying employee age must 
be maintained to prevent underage employment. 
• all recruitment processes include stringent age 
veriﬁcation measures to prevent risk of child labour . 
 
 
 
Prevent Forced Labour 
 
Suppliers must ensure that: 
• no form of forced, bonded, indentured or 
involuntary prison labour shall be utilised. 
• workers are not required to surrender identiﬁcation 
documents or provide deposits as a condition of 
employment. 
• employment contracts are voluntary, transparent 
and legally compliant. 
• workers retain the right to terminate employment 
freely upon reasonable notice.
Key Principles of Our Supply Chain Management 
 
1. Sustainable Sourcing  
a. Prioritising suppliers that adhere to environmental best 
practices, including reducing GHG emissions, energy 
efficiency and responsible waste management. 
 
b. Committing to source raw materials from certiﬁed 
sustainable sources, such as RSPO-certiﬁed palm oil, to 
ensure responsible and ethical supply chain practices. 
 
2. Environmental Stewardship  
a. Encouraging suppliers to align with our environmental 
policies, including climate action, pollution prevention, 
water conservation and biodiversity protection. 
 
b. Driving sustainability through eco-conscious supplier 
engagement programs that promote best practices and 
continuous improvement. 
 
3. Ethical and Regulatory Compliance  
a. Ensuring suppliers comply with local, national and 
international laws, including labour rights, human 
rights (prevent child labour and forced labour), anti-
corruption policies and fair trade principles. 
 
b. Mandating adherence to global sustainability 
frameworks such as the UNSDGs, GRI standards and 
IFRS S1 & S2. 
 
c. Complying with local laws regarding freedom of 
association, ensuring that workers have the right to 
organise and participate in collective bargaining in 
accordance with applicable regulations. 
 
d. Respecting and supporting the right to freedom of 
association, encouraging open dialogue between 
suppliers and workers to uphold fair labour practices. 
 
e. Complying with local laws regarding collective 
bargaining, ensuring that suppliers follow national 
labour laws governing collective agreements between 
workers and employers.
EMPOWERING GOOD GOVERNANCE

=== PAGE 30 ===

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202528
f. Respecting and supporting the right to collective 
bargaining, ensuring that suppliers create an enabling 
environment for fair negotiations and uphold 
internationally recognised labour rights. 
 
g. Complying with local laws on working hours, ensuring 
that suppliers adhere to national regulations on 
maximum working hours and overtime limits. 
 
h. Establishing a clear policy to reduce excessive working 
hours  by  promoting  work-life balance, fair 
remuneration for overtime and responsible workforce 
planning to prevent labor exploitation. 
 
i. Supplier policy addresses equal opportunity and non- 
discrimination: 
(a) committing to create a diverse, inclusive and fair 
working  environment  that  is  free from 
discrimination based on gender , race, ethnicity, 
disability, or any other protected characteristics; 
(b) ensuring that hiring and promotion policies 
promote workplace equality and prevent bias, 
harassment or unfair treatment. 
 
j. Supplier policy supports the right to a minimum or 
living wage: 
(a) compliance with local laws on minimum wage by 
paying workers at least the legally mandated 
minimum wage; and 
(b) commitment to exceed local minimum wage or 
meet a living wage standard to enhance worker 
well-being and promote fair compensation. 
 
k. Supplier policy addresses health and safety standards: 
(a) compliance with local laws on workplace health and 
safety; and 
(b) disclosures of measures for accident prevention, 
safety training and implementation of occupational 
health and safety best practices. 
 
l. Supplier policy addresses animal welfare: 
(a) compliance with local laws and regulations 
regarding animal welfare in sourcing and 
production; and 
(b) establishing principles and guidelines to uphold 
ethical treatment of animals, encourage humane 
farming practices and eliminate  inhumane 
treatment throughout the supply chain.
 
Right to Freedom of Association and Collective 
Bargaining 
 
Suppliers must respect workers’ rights as provided 
by the local laws, to: 
• form and join trade unions of their choice without 
fear of retaliation; 
• engage in collective bargaining to safeguard their 
interests; 
• express concerns regarding working conditions 
without risk of discrimination or disciplinary 
action; and 
• access to open communication channels and 
grievance resolution mechanisms. 
 
 
Non-Discrimination and Equal Opportunity 
 
Suppliers must ensure that: 
• employment decisions, including recruitment, 
promotion and compensation are based on merit 
and not on race, gender , age, nationality, 
disability, religion, sexual orientation or any other 
protected status; 
• a workplace environment that is free from 
harassment, abuse and discrimination is 
maintained; and 
• equal pay for equal work is promoted, aligning 
with fair and ethical employment practices. 
 
 
4. Risk Management and Resilience 
 
a. Conducting periodic supplier audits to identify and 
mitigate supply chain risks, including sustainability and 
operational risks. 
 
b. Strengthening supply chain resilience by diversifying 
sourcing strategies and incorporating risk assessments 
into procurement decisions. 
 
c. Conducting risk assessments regarding sustainability 
issues including environmental and social for: 
(i) potential new suppliers as part of the due diligence 
process; and 
(ii) existing suppliers by identifying those that are high 
risk and require further monitoring or corrective 
actions.

Key Principles of Our Supply Chain Management 
 
5. Supplier Development and Collaboration  
a. Communicating globally to all suppliers, where 
necessary and translating into relevant languages 
using online translator applications. 
 
b. Engaging suppliers through training programmes 
and capacity-building initiatives to strengthen 
sustainability knowledge and implementation. 
 
c. Collaborating with industry partners, non-

agencies  to  drive sustainability improvements 
across the supply chain.  
 
d. Integrating social supply chain policies into buyer 
training,  purchasing  policy  or  supplier  contract to 
ensure responsible sourcing standards are 
understood and upheld by procurement teams. 
 
e. Capacity building for supplier: 
(a) supplier training on social issues to enhance 
awareness and compliance with ethical labour 
practices; and 
(b) supplier mentoring, secondments and sharing of 
best practices to drive continuous improvement 
and reinforce sustainability commitments. 
 
6. Transparency and Reporting  
a. Establishing structured reporting mechanisms to 
monitor and evaluate supplier sustainability performance. 
 
b. Encouraging suppliers on sustainability data disclosure 
and participate in third-party sustainability assessments. 
 
c. Enforcing corrective measures for non-compliance, 
including implementing remediation plans, enhanced 
monitoring and potential contract termination for 
repeated violations. 
 
Supplier Responsibilities 
 
To maintain compliance, suppliers must: 
• enforce strict hiring policies and veriﬁcation 
procedures to prevent child and forced labour; 
• conduct periodic internal audits to ensure 
adherence to these hiring policies; 
• provide  training  programmes on fair labour 
practices and non-discrimination principles; 
• establish grievance mechanisms to address worker 
concerns transparently and fairly; and 
• report any violations immediately to Genting 
Berhad’s management team. 
 
 
Monitoring and Enforcement 
 
• Genting Berhad reserves the right to conduct 
audits and assessments to verify supplier 
compliance with these hiring policies. 
• Suppliers found in violation of these hiring policy 
must take immediate corrective actions or risk 
termination of their business relationship with 
Genting Berhad. 
• A conﬁdential whistleblower mechanism is 
available for workers and other stakeholders to 
report concerns about labour right violations 
without fear of retaliation. 
• Genting Berhad will work with non-compliant 
suppliers to develop corrective action plans where 
possible to promote long-term adherence. 
Group Procurement 
 
Our business units maintain rigorous procurement systems with comprehensive due diligence to ensure that suppliers 
and vendors operate ethically and responsibly. Appointed suppliers and vendors undergo a thorough assessment 
and are required to comply with all relevant laws and the Company’s Code of Conduct as well as Code of Business 
Conduct for Third Parties. 
 
Additionally, our operating subsidiaries mandate that suppliers and vendors adhere to their respective supplier 
policies or statements, which strictly prohibit child labour and forced labour while promoting fair treatment, equal 
opportunity and ethical business practices. 
 
To enhance cost efficiency and ensure consistency, Genting Berhad adopts the procurement system of Genting 
Malaysia and aligns its Supplier’s Code of Conduct with Genting Malaysia’s version. The Code of Conduct 
communicated globally to all suppliers through its corporate website, which is publicly accessible at 
https://www.gentingmalaysia.com/procurement/ and available for translation into the relevant languages using online 
translator applications.

Guided by Genting Malaysia’s Sustainable Procurement Policy and Supplier Code of Conduct, the company embedded ethical 
sourcing, fair labour practices, occupational safety, human rights and environmental stewardship across procurement activities. 
 
Suppliers are encouraged to report their ESG programmes and environmental and social impacts through Ariba proﬁles to maintain 
transparency and track progress. The company conduct comprehensive due diligence, including random audits and site visits to 
monitor compliance. Additionally, adoption of eco-friendly solutions, such as sustainable hotel amenities  were promoted during 
procurement. 
 
Genting Malaysia’s supplier capacity building through training programmes and regular communication, ensuring suppliers fully

Genting Singapore approaches procurement as an opportunity to accelerate the adoption of leading environmental and social 
practices, supporting and uplifting suppliers in capability building and driving innovation to address real-world business cases. 
With reference to ISO 20400 guidelines, Genting Singapore integrate sustainability considerations into its sourcing and award 
decisions. Its strategy is outlined in its publicly available Sustainable Procurement Policy and guided by three pillars: 
 Roadmap to Strengthening Sustainable Procurement 
 
Genting Singapore is developing a comprehensive long-term roadmap to identify and address risks and opportunities through 
product lifecycle analysis. This roadmap will set clear targets for 22 strategic categories, which is in line with ISO 20400 
Sustainable Procurement principles, reviewed and refreshed periodically to mitigate these risks and improve the category 
sustainability. Genting Singapore will complement these efforts by deepening its supplier engagement efforts and introducing 
ESG development programs for its key suppliers, fostering a collaborative approach to integrate sustainability considerations, 
transparency and ethical practices across its procurement ecosystem. 
 
 
OUR PROGRESS 
Genting Singapore’s approach to sustainable procurement is rooted in creating positive impact — uplifting local communities 
and driving changes that matter . Every initiative reﬂects its commitment to a future where business growth and environmental 
stewardship go hand in hand. 
 
Supporting Local Communities 
 
Genting Singapore achieved over 80% of its total procurement spend was attributed to locally registered companies, with two-
thirds are locally owned to businesses with substantial local ownership (over 30%) in 2025. Over 60% comprised of SMEs and 
individual professionals. The company maintained the highest tier accreditation under the SFA Farm-to-Table program for the 
third consecutive year , aligning with Singapore’s updated vision to strengthen food security and resilience under Singapore 
Food Story 2.0. 
 
Driving Responsible Sourcing 
 
Genting Singapore’s overall seafood consumption declined signiﬁcantly in 2025, due to change in guest preferences hence 
resulting in a reduction in responsibly sourced seafood to 54%, below its target of 60%. The company maintain over 80% of 
biodegradable cleaning chemicals. Reduction of ride hailing services and consolidated demand to mass night staff transport 
vehicles using an automated, optimised software solution to consolidate team members who stay in the same area to deliver 
both positive economic and environmental impact. Notable achievement on a full transition to reﬁllable bulk dispenser bottles 
across all Resorts World Sentosa hotels, including newly opened properties in 2025. Around 60 tonnes of audio-visual 
equipment had successfully recycled or sold ensuring the equipment has a second lease of life.

sourcing practices, safety standards, transparency and alignment with recognised sustainability certiﬁcations including 
Malaysian Sustainable Palm Oil (“MSPO”), Indonesian Sustainable Palm Oil (“ISPO”), Roundtable on Sustainable  Palm Oil

Embedding Traceability Practices 
Genting Plantations ensures transparency by informing customers that 
are its materials sourced from responsible sources, supported by 
traceability systems that identify environmental and social risks. The 
company strengthened its long-standing no-deforestation commitment 
by monitoring 11% of fresh fruit bunches (“FFB“) suppliers in 2025 using 
EUDR-aligned mapping, while also assessing key social indicators and 
screening a signiﬁcant portion of procurement for sustainability risks. 
These efforts reinforced its Code of Business Conduct for Third Parties and 
meet growing customer expectations for transparency. 
 
Enforcing Ethical Supplier Standards 
Genting Plantations’ supplier engagement efforts strengthens its 
sustainable supply chain management and supports responsible palm oil 
production, with the FFB and crude palm oil (“CPO”) Supplier Engagement 
Programme driving continuous improvement. The company expect all 
suppliers, including raw material providers, labour agencies, contractors, 
logistics partners and service vendors to uphold ethical and responsible 
practices and incorporate its sustainability requirements into contractual 
agreements where appropriate. 
 
Supporting Local Suppliers 
In 2025, Genting Plantations engaged 3,192 suppliers, with 3,066 local 
suppliers representing 97% of procurement expenditure in Malaysia. A 
strong base of local suppliers supports supply chain stability, contributes 
to local economic development and reinforces its responsible sourcing 
commitments.

Traceability Mechanisms
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS (cont’d)
Supplier Review 
To review and register its suppliers to ensure 
that they meet the company’s  traceability 
requirements. 
 
 
FFB Handling Procedure 
To regulate the method for receiving FFB from 
external sources as per criteria used for 
traceability. 
 
 
Supply Chain Compliance Roadmap 
To engage with Genting Plantations’ suppliers 
that have yet to achieve full compliance. 
 
 
Grievance Handling Mechanism 
To communicate all grievances from 
stakeholders and external parties. 
 
 
Monitoring Reporting and Veriﬁcation of 
Compliance 
To perform ongoing assessment reporting 
and veriﬁcation of registered suppliers to 
ensure continued compliance to Genting 
Plantations’ traceability requirements. 
Supplier Evaluation 
Genting Plantations evaluated supplier performance through 
regular engagement, including meetings, questionnaires, 
traceability submissions, consultations and on-site 
assessments. The company also conducted traceability 
reviews, forced-labour risk assessments and ensure no 
clearing occurs in High Conservation Value (“HCV”) or High 
Carbon Stock ("HCS") areas. 
 
When gaps are identiﬁed, Genting Plantations worked closely 
with suppliers to support corrective actions. Recent 
assessments conﬁrmed that no deforestation-related 
complaints were identiﬁed within its operations or among its 
palm product suppliers. 
Evaluating Compliance 
We assess supplier adherence to key standards such as 
health and safety, workers’ rights, human rights and the 
principles of No Deforestation, No Peat, No Exploitation 
(“NDPE”). This includes governance and alignment with 
our sustainable palm oil policies. 
 
On-Site Assessments 
We conduct site visits and due diligence for FFB suppliers 
to evaluate their commitment to responsible agricultural 
practices and sustainable palm oil production. 
 
Certiﬁcation Readiness 
We review the preparedness of FFB suppliers to achieve 
RSPO, MSPO and ISPO certiﬁcations. 
Non-Compliance Remediation Strategy  
As part of non-compliance remediation strategy, Genting 
Plantations recognised supplier challenges and implemented 
a structured recovery plan to address sustainability risks. 
Non-compliant suppliers receive a six-month grace period 
and are supported on resolution of key issues such as forced 
labour , safety and environmental practices. 
 
In 2025, 37 external suppliers representing 8% of the 463 
external FFB and CPO suppliers screened in Malaysia and 
Indonesia were identiﬁed with minor non-conformances. 
Genting Plantations  reported zero environmental or social  
complaints in 2025. 
Determining High-Risk Suppliers 
High-risk suppliers, identiﬁed through assessments and 
desktop  analysis as non-compliant with NDPE, undergo 
enhanced scrutiny and are engaged in a time-bound 
action plan to meet  our sourcing requirements and 
commitments. 
 
Identifying Shortcomings 
Our approach prioritises engaging with identiﬁed 
suppliers,  recognising that cutting ties is not a 
sustainable long-term  solution. Suspected violations are 
thoroughly investigated, with  purchases from the 
supplier or supply chain temporarily  suspended during 
the investigation process. 
 
Responding to Conﬁrmed Allegations 
Suppliers are required to conduct assessments, provide 
detailed corrective action plans and submit cases to the 
RSPO  grievance channel. While we remain engaged with 
the supplier and stakeholders to improve operations, 
procurement will cease if no progress is demonstrated.
