WHY IT 
MATTERS
As an established investment 
holding and management company 
that has been listed on Bursa 
Malaysia since 1971, we remain 
committed to deliver value for our 
investors while ensuring sustainable 
and long-term growth. By 
embedding sustainability as a core 
driver in our portfolio management 
and investment decisions, we 
strengthen our ability to anticipate 
risks, capture opportunities and 
deliver resilient returns. 
 
Responsible investing enables us to 
support initiatives that create 
meaningful, positive impacts on 
society. Beyond ﬁnancial gains, we 
believe our investments contribute 
to an improved quality of life. 
 
Sustainability integration beneﬁts all 
stakeholders – our customers 
receive products, services and 
solutions that meet their needs and 
expectations; our suppliers compete 
on fair terms; our employees thrive 
in safe and inclusive workplaces; 
while our shareholders and lenders 
gain conﬁdence in the sustainable 
returns generated by our business.  
OUR APPROACH 
 
As an engaged investor with long-term assets, we believe that our portfolio 
companies achieve stronger performance when sustainability considerations 
are embedded into our company culture and strategies. 
 
Our equity holdings  in these companies allow us to be active stewards that can 
inﬂuence these organisations to integrate sustainability into their operations 
and adopt sustainability-driven initiatives that deliver long-term value for us and 
our stakeholders. Our responsible stewardship approach is streamlined into 
three steps.
MONITOR 
 
Ongoing due diligence to assess business performance, 
governance practices, risks and opportunities 
 
 
ENGAGE 
 
Communicate regularly with the Board, Management and 
senior executives on sustainability matters and inﬂuence 
actions 
 
 
ACT 
Leverage equity holdings to vote on sustainability issues, 
drive outcomes and increase or decrease our holdings as 
needed 
Supporting Infrastructure Investments 
 
On 30 June 2025, our indirect joint venture company, SDIC Genting Meizhou Wan Electric Power Company Limited  successfully 
handed over its 2 x 393MW subcritical coal-ﬁred Meizhou Wan Phase I power plant to Fujian Investment & Development Group 
Co., Ltd upon the expiry of its 21.5-year Build-Operate-Transfer power purchase agreement. This handover marks the 
Company’s strategy supporting its long-term commitment to building a cleaner , more sustainable energy portfolio. 
 
This commitment is evidenced by developments under the Power Division, including the joint venture with SDIC Power Holdings 
Co., Ltd (“SDIC Power”) in the 120MWp Dongwucha aquaculture-complementary solar power plant in Fujian, China, which 
commenced commercial operation on 31 May 2025. Additionally, together with SDIC Power , the Power division is developing 
a hydrogen-ready 2 x 745MW gas-ﬁred power plant in Zhoushan, China, with the expected commercial operation date in 2026. 
The capability of Zhoushan gas power plant to ﬁre hydrogen-blended fuel has positioned it to be forward-thinking and 
adaptable to future energy transitions. By being hydrogen-ready today, the Zhoushan gas power plant can seamlessly adapt 
to a cleaner fuel source when hydrogen becomes more accessible, representing a strategic advantage in this rapid energy 
transition landscape.

Impact Investing 
 
We demonstrate our commitment to responsible investment 
through impact investment, especially in life science and 
biotechnology companies at various stages of research and 
development (“R&D”), aimed at discovering new treatments 
and improving the physical and mental well-being of society. 
We are especially focused on the diagnosis and treatment of 
cognitive decline. 
 
We acknowledge that such investments carry higher amounts 
of risk and have potentially longer gestation period before any 
signiﬁcant innovations are achieved. However , we believe the 
potential implications these ﬁndings would have on human 
health and organisational value make our investments highly 
worthwhile. 
 
Our associate company TauRx Pharmaceuticals Ltd is 
developing hydromethylthionine mesylate (“HMTM“), an oral 
anti-tau treatment for Alzheimer’s disease. 
 
 
The Company is currently pursuing regulatory interactions 
and submissions for its oral anti-tau product, HMTM, to seek 
regulatory approval in the US, UK and other territories. 
 
Our investments into these companies run in parallel with our 
other efforts to care for those suffering from dementia. In 
Kuala Lumpur , we established the Genting Dementia Care 
Centre which currently provides daycare services for clients 
living with dementia. 
 
Under our impact investment, Genting Plantations conducts 
R&D to increase the productivity and yield of oil palm trees at 
its plantations.

ASSETS BUSINESS MODEL SUSTAINABILITY
SUSTAINING ECONOMIC VALUE CREATION
GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202538
WHY IT 
MATTERS
As an investment holding company, Genting Berhad’s 
wealth creation is anchored in the resilience of its 
investment assets’ business models. Therefore, sustaining 
the Group’s ﬁnancial performance depends on its portfolio 
companies’ commitment  in  upholding their brand 
reputation, integrity and operational excellence, while 
ensuring their operational approvals are secured.
OUR APPROACH 
 
Approvals and Licenses to Operate 
 
The Group ensures compliance with all regulatory requirements 
and licensing conditions to safeguard the necessary operating 
licenses and approvals granted to carry out its key business 
activities. 
 
Genting and its portfolio companies have implemented internal 
systems to facilitate accurate and transparent ﬁnancial and 
governance disclosures in accordance with the relevant listing 
requirements, regulations and laws to ensure adherence to 
reporting obligations. These systems are overseen by the 
respective company audit departments and Boards. 
 
In addition, Genting and its portfolio companies that operate 
gaming businesses, together with their directors and senior 
management have been assessed as suitable and/or are licensed 
under gaming regulations and laws in all jurisdictions where we 
operate, including Singapore, the Bahamas and New York State. 
We continue our commitment to ensure compliance with 
applicable gambling laws and regulatory standards to avoid ﬁnes, 
penalties, sanctions, suitability reviews or revocation of licenses. 
 
A comprehensive Nevada casino licensing process has already 
been undertaken by Genting Berhad, to ensure that its directors, 
key officers and the relevant subsidiaries involved in the 
development and construction of Resorts World Las Vegas meet 
regulatory requirements. This includes ﬁndings of suitability for 
certain officers and directors of Genting Berhad and the 
registration of Genting Berhad as a publicly traded corporation 
by the Nevada Gaming Commission. 
 
On 15 December 2025, Genting New York LLC has been selected 
for the award of a commercial casino license by the New York 
State Gaming Commission. This achievement reﬂects Genting and 
its portfolio companies’ strong commitment and collective efforts 
in maintaining compliance with applicable gambling laws and 
regulatory standards. 
 
Brand and Reputation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Protecting our brand assets and intellectual property 
(“IP”) is a key component of Genting Group’s risk 
management strategy as these assets are critical to 
ensuring our brand value remains strong and supporting 
our business growth. Our IP rights, particularly our 
“RESORTS WORLD” and “GENTING” brand names are 
valuable company assets. It reﬂects our strong brand 
recognition, especially in Asia, due to their association 
with premier leisure destinations. The Genting brand is 
also widely regarded as one of Malaysia’s top leading 
brands. Usage of our brand assets is strictly governed by 
their respective brand guidelines and manuals. 
 
The Genting Group markets and offers a suite of products 
and premier leisure brands that include Genting, Resorts 
World Genting Grand, Genting Club, Crockfords and 
Maxims. In addition, the Group partners with other 
established brands such as Universal Studios, Premium 
Outlets and Zouk. 
 
Unauthorised use of our brand assets and IP by third 
parties poses legal and reputational risks. Therefore, we 
actively monitor and address any potential IP 
infringements by third parties to safeguard our rights and 
reputation. To preserve the good reputation we have 
built, we will not hesitate to take legal action against 
those caught infringing our rights. These proactive 
measures reinforce our commitment in sustaining our 
brand value and business growth. 
 
Besides protecting our IP , we also remain alert about the 
misuse of our Group leadership’s images. In 2024, we 
learned that the likeness of our Chairman and Chief 
Executive has been unlawfully and improperly used to 
promote online trading platforms. 
 
A pop-up notice has been implemented on our corporate 
website to clearly disassociate ourselves from these 
unauthorised platforms and urge the public to not 
disseminate such materials linking our Chairman and 
Chief Executive to any trading sites. The notice also 
advises stakeholders, including the public to visit our 
corporate website www.genting.com to verify any 
matters related to the Group. We continue to review and 
consider additional measures to address and prevent 
these misrepresentations.

CYBERSECURITY AND DATA PROTECTION
SUSTAINING ECONOMIC VALUE CREATION
GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202540
WHY IT 
MATTERS
Cybersecurity is designed to safeguard electronic information across websites, networks and devices from malicious 
attacks by hackers. With rapid technological advancements, Genting Berhad and its subsidiaries must remain vigilant 
and proactive in staying ahead of cybercriminals who aim to exploit customer personal data. A comprehensive and 
robust cybersecurity system ensures that customer information remains secure, yet accessible to the Group, minimising 
the risk of business disruptions.
OUR APPROACH 
 
We are dedicated to treating every guest’s personal data with 
utmost care and respect, ensuring their rights are upheld in 
the collection, use, access and disclosure of their information. 
Our commitment is outlined in the personal data protection 
policies of all our operating units that manage customer data, 
as well as our portfolio companies, which are developed in 
compliance with applicable data privacy laws. 
 
This dedication to data privacy is further reinforced by robust 
cybersecurity procedures designed to ensure the safe 
handling of our customers’ personal data. We have 
implemented stringent measures to prevent data loss, 
unauthorised access, misuse, alteration, or disclosure. These 
measures are regularly reviewed and updated. 
 
All relevant parties are required to adhere to these measures 
and are entrusted with the responsibility of monitoring data, 
managing access rights and classifying data. This is to ensure 
that the security levels correspond to the potential risks and 
consequences of a data privacy breach. 
 
Data Security and Cybersecurity 
 
Genting Berhad has established comprehensive policies and 
frameworks to safeguard data security and strengthen 
cybersecurity. The Privacy Statement, Personal Data Breach 
Management Policy, Corporate Information Technology 
Security Policy and Personal Data Protection Policy form the 
foundation of Genting Berhad’s approach in ensuring that the 
security and conﬁdentiality of resources, data and information 
are preserved at all times.  
 
The efficiency and reliability of our operating systems and 
information technology (“IT”) resources are essential for 
supporting our daily operations and maintaining seamless 
connectivity between Genting Berhad and its portfolio 
companies. At our corporate offices in Wisma Genting, we 
have implemented robust processes and risk management 
practices to safeguard the integrity of our systems and IT 
resources, ensuring minimal disruption to business activities. 
 
As the adoption of advanced technologies, including artiﬁcial 
intelligence, accelerates across our operations, these 
measures are increasingly important to mitigate emerging 
threats and maintain robust governance over technology-
related risks.  
 
 
Genting Berhad’s Privacy Policy serves as a comprehensive 
framework for managing personal data, designed to protect 
the rights of customers, shareholders, employees and other 
stakeholders in full compliance with personal data protection 
laws. These include: 
continuous monitoring and managing of network traffic •
and accessibility to Genting’s systems; 
investigation of suspicious network traffic notiﬁcations and •
alerts; 
regular maintenance of Company systems to close gaps •
identiﬁed; and 
technical measures including network gateway protection •
systems limit, anti-malware software installed in all 
systems and endpoints; and encryption used to protect 
critical and conﬁdential data. 
 
At Genting Berhad, cybersecurity is governed by an IT 
Committee, chaired by the Chief Executive Officer and 
supported by the relevant heads of departments and IT 
support teams. The committee meeting was held every 
quarter in 2025 to review the company’s IT-related material 
matters, reinforcing our commitment to strong governance 
and data protection. 
 
Regular engagements with employees were undertaken in 
2025 to ensure seamless operation and heightened 
awareness among employees. These engagements included 
clear communication channels to feedback on operating 
systems as well as the informative sharing sessions on 
emerging cybersecurity threats through emails, promoting 
awareness and readiness among employees.   
 
During the reporting period, there were no substantiated 
complaints concerning breaches of customer privacy and 
losses of customer data at Genting Berhad. There were no 
identiﬁed cases of leaks, thefts or losses of customer data. 
 
There were also no incidents of service interruption or major 
downtime resulting in permanent loss of data over the past 
ﬁve years.

Genting Malaysia’s cybersecurity and data protection are guided by ISO 27001:2022, the National Institute of Standards and  
Technology (“NIST”) Cybersecurity Framework and Bank Negara Malaysia guidelines. Regular audits, vulnerability assessments 
and penetration tests ensure robust controls to safeguard customer and employee data. 
 
Any incidents requiring investigation are promptly escalated to the Risk Management Committee, reinforcing a strong culture 
of accountability. A robust governance framework, which includes audits and continuous monitoring, supports this to ensure 
strict adherence to data protection standards. 
 
Genting Malaysia anchors its integrated cybersecurity approach on the following priorities: 
• achievement and maintenance of world-class data security certiﬁcations; 
• ongoing risk assessments supported by proactive mitigation strategies; 
• comprehensive employee awareness and training programmes to strengthen cybersecurity practices; and 
• high standards of transparency and accountability through structured reporting mechanisms. 
Malaysia 
 
Genting Malaysia’s local operations, covering data centres 
are ISO 27001:2022 certiﬁed to ensure comprehensive 
security for servers, network and system administration. 
The Data Protection Office has put in place policies and  
procedures to guide all businesses units in putting in  place 
robust data protection programme. 
 
A global Managed Security Services provider monitors 
cyber threats 24/7, enabling swift detection and response. 
Incident procedures assess, contain and notify regulators 
and affected individuals within required timelines. Genting 
Malaysia documents and reviews all incidents and takes 
corrective actions. 
 
Genting Malaysia subscribed to an Attack Surface 
Monitoring platform, providing continuous visibility into 
internet-facing assets in 2025. The platform detects 
vulnerabilities, misconﬁgurations, exposed data and 
suspicious activity, with high-risk issues highlighted for 
immediate action. 
 
Genting Malaysia remains committed to strengthening 
cybersecurity resilience and safeguarding customer data, 
systems and business operations. IT department 
conducted quarterly phishing email simulation exercises 
for all employees. To further promote accountability and 
encourage safe cyber practices, Genting Malaysia has 
implemented a disciplinary procedures effective 1 January 
2026 for Malaysia-based employees who repeatedly fail 
phishing simulation exercises. Tracking all results on a 
calendar-year basis will ensure consistent monitoring and 
continuous improvement.

zero-day threat protection and real-time network monitoring to enable timely detection and response to suspicious activities.

complemented by regular audits to strengthen oversight and accountability. Cyber hygiene is reinforced through a 90-day 
password reset policy and mandatory annual cybersecurity and data privacy training, achieving a 99% completion rate in 
2025, with contractors and third parties also required to complete the training. Genting Malaysia’s US operation's practices

strengthening defences and evaluating emerging technologies. 
Resorts World Las Vegas maintains a strong security posture through continuous awareness programs and comprehensive

mitigating risk, this approach assists in operational resilience. Since opening in June 2021, Resorts World Las Vegas has not 
experienced any major service interruptions or operational downtime.

market dynamics, we strengthen proﬁtability and ensure long-

Value creation is at the core of our strategy, driving positive 
economic impacts through employment creation, tax 
contributions and broader community development. We 
uphold responsible business practices that align commercial 
success with societal progress. Through prudent ﬁnancial 
management and sustainable growth strategies, we aim to 
create enduring value while  strengthening our position as a 
global industry leader . Genting Berhad generated a total

compared with the previous year .

contributor , accounting for 82% of the total economic value.

Singapore, the United States, the Bahamas, the United

in driving the tourism industry and strengthening local 
economies through employment opportunities and business 
growth.

in their respective markets. The Group’s Plantations division 
contributes to sustainable agricultural development, 
supporting food security, employment and rural economic 
advancement.

DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
OUR COMMITMENT
GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202544
OUR COMMITMENTS 
 
As a global conglomerate operating across multiple industries, Genting Berhad committed to environmental stewardship, 
recognising the important role it plays in protecting biodiversity and mitigating climate change. Our sustainability strategy 
is designed to integrate responsible environmental management practices across all operations, ensuring operational 
resilience, regulatory compliance and a positive impact on ecosystems and communities. 
Environmental Governance and Compliance 
 
Genting Berhad adheres to international and national environmental regulations and frameworks, ensuring 
accountability and continuous improvement in environmental stewardship. Our environmental governance approach 
includes: 
 
• IFRS S1 and S2 Integration:  Aligning sustainability disclosures with global standards to enhance transparency in 
environmental and sustainability impact reporting; 
 
• Stakeholder Engagement:  Engaging with government agencies, NGOs and industry partners to support the 
development and adoption of sustainable policies and best practices; and 
 
• Sustainability Performance Monitoring : Regularly assessing environmental performance through key indicators, 
audits and independent evaluations to track progress and reinforce accountability. 
 
In conjunction with the Genting Group’s 60 th anniversary, a group-wide sustainability webinar was convened in 
September 2025 to strengthen climate preparedness and reinforce future readiness. The webinar brought together 
company directors, management and sustainability working teams to discuss and share insights on climate-related risks 
and opportunities across the Group. These dialogues enabled participants to exchange diverse perspectives and identify 
shared priorities for future action.
Commitment to Biodiversity Conservation 
 
Biodiversity conservation is integral to our environmental 
approach. Genting Berhad is committed to preserving and 
enhancing the natural habitats surrounding our operations. 
Our biodiversity strategy focuses on: 
 
• Habitat Protection and Restoration: Implementing 
conservation programmes to protect forests, marine 
ecosystems and wildlife corridors within and around our 
business operations; 
 
• Sustainable Land Use Management: Adopting 
responsible agricultural and land management practices 
to minimise deforestation, prevent soil degradation and 
avoid habitat destruction; 
 
• Collaboration with Environmental Organisations: 
Partnering with global and local conservation groups to 
support biodiversity research, protection efforts and 
ecosystem restoration projects; and 
 
• Reducing Impact on Wildlife:  Implementing measures 
to mitigate operational impacts on biodiversity including 
responsible tourism practices, sustainable sourcing and 
wildlife protection initiatives. 
Climate Change Mitigation and Adaptation 
 
Addressing climate change is a fundamental pillar of 
Genting Berhad’s environmental strategy. Our initiatives aim 
to reduce GHG emissions, improve energy efficiency and 
enhance climate resilience through: 
 
• Carbon Reduction Strategies:  Investing in renewable 
energy, energy efficiency measures and low-carbon 
technologies to minimise our carbon footprint; 
 
• Sustainable Energy Transition:  Increasing the use of 
clean energy sources, optimising energy consumption 
and supporting credible carbon offset projects; 
 
• Climate Risk Management:  Identifying, assessing and 
managing  climate-related  risks,  such  as extreme 
weather events, rising temperatures and resource 
scarcity, to safeguard business continuity; and 
 
• Green Infrastructure and Sustainable Design:  
Integrating environmental-friendly construction practices 
and design principles, such as green building standards, 
water conservation systems and nature-based solutions 
in new developments.

WHY IT 
MATTERS
We recognise the vital role of biodiversity in maintaining resilient ecosystems and mitigating long-term environmental 
risks. Biodiversity conservation is key to safeguard natural resources and ecosystems for the beneﬁt of the people and 
the planet. 
 
With business operations across nine countries, the Group recognises that several of our properties are located in areas 
that require responsible management and careful stewardship of local biodiversity.
OUR APPROACH 
 
Group is committed to integrating environmental stewardship into its business operations. We aim to balance the development 
projects of our operating subsidiaries with the conservation of their surrounding ecosystems, as several of our resorts, 
plantations and energy facilities are located near biodiversity-rich areas. In contrast, our corporate offices located at Wisma 
Genting, Kuala Lumpur , have minimal biodiversity footprint. 
 
Our biodiversity commitment is guided by national policies, frameworks and procedures to support sustainable practices, such 
as Malaysia’s National Policy on Biological Diversity 2022-2030. 
 
Biodiversity risks for potential or new projects are evaluated as part of environmental due diligence, focusing on ecosystem 
impacts, proximity to protected areas and compliance with applicable regulations. The ﬁndings of these assessments guide 
our team’s project planning, decision-making and the implementation of risk mitigation measures. Biodiversity risks for current 
operations and projects are also assessed through the risk management’s due diligence process to mitigate ecosystem impacts 
and ensure responsible project development. 
 
Managing Our Impact on Biodiversity 
 
The Group’s biodiversity management approach is anchored on four key pillars - conservation, preservation, sustainability and 
education. This framework strengthens our commitment to protecting natural ecosystems while supporting long-term business 
resilience. 
Our operating teams implement robust risk management processes within their respective risk management frameworks, 
including environmental and biodiversity risk assessments. They proactively monitor and report signiﬁcant environmental 
impacts, strengthening accountability and supporting the integration of biodiversity considerations into our corporate strategy 
and overall risk management approach.

OUR COMMITMENTS 
 
As a group, we are dedicated to ensuring our biodiversity 
efforts are aligned with the national conservation targets of 
the countries and jurisdictions where we operate. 
 
Malaysia, Singapore, Indonesia, China, India, Egypt, the 
Bahamas and the United Kingdom (with the exception of the 
United States) are signatories to the Convention on Biological 
Diversity and the Post-2020 Global Biodiversity Framework. 
 
Our biodiversity strategy draws on national action plans and 
policies on biodiversity, including Malaysia’s National Policy 
on Biological Diversity 2022–2030, Singapore’s Biodiversity 
Conservation Strategy and Action Plan and its “City in Nature” 
initiative under the Singapore Green Plan 2030 and 
Indonesia’s National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan 
which targets to restore two million hectares of degraded 
ecosystems including forests, peatlands and mangroves by 
2030. 
 
Environmental assessments such as Environmental Impact 
Assessments, High Conservation Value Assessments and High 
Carbon Stock Assessments are conducted regularly by our 
teams to protect the biodiversity and carbon-rich areas of our 
operations. These assessments guide decision-making, 
mitigating risks and reinforce the commitment to sustainable 
development. 
 
Commitment to Net Positive Biodiversity Impact 
 
Through our group-wide initiatives, we aim to deliver nature-
positive outcomes, strengthen ecosystem resilience and 
contribute meaningfully to the global goal of halting and 
reversing biodiversity loss. 
 
As part of our broader sustainability and environmental 
stewardship strategy, we are committed to achieving a net 
positive impact on biodiversity across our operations. Our  
efforts focus on delivering measurable conservation 
outcomes that exceed any potential adverse impacts resulting 
from our activities. 
 
Where avoidance and minimisation of biodiversity impacts are 
not feasible, we apply the mitigation hierarchy, which  
includes restoration, rehabilitation and biodiversity offset 
programmes to address or compensate for residual impacts. 
 
Our practices are benchmarked against internationally 
recognised standards and best practices including the 
principles of the Business and Biodiversity Offsets Programme 
and are aligned with the objectives of the Post-2020 Global 
Biodiversity Framework. This alignment ensures that our 
biodiversity strategy remains robust, credible and effective. 
 
We are committed to integrate nature-positive principles 
into project design, land-use planning and operational 
decision-making processes, ensuring that biodiversity 
conservation remains a priority and the ecosystem integrity, 
functionality and services are preserved and enhanced over 
the long term. 
  
 
 
 
 
Engagements with Stakeholders 
 
We work closely with the authorities as well as local and 
international non-governmental organisations to support the 
effective implementation of our biodiversity strategies aimed 
at reducing biodiversity loss. 
 
Our operating teams maintain active engagements with 
government agencies, regulators and key stakeholders to 
contribute to the national and regional biodiversity-
conservation agendas. These collaborations ensure our 
efforts remain aligned with broader conservation priorities. 
 
Our engagements involve participating in policy dialogues, 
industry forums and collaborative initiatives to align our 
biodiversity actions with national conservation targets across 
the jurisdictions where we operate. Through these 
engagements, we advocate for sustainable land use, 
ecosystem restoration and nature-positive outcomes that 
deliver long-term environmental value. 
 
Monitoring of Biodiversity Action Plans 
 
Biodiversity action plans form a core component of the 
Group’s environmental management approach and are 
implemented across all relevant operational sites. The site-
speciﬁc action plans undertaken in 2025 are detailed in this 
section. 
 
Regular internal audits are conducted to assess the 
effectiveness of these action plans and to evaluate compliance 
with applicable regulatory framework and alignment with 
international biodiversity commitments. These audits review 
mitigation actions, habitat enhancement  activities and 
species protection initiatives, ensuring  consistent 
implementation across our operations. Insights from these 
audits are used to strengthen our biodiversity management 
practices and reinforce alignment with national  conservation 
priorities and global biodiversity frameworks. 
 
The Group, through Genting Plantations, undertakes 
additional biodiversity-related assessments and certiﬁcations 
using pilot schemes and structured implementation  
approaches. 
 
Pilot schemes include the application of High Carbon Stock 
(“HCS”) and High Conservation Value (“HCV”) assessments at 
selected sites to identify, protect and manage areas of  
signiﬁcant ecological importance. These assessments  
support IFRS S2’s expectations for clear processes to identify  
and monitor nature-related risks and they complement our  
compliance with recognised certiﬁcation schemes. 
 
Building on these pilot initiatives, Genting Plantations has 
progressed to a systematic integration of HCS and HCV 
elements across its landholdings, alongside adherence to  
industry standard certiﬁcations such as the Roundtable on 
Sustainability Palm Oil and Malaysian Sustainability Palm Oil 
requirements. Further details on sustainable palm are 
provided in the feature section on Genting Plantations. 
 
This structured approach demonstrates the Group’s 
commitment to scaling up biodiversity-positive actions 
beyond pilot phase.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA
Genting Malaysia is committed to supporting the National Policy on Biological Diversity (“NPBD”) 2022–2030, aligning with 
Malaysia’s response to the Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework. Malaysia is recognised as one of the world’s 
megadiverse countries, with high levels of species richness and endemism. Within this national context, Genting Highlands 
represents an important area where environmental considerations are integrated into long-term planning and development. 
 
 
Habitat Areas Protected or Restored in Malaysia 
 
Approximately 10,000 acres of land under Genting Malaysia’s care include areas that 
support a diverse range of ﬂora and fauna characteristic of Malaysia’s highland ecosystems. 
 
Ecological surveys recorded over 460 plant species, 
including the rare Thismia limkokthayi, alongside a 
variety of wildlife, including six primate species, 254 
bird species and approximately 1,200 insect species. 
Observed fauna include native species such as the 
Dusky Leaf Monkey, Surili Leaf Monkey, Siamang 
(black  gibbon)  and  Serow  (wild  mountain goat), which contribute to the functioning 
of the local ecosystem. 
 
Genting Malaysia remains committed to  conservation efforts by carefully planning 
developments to minimise potential impacts on species listed on the International Union 
for Conservation of Nature (“IUCN”) Red List, while strictly respecting legally protected 
areas. Genting Malaysia seeks to balance biodiversity considerations with long-term 
operational and development needs, supporting national biodiversity objectives and 
responsible stewardship of the highlands through sustainable management practices 
and integrated planning. 
 
The Alliance of Rainforest
Malaysia
Bio Parks

fauna essential to the ecosystem, contributing 
to sustainable biodiversity management, 
education and responsible tourism with 
activities carefully planned to minimise impacts 
on species of conservation concern and respect 
protected areas.
Biodiversity as Climate Regulator  
 
Biodiversity plays a critical role as a natural 
climate regulator . Forests are vital carbon sinks, 
helping mitigate GHG emissions. Genting 
Malaysia supports carbon capture, water 
regulation and soil erosion prevention through 
forest management.

OUR APPROACH 
 
Impact Assessment 
 
Genting Malaysia implemented robust biodiversity 
conservation planning and conducted biodiversity impact 
assessments across four levels: Global, Policy, Programme 
and Planning, and Project. 
 
Conservation and Preservation through Collaboration  
 
Genting Malaysia collaborated with leading organisations, 
external agencies and species experts such as the Department 
of Wildlife and National Parks (“PERHILITAN”), the Forest 
Research Institute Malaysia ("FRIM") and Department of 
Fisheries Terengganu. Participation from local universities 
provide expertise to strengthen the conservation efforts and 
initiatives at Resorts World Genting.  
 
 
The Verges 
 
The “Verges” initiative by Genting Malaysia is designed to 
create functional living habitats that beneﬁt the environment 
and biodiversity. This initiative prevents soil erosion, creates 
corridors for wildlife movement, provides food sources for 
insects and birds as well as offers shade for other plant 
species. 
 
The select species are evaluated based on their ecological 
value with enhancements incorporated to accelerate habitat 
maturity. Over the past year , Genting Malaysia in collaboration 
with more than 120 companies, planted over 6,900 plants, 
contributing directly to the restoration of natural habitats and 
the strengthening of local ecosystems. 
 
Genting Malaysia is committed to protecting endangered ﬂora 
and fauna through in-situ and ex-situ conservation 
programmes. 
 
 
GENTING NATURE ADVENTURE (“GNA”) 
 
GNA is Resorts World Genting’s ﬂagship initiative that  
supports the NPBD 2022-2030 and UNSDGs by transforming 
a simple walk in the woods into an immersive and educational 
journey. GNA’s guided trails introduce visitors to the rich 
biodiversity of the rainforest, from native ﬂora and fauna to 
intricate ecosystems. 
 
The initiative aligns with Goal 1, Goal 2 and Goal 3 of the NPBD 
2022–2030, which focus on strengthening biodiversity 
conservation, mainstreaming biodiversity across sectors and 
enhancing implementation through partnerships. 
 
Guided by ISO 9001:2015 and ISO 21101:2014, the GNA 
initiative places strong emphasis on environmental 
stewardship, innovation and the promotion of sustainable 
tourism practices. For more details on GNA, please refer to 
Genting Malaysia Sustainability Report 2025. 
 
In-situ 
Preserving the Genting Bio Park, a Montane Ericaceous 
Forest (Mossy Forest), in collaboration with FRIM strict 
governance measures to regulate access to sensitive 
areas and controlled entry systems, regular security 
checks and wildlife patrols to prevent illegal activities. 
 
Ex-situ 
Protecting rare and endangered species listed on the 
IUCN Red List, including Dacrydium comosum, 
Nepenthes macfarlanei and Nepenthes ramispina.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont’d)
Life Cycle Analysis 
 
Life Cycle Analysis is essential in all our local and international projects, as a systematic method for assessing environmenta l 
impacts of a project at every stage, from material sourcing to project completion. This approach ensures responsible and 
sustainability practices are upheld throughout the refurbishment lifecycle, from cradle to grave. 
 
Aligned with circular economy principles, our approach prioritises upcycling and repurposing to minimise waste and reduce  
reliance on landﬁlls. Fallen trees are transformed into landscape features, while old wooden planks are restored and repurposed 
into decorative elements or functional furniture, extending their useful life and reducing resource consumption. 
 
Long-term Landscaping 
 
Since early 2025, Genting Malaysia has rolled out a Landscape Sustainable Development 
Plan to strengthen ecological stewardship across its operations. 
 
Key initiatives included established nursery inventory records and advancing plant 
propagation techniques, piloted vermicomposting for food and garden waste, alongside 
composting trials for garden waste as well as focused on forest tree propagation and 
replanting to increase the diversity of native plant species within landscaped areas. 
 
Genting Malaysia introduced landscape plant tagging in high-traffic areas of the highlands 
to educate visitors on the ecological value of native species, promoting greater 
environmental awareness.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE
Situated amidst Sentosa Island’s vibrant terrestrial and marine biodiversity, Genting Singapore is deeply committed to 
environmental stewardship, striving to minimise its ecological footprint while championing conservation efforts. Genting 
Singapore goes beyond operational priorities to implement meaningful conservation initiatives including diligent monitoring 
of its terrestrial forest, protection of marine biodiversity, active advocacy for biodiversity conservation and cultivation of 
meaningful partnerships in research and conservation.
Terrestrial Conservation  
 
Forest Preservation and Monitoring  
 
Resorts World Sentosa's 2.9-hectare coastal forest serves as a 
sanctuary for diverse plant and animal species, supporting 
local wildlife while contributing to Sentosa's ecosystem health. 
In partnership with Nature Society Singapore and National 
Parks Board, Genting Singapore actively monitored the 
forest's biodiversity through comprehensive surveys. 
 
Over 33% of the 93 identiﬁed plant species are IUCN-listed  as 
threatened, prompting proactive measures by the  Resorts 
World Sentosa landscape team, including targeted  pruning 
to control invasive species. A 2023 survey recorded  17 bird 
species and 21 butterﬂy species, highlighting a  healthy 
coastal forest ecosystem and the impact of  sustained 
conservation efforts. 
 
Eco-pond and Biodiversity Sanctuary  
 
Resorts World Sentosa’s eco-pond featuring bio-swale ﬁlters 
and a stormwater collection system for water puriﬁcation and 
irrigation, which conserves resources while creating a thriving 
habitat for diverse biodiversity, including egrets, hornbills and 
more, showcasing harmony between nature and the resort. 
 
 
Preserving Heritage Trees  
 
At Resorts World Sentosa, three Heritage Trees namely the 
Seashore Mangosteen (Garcinia celebica (hombroniana)), the 
towering 35-metre Red-ﬂowered Malayan Spindle Tree (Bhesa 
robusta) in its coastal forest, a towering specimen that 
reaches 35 metres in height and the Common Pulai (Alstonia 
angustiloba) received dedicated protection.  
 
Genting Singapore installed four additional lightning  
protection system units for the large Alstonia angustiloba  
tree located near Tree Top Loft. These heritage trees are  
signiﬁcant in size and ecological value, warranting further  
efforts for their preservation. 
 
Enhancing Biodiversity Through Landscape Design 
 
As part of the RWS 2.0 expansion plan, the newly redesigned 
Rejuvenation Garden at The Laurus, created with landscape 
specialists as a biodiverse sanctuary at the forest edge.

Marine Conservation  
 
Genting Singapore is committed to the highest standards of animal welfare, conservation and environmental education. With 
the opening of Singapore Oceanarium, Genting Singapore is strengthening its focus on marine conservation and science. As 
Asia’s leading ocean-focused destination, it now serves as a hub for research, learning and public engagement to support local 
and regional partners working in protecting marine ecosystems. 
Capacity Building and Training 
Resorts World Sentosa volunteers received training in 
biodiversity photography, species identiﬁcation, specimen 
curation and ﬁeld survey techniques, enabling them to 
actively contribute to biodiversity surveys across 
Singapore’s Southern Islands. 
RWS-NUS Living Laboratory 
Resorts World Sentosa committed SGD5 million  over three years (2022 to 2025) to support research initiatives under the 
Marine Biodiversity Living Lab in collaboration with National University of Singapore. Over the past three years, the Marine 
Biodiversity Living Lab has made signiﬁcant contributions to marine science and conservation. Research teams advanced 
population studies of the Mosaic Reef Crab at Tanjong Rimau, achieving successful ex-situ culture and uncovering new 
insights into its ecology, distribution and seasonal dynamics to chart knowledge that supports long-term species 
management.  
Education, Outreach and Knowledge Sharing 
In conjunction with the opening of Singapore Oceanarium, 
the inaugural Research and Learning Week fostered 
meaningful dialogue, showcasing research progress and 
underscored the role in driving marine education and 
capacity building.
Partnerships for Conservation and Research 
 
Singapore Oceanarium aspires to become Asia’s leading destination for marine education, research and conservation, 
championing the protection and restoration of marine species and ecosystems. Genting Singapore harnesses its expertise, 
experience and cutting-edge facilities to forge strategic partnerships. These collaborations with marine research and 
conservation partners empower the company to undertake initiatives that deliver tangible beneﬁts to both the marine 
environment within the aquarium and the broader ecosystem, ensuring a sustainable future for the environment.
 Species Conservation and Research Initiatives 
 
Genting Singapore advanced marine conservation 
through a series of collaborative initiatives, including 
hosting a Population Viability Analysis Workshop for the 
Critically Endangered Bowmouth Guitarﬁsh, convening 
over 60 global experts and positioning Singapore 
Oceanarium as a key hub for species recovery. 
 
Conservation efforts also extended to coral reef  
restoration in Terengganu with Coralku Malaysia,  
establishing 10 reef frames, out-planting 300 coral  
fragments and deploying 30 spawning tents. To  address 
microplastic pollution, a year-long study  on plastic nurdle 
distribution along the Johor  Strait was launched, with 
early ﬁndings shared  regionally, while community 
engagement in  Sabah involved over 80 ﬁshers and 
officials in  dialogues on sustainable shark and ray 
ﬁsheries,  laying the groundwork for future collaborative  
conservation action.
 Global Policy Engagement and Collaboration 
 
Genting Singapore hosted the opening ceremony of the 
Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species 
of Wild Fauna and Flora (“CITES”) and Global Youth Summit 
2025 at Singapore Oceanarium, welcoming 76 youth 
participants from 53 countries, alongside a workshop on 
marine wildlife trafficking to deepen understanding of 
threats to marine species. 
 
Participation as an observer at the CITES Conference of the 
Parties further supported youth-led policy advancements 
and strengthened international conservation 
partnerships, reflecting a strong commitment to 
empowering youth leadership and global biodiversity 
action.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS
Genting Plantations recognises that its No Deforestation, No 
Peat and No Exploitation policy plays an important role in 
maintaining access to global markets and supporting both 
environmental and social integrity. The company adopts key 
frameworks and practices, including the RSPO Principles and 
Criteria, comprehensive environmental and conservation 
assessments and ongoing engagement with local 
communities. These efforts guide responsible land use, 
contribute to biodiversity protection and promote operational 
transparency. 
 
Navigating Our Climate Challenges 
Genting Plantations faces growing climate-related risks, 
including more frequent ﬁre and ﬂood incidents across its 
plantation operations. The company actively implements 
measures to minimise impacts on both its operations and the 
environment. Guided by the RSPO’s New Planting Procedure 
("NPP"), MSPO standards and its Sustainability Policy, Genting 
Plantations upholds its commitments to no deforestation, 
conservation and biodiversity. 
 
The company manages climate-related risks through its 
Environmental Policy and Environmental Management 
Guidelines, supported by a climate action plan focused on 
emission reduction, resource efficiency and supply chain 
resilience. Oversight is provided by its Board and Senior 
Management. 
 
In addition, Genting Plantations works closely with its 
suppliers to ensure compliance with the environmental 
standards and regulatory requirements such as the EUDR. To 
manage residual risks, Genting Plantations applies climate 
adaptation measures, including good agronomic practices, 
efficient water management and responsible resource use. 
 
Key Policies, Standards and Practices 
 
• No Deforestation, No Peat and No Exploitation policy. 
• Compliance with the RSPO New Planting Procedure. 
• Environmental Impact Assessments, High Conservation 
Value and High Carbon Stock assessments. 
• Genting Plantations’ Sustainability Policy. 
• Community engagement via the Free, Prior and Informed 
Consent. 
• Collaboration and partnerships with non-governmental       
organisations, such as HUTAN and the Bringing Back Our 
Rare Animals. 
• Compliance with Indonesian regulatory requirements for 
plantation development.

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202554
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS (cont’d)
Driving Conservation for a Greener Future 
Genting Plantations undertakes targeted conservation 
initiatives to protect biodiversity and minimise environmental 
impacts, focusing on safeguarding habitats for endangered 
species, preserving HCV and HCS areas. The company is 
managing ecological risks associated with plantation 
development. 
 
Aligned with SDG 15 on the protection, restoration and 
sustainable use of terrestrial ecosystems, forests and 
biodiversity, Genting Plantations prohibits development 
within HCV and HCS areas, avoids peatland development 
regardless of depth and enforces zero-burning practices 
across all operations. These commitments are implemented 
through robust internal protocols and reinforced by 
environmental assessments, operational planning and 
ongoing ﬁeld-level controls. 
 
Managing Ecological Impacts 
Genting Plantations embeds NDPE principles into operational 
practices through company policies and Environmental 
Management Guidelines, which have guided implementation 
since 2015. To protect sensitive ecosystems, the company 
excludes HCV and HCS areas from land clearing and manages 
them through targeted site-level controls, supporting 
continued access to markets that require strict NDPE 
compliance. 
 
Preventing Deforestation 
Genting Plantations has implemented strict policies and 
procedures to prevent deforestation across its operations and 
supply chain. All new developments are required to  undergo 
comprehensive environmental, social and soil assessments, 
including HCV and HCS evaluations by accredited assessors 
and a 30-day RSPO public consultation prior to approval. 
 
The company works closely with FFB suppliers and plasma 
scheme smallholders to ensure alignment  with its no-
deforestation standards. The oversight was expanded in  2025 
to monitor 11% of suppliers covering 19,758 hectares. Where 
suppliers are found to have cleared land after the EUDR cut-
off date, reforestation may be required. 
 
As part of its conservation commitments, Genting Plantations 
manages about 16,153 hectares of HCV areas  and 13,125 
hectares of other conservation sites, all of which  remain 
under protection. These areas are monitored on a  biannual 
basis to prevent encroachment, poaching and other illegal 
activities. 
 
Preserving Peatlands 
Genting Plantations upholds a strict no-development policy 
on peatlands in line with RSPO requirements, applying this 
standard across its operations and plasma scheme 
smallholders. 
 
Genting Plantations has identiﬁed 9,102 hectares of  peatland 
within its landbank, of which 8,292 hectares  were developed 
prior to 2009, representing about 3% of  total land area with 
no new peatland development since  then. 
 
The company continues rehabilitation efforts and manages 
peatlands according to RSPO Best Management Practices, 
including water level controls to reduce ﬁre risk, subsidence 
and degradation. These measures support the long-term 
ecosystem integrity and carbon storage. 
 
Integrated Pest Management 
Genting Plantations applies Integrated Pest Management to 
reduce reliance on chemical inputs, prioritising non-chemical 
controls and using agrochemicals only when necessary to 
address severe pest outbreaks under strict internal 
procedures. All applications are documented and reviewed in 
line with the Sustainability Management Procedure Manual. 
 
The company also manages surface run-off to prevent 
agrochemical residues from entering conservation areas, 
helping protect sensitive ecosystems and maintain long-term 
ecological balance. 
 
Fertiliser Application Management 
Genting Plantations manages fertiliser application through 
established standard operating procedures to ensure efficient 
nutrient delivery while minimising environmental impacts. 
These efforts are supported by regular soil and leaf analyses 
conducted by the Agronomic Analytic and Advisory team to 
determine estate-speciﬁc requirements. 
 
Fertiliser is applied during periods of moderate rainfall to 
reduce run-off and prevent nutrient volatilisation. Product  
quality is also reviewed periodically to ensure it meets  
agronomic standards and ﬁeld requirements. 
 
While chemical and inorganic fertilisers remain part of 
operations, Genting Plantations is committed to reducing  
dependency on these inputs. The company implements run-
off controls to prevent nutrient ﬂow into conservation areas, 
supporting ecosystem protection and long-term landscape 
stewardship.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS (cont’d)
Biodiversity: Preserving Nature’s Legacy 
Biodiversity conservation is integral to Genting Plantations’ 
operations, supporting ecological balance, genetic diversity 
and essential ecosystem services. To protect sensitive 
habitats, the company prohibits development in HCV, HCS and 
peatland areas and implements targeted conservation 
initiatives to safeguard landscapes that support endangered, 
rare and threatened species. These actions reinforce the 
company’s commitment to long-term environmental 
stewardship. 
 
Protecting Wildlife in Our Conservation Areas  
Genting Plantations identiﬁed a wide range of wildlife species 
across its operations, including reptiles, birds, mammals and 
ﬂora listed on the IUCN Red List and uses these observations 
to inform biodiversity management and habitat protection 
strategies. 
 
In Indonesia, internal teams conducted wildlife stock 
veriﬁcation and ground-truthing surveys to better understand 
species distribution within conservation areas, supporting 
more informed conservation planning and the development 
of targeted management plans for species protection. 
 
Conservation and Restoration Projects 
Genting Plantations supports ecological restoration through 
long-term partnerships, including its collaboration since 2021 
with the Borneo Orangutan Rescue Alliance to restore 
habitats and enhance biodiversity across its estates. 
Restoration efforts began with planting orangutan food trees 
in Genting Layang, Tenegang and Tanjung, guided by initial 
site assessments and later expanded to additional estates and 
riparian zones using native species such as Berembang, 
Tangkol and Ficus. 
 
These activities are supported by in-ﬁeld nurseries and estate 
worker participation. Field monitoring from 2021 to 2025 
showed strong performance of Ficus in direct sunlight and 
recorded orangutans feeding on restored plantings, 
indicating positive habitat use and early signs of ecological 
recovery. 
Keruak Wildlife Corridor 
Established in 2018, the Keruak Wildlife Corridor is a 
partnership between Genting Plantations, HUTAN and the 
Sabah State Government to restore forests and enhance 
habitat connectivity along Kinabatangan River . The initiative 
rehabilitates buffer zones within plantation landscapes by 
planting native species in underutilised areas to support 
natural regeneration.  
 
A biodiversity survey conducted under the Carnivore Project 
between December 2023 and January 2024 provided insights 
into species presence and corridor use. Local communities 
also participate through nursery training programmes that 
build skills and provide alternative income, linking ecological 
restoration with positive social impact.  
 
Wildlife Monitoring 
Genting Plantations enforces strict controls across its 
concession areas to prevent illegal or unauthorised hunting, 
with particular focus on protecting species listed as 
endangered or threatened under the IUCN Red List. These 
measures safeguard vulnerable wildlife populations and help 
maintain ecological balance across the landscape under the 
company’s management. 
 
Borneo Pygmy Elephants Conservation 
Genting Plantations continues to conserve the Borneo Pygmy 
Elephant through collaboration with HUTAN, Seratu Aatai and 
the Sabah Wildlife Department, focusing on protecting key 
habitats, enhancing landscape connectivity and reducing 
human-wildlife conﬂict, particularly in the Keruak Wildlife 
Corridor .  
 
The company conducts regular monitoring to track elephant 
movements, behaviour and habitat use, with ﬁeld teams 
actively observing elephant presence in the Rasig Division to 
inform ongoing conservation planning and support long-term 
protection of this endangered species.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING ENERGY
Genting Energy’s biodiversity initiatives focus on actively 
restoring habitats and protecting endangered species across 
our operations. 
 
Environmental protection remains a central focus of the 
Kasuri Production Sharing Contract (“Kasuri PSC”) in West 
Papua, Indonesia. The project conducted a comprehensive 
Environmental Impact Assessment, which forms the basis for 
selecting suitable sites and identifying necessary risk 
mitigation strategies, ensuring that environmental 
considerations remain integral throughout the entire project 
lifecycle from exploration to production, closure and 
abandonment. 
 
In 2025, Genting Energy has planted over 85,000 trees as part 
of its ecosystem rehabilitation efforts. Amongst them, over  
83,000  trees  were  planted  across an area of approximately 
120  hectares  to  support  long-term environmental 
sustainability in West Papua, Indonesia. The team also 
participated in a Tree Planting Day organised by the 
Indonesian Ministry of Forestry, during which 400 trees were 
planted in a watershed rehabilitation area in the Tanah Rubuh 
District of the Manokwari Regency. 
 
Sustainability efforts are also embedded within Genting 
Energy’s Power division. At the Banten power plant, 
environmental transparency and regulatory compliance are 
maintained through the submission of quarterly reports to 
the relevant authorities. The plant’s consistent environmental 
performance was recognised under Indonesia’s PROPER 
programme, a national environmental reporting initiative that 
promotes industrial compliance with pollution control 
regulations and stronger environmental management 
systems, with the plant receiving a “Blue” rating for the 
seventh consecutive year in 2025. 
 
Over the years, the number of monitored ﬂora species has 
grown through Banten power plant’s partnerships with 
relevant government bodies and local communities, with the 
power plant actively participating in local biodiversity 
initiatives to restore and protect the surrounding ecosystem 
within the Banten Regency municipality. For example, the 
Banten power plant has been actively involved in the 
Mangrove Conservation Programme for seven continuous 
years since its initial participation in 2019.  
 
In 2025, the team planted 4,000 mangroves, bringing the 
cumulative total to 25,000 mangroves across 2.45 hectares. In 
addition, the team also took part in other initiatives such as 
the World Environmental Day, National Tree Planting Day and 
Greening Activity at Salira Village, planting an additional 950 
trees to further support local biodiversity.

CLIMATE CHANGE,ENERGY  
AND EMISSIONS MONITORING
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
Genting Berhad’s emission targets are aligned with Malaysia’s national sustainability targets, which aim 
to reduce economy-wide absolute GHG emissions by 2035 from the projected peak (medium-term) and 
to achieve net zero emissions by 2050 (long-term).  
 
Genting Berhad targets achieving net zero emissions by 2050, supported by a short-term objective to 
reduce carbon intensity. This commitment covers Scopes 1, 2 and 3 emissions across all key operating 
units. Within the Group, Genting Singapore targets a 30% reduction in emissions intensity by 2030 (from 
a 2015 baseline) through its decarbonisation strategies, while Genting Plantations  aims for carbon 
neutrality by 2030 for its Malaysian operations and 2035 for its Indonesian operations (from a 2016 
baseline). The Group is working to establish a harmonised baseline and reduction pathway, taking into 
consideration the varying baselines across its subsidiaries, with disclosure in the next reporting period. 
 
Additionally, the Group prioritises climate adaptation through risk assessments, sustainable resource 
management and infrastructure improvements to enhance long term resilience. Our teams’ 
decarbonisation initiatives are featured in this section.
WHY IT 
MATTERS
The ongoing climate crisis poses signiﬁcant challenge, with the destructive impact of global warming threatening the 
environment in unprecedented ways. Rising global temperatures, extreme weather events and resource depletion 
present direct threats to our operations and the communities where we operate. Failing to act now will only exacerbate 
these risks, leading to potentially irreversible consequences and damage. 
 
Recognising the urgency of global calls for climate action, the Group is committed to reducing emissions and supporting 
climate adaptation plans in the countries where it operates. Eight of the nine countries in our portfolios are signatories 
to the Paris Agreement, which aims to limit global temperature rise to well below 2°C. The Group demonstrates its 
support for climate mitigation through its position on public policy and regulation. Our portfolio company, Genting 
Singapore has been a signatory to the United Nation Global Compact since 2022.  The Group will continue contributing 
to these targets through actionable and measurable steps.
Carbon 
emission 
- to 
achieve

by 2050
OUR APPROACH
Our board of directors, headed by the Executive Chairman, is responsible to provide strategic oversight on climate-related 
matters, ensuring that climate risks and opportunities are integrated into our business strategy and decision-making 
processes. 
 
Our directors are regularly briefed by the Executive Committee on any key sustainability development that could impact the 
Company, including climate-related risks and opportunities. The Executive Committee comprises senior management team 
and is headed by the Chief Executive Officer of Genting Berhad. 
 
Climate change is incorporated into Genting Berhad’s Sustainability Policy which encompasses strategies for managing  
sustainability-related risks and opportunities. We ensure our climate change stance, as outlined in our sustainability policy, 
aligns with the commitments of the trade associations our company and operating units are part of. Through our operating 
teams, we engage with industry groups to promote sustainable practices, advocate for climate responsibility and support 
impactful climate policies. Where misalignment arises, we will engage with the trade association to seek alignment. If 
necessary, we will reassess our  membership  to ensure  consistency  with  our sustainability values and objectives. 
 
Additionally, we aim to enhance environmental stewardship through stakeholder engagement, especially with regulators and 
investors to meet their expectations, improved environmental reporting and strong sustainability governance to monitor and 
achieve our climate action goals. 
 
We uphold transparency and accountability in sustainability reporting  by  aligning  with  Malaysia’s  National Sustainability 
Reporting Framework ("NSRF"), which incorporates the IFRS including IFRS S1 and S2 and integrates the Task Force on Climate-
related Financial Disclosures ("TCFD") recommendations as baseline for sustainability disclosures. 
 
More  information is outlined in the ISSB Sustainability Statement of Genting Berhad's Integrated Annual Report 2025.

Energy Consumption 
 
Genting Berhad expanded its energy consumption data coverage in 
2023 to include all principal operational units. This report presents 
Group energy consumption data for 2023 to 2025, covering Genting 
Berhad's corporate office at Wisma Genting, Leisure & Hospitality 
(Genting Malaysia, Genting Singapore and Resorts World Las Vegas), 
Energy (Genting Energy) and Plantations (Genting Plantations).  
 
At the Group level (comprising the Group’s key operational units and 
the Company’s corporate office), total energy consumption  reduced in 
2025 driven by energy efficiency efforts, mainly from Energy Division, 
reaching 1,744,158  MWh in 2025 (from 1,780,132 MWh in 2024). 
 
The energy intensity per Ringgit revenue was broadly stable at around 
0.063kWh in 2025 compared with previous year (2024: 0.064kWh). 
 
The Leisure & Hospitality division (including three mega integrated 
resorts) remained the largest energy consumer , followed by  the Energy 
division in the past three years. 
 
The energy consumption at Genting Berhad’s corporate office 
decreased by 2.4% in 2025 to 222 MWh (from 228 MWh in 2024). This 
reduction was driven by energy conservation initiatives led by the 
Human Resources team, including awareness campaigns, targeted 
notiﬁcations and energy-saving contests, which encouraged employees 
to adopt energy-efficient practices. 
Genting Berhad’s operating units are actively  
involved with many organisations dedicated to  
climate-related issues, notably: 
 
• Genting Singapore is a founding member of  
the Sentosa Carbon Neutral Network and a  
member of the Global Sustainable Tourism  
Council. 
 
• Genting Plantations is an active member of  
RSPO since 2006, contributing to the  
development and adoption of sustainable  
palm oil practices. The company participated  
in the RSPO Principles and Criteria Review  
Task Force, leading to the endorsement of  
the RSPO P&C 2018. It also partners with  
conservation organisations such as BORA  
and HUTAN for habitat restoration and  
wildlife monitoring, contributing to climate  
action efforts. 
 
• Genting Malaysia is involved with Jabatan  
Perhilitan Malaysia and the Forest Research  
Institute Malaysia on forest conservation  
initiatives. 
 
Additional memberships are disclosed in our  
corporate website at www.genting.com. 
GHG Emissions 
 
The Group’s Scopes 1, 2 and 3 emissions are calculated in accordance with the GHG Protocol: A Corporate Accounting and 
Reporting Standard (2004).The Group's total GHG emissions intensity amounted to 184.0 metric tonnes of carbon dioxide 
equivalent ("tCO2e") per RM million revenue in 2025 (2024: 167.7 tCO2e per RM million revenue). This represents a year‑on‑year 
increase of 9.7%, mainly due to additional coverage of emissions disclosures from our plantation operations in Indonesia. The 
emissions for 2023 and 2024 were restated due to alignment of data from the Leisure & Hospitality and Energy divisions. 
 
The Group’s Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions reporting encompass all business divisions. 
• Scope 1 emissions (direct GHG emissions from sources owned or controlled by a company) totalled 4,563,513 tCO2e in 2025,  
representing about 89.5% of the Group's total emissions. 
 
• Scope 2 emissions (indirect emissions from purchased energy) are calculated using location-based method as deﬁned by 
the GHG Protocol Corporate Standard. It amounted to 291,769 tCO 2e in 2025, a 4.8% increase year-on-year , representing 
about 5.7% of the Group's total emissions. 
 
• Scope 3 emissions amounted 245,029 tCO 2e in 2025, representing 4.8% of the Group's total emissions. Emissions from 
business travel and employee commuting amounted to 25,752 tCO2e in 2025, 21% reduction as compared to the previous 
year resulting from initiatives by the Group's operational teams, including provide onsite accommodation, optimise staff 
transportation and providing green transportation options. We target to extend Scope 3 reporting to all key operational 
units within the next two years. 
 
The Energy division, comprising power generation and oil and gas operations, accounted for 80.2% of total emissions in 2025 
and remained the Group’s largest contributor to GHG emissions. The Energy division’s Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions 
amounted to 4,084,519 tCO₂e and 6,149 tCO₂e, respectively. The Leisure & Hospitality division accounted for 9.6% (489,665 
tCO₂e), while the Plantations division accounted for 10.2% (519,643 tCO₂e) of total emissions in 2025. 
 
Scope 2 emissions at Genting Berhad’s corporate office decreased by 0.3% in 2025 to 172 tCO₂e (from 173 tCO₂e  in 2024).

Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions are based primarily on metered and invoiced data, while Scope 3 emissions are calculated using 
activity‑based estimates where direct measurement is not feasible. Uncertainties are managed through consistent methodologies, 
internal validation and periodic reviews, with independent third‑party veriﬁcation applied where applicable.  
 
Genting Singapore engaged an external veriﬁer in 2025 to undertake a limited assurance in GHG emissions across Scopes 1, 2 
and selected Scope 3 categories. The GHG emissions data for Genting Plantations' operations in Malaysia have been independently 
audited in accordance with the International Sustainability and Carbon Certiﬁcation (“ISCC”) requirements, while others selected 
data has been veriﬁed through internal audit.  
 
The Group assessed all 15 Scope 3 categories and identiﬁed 8 categories as material for disclosure in 2025, while the remaining 
7 categories were excluded due to immaterial impact and limited relevance to the operations. For material categories not yet 
fully tracked, such as prior years’ data for Category 8, the Group is enhancing data collection and will expand coverage in future 
reporting periods.  
 
The Group does not currently account for GHG removals or storage and accordingly, no such disclosures are applicable.

=== PAGE 62 ===

Climate Change and GHG Management 
 
Genting Malaysia’s approach focuses on reducing 
environmental impact and enhancing operational efficiency 
through sustainable practices, innovation and continuous 
improvement in resource management. Climate 
considerations are embedded across the company’s business 
strategy, research and development as well as technology 
investments. 
 
This integration future-proofs operations while delivering 
positive environmental outcomes. Climate and environmental 
risk has been formally embedded within Genting Malaysia’s 
Top Risk Register , where it is designated as Top Risk Number 
Two and reﬂected in Genting Malaysia’s Risk Register and Risk 
Appetite documentation. 
 
Genting Malaysia manages climate- and environmental- 
related risks across all departments, including operations, 
ﬁnance and sustainability, through a dedicated assessment 
and mitigation process. This process considers physical 
climate-related impacts such as extreme weather events, 
ﬂooding and landslide risks and informs the implementation 
of measures including energy-efficiency improvements and 
site-speciﬁc resilience measures. 
 
Climate risks and opportunities are also incorporated into 
ﬁnancial planning, including both operational expenditure 
and capital expenditure. The ﬁnancial planning includes 
evaluating potential costs and savings associated with 
initiatives that enhance resilience and sustainability, such as 
renewable energy adoption, water conservation systems, 
sustainable infrastructure investments and ongoing 
inspection, maintenance  and timely repair programmes. 
By providing on-site accommodation to employees, it 
signiﬁcantly reduced transportation-related emissions. 
 
Climate-driven physical risks, including landslides linked to 
increased precipitation, are actively monitored and managed. 
 
Mitigation measures focus on: 
• designing infrastructure with climate resilience in mind, 
informed by site-speciﬁc assessments; 
• implementing and maintaining adequate drainage systems 
and slope stabilisation measures; 
• robust emergency response procedures and business 
continuity planning; and 
• conducting regular inspections and proactive maintenance 
of critical utilities and infrastructure. 
 
These actions strengthen operational resilience and reduce 
disruption from extreme weather events.
GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202560
OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA 
OUR COMMITMENT 
 
Genting Malaysia recognises the urgent need for climate action and views climate change as both a risk and an opportunity 
across the short-, medium- and long-term. Peers and industry bodies engagement allows the company to contribute insights 
and technical perspectives as well as collaboration on joint initiatives that advance sustainable practices in supporting its 
climate change commitment. 
 
Genting Malaysia considers climate-related matters within the context of recognised global sustainability initiatives and 
evolving regulatory and policy developments. The company supports the climate targets of the operating countries, including 
Malaysia, the United Kingdom, Egypt and the Bahamas. These countries are signatories to the Paris Agreement, committing 
to limit global temperature rise to well below 2°C above pre-industrial levels while striving to cap warming at 1.5°C. The IPCC 
underscores the urgency of this ambition, warning that exceeding the 1.5°C threshold would trigger signiﬁcantly more severe 
climate impacts. 
 
Genting Malaysia is committed to managing energy consumption amid global challenges including climate change and 
resources depletion. The company managed energy use with a focus on efficiency and supporting broader sustainability 
objectives. Comprehensive energy assessments help identify opportunities to reduce environmental impact and enhance 
responsible operational practices.

Energy Management 
 
Genting Malaysia is committed to managing energy consumption amid global challenges 
like climate change and resources depletion. 
 
In 2025, energy consumption at Resorts World Genting’s hilltop operations decreased 
compared with the previous year , supported by ongoing energy efficiency projects and 
operational adjustments. 
 
Genting Malaysia is advancing initiatives to increase the use of renewable energy and 
improve energy efficiency across its operations, supporting to Malaysia’s goal of 
achieving net-zero emissions by 2050.

Operational Energy Efficiency Measures 
 
Resorts World Genting has implemented a range of energy efficiency initiatives to optimise overall consumption. These  include 
the installation of smart metering systems to monitor and analyse power usage, enabling the identiﬁcation of  high-
consumption areas and the implementation of targeted improvement measures. 
 
Operational adjustments have been made to optimise system performance, such as improving water distribution  efficiency 
and transitioning to energy-efficient LED lighting. In addition, Genting Malaysia continues to enhance the  performance of 
mechanical systems, including ventilation and compressed air equipment, to minimise energy losses. 
 
Further measures include reducing hot water temperatures, optimising equipment operating hours and shutting down  
selected facilities during off-peak periods to avoid unnecessary energy consumption.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont’d)
Solar Photovoltaic Panels 
 
Genting Malaysia has expanded renewable energy adoption 
after having initiated its solar panel programme, beginning 
with the rooftop at Awana SkyCentral at the mid-hill. In 2025, 
Genting Malaysia completed the installation of a 0.568 MWp 
solar photovoltaic system at the rooftop of Awana SkyCentral. 
The solar installation generated 16.5 MWh of electricity by 
end of 2025,  equivalent  to  an  average  daily  generation  of 
approximately 1,030 kWh per day. 
 
Energy-Efficient Lighting 
 
Genting Malaysia continues to enhance energy efficiency by 
progressively replacing outdated lighting with energy-saving 
LED alternatives across its facilities. In 2025, upgrades were 
completed in key public areas including Genting Grand Hotel, 
SkyAvenue Crystal Bridge, street lighting, and staff quarters 
(RSC2 and RSC5). The full transition to LED street lighting was 
also achieved, delivering reduced electricity consumption, 
lower carbon emissions and improved lighting efficiency and 
reliability. Further retroﬁts are ongoing across other Resorts 
World Genting premises. 
EV Mobility Hub at Genting Highlands 
 
In partnership with Shell Malaysia and Porsche Malaysia,  EV 
charging stations at Genting Highlands continue to  support 
electric vehicle users by providing reliable access  to charging 
facilities for guests. The site is equipped with  four 360 kW 
ultra-fast chargers and six 22 kW chargers.  In 2025, the EV 
charging infrastructure recorded a total  energy dispensed of 
more than 215,000kWh, reﬂecting growing adoption of 
electric vehicles among visitors. 
 
Awareness Programme 
 
Genting Malaysia’s S.A.V.E environment project  promotes 
environmental protection through employee  and community 
engagement. Resorts World Genting  organised initiatives 
such as Monthly Energy  Conservation Day, “Movie in the 
Dark” events and Earth  Day activities to raise awareness on 
energy efficiency and  encourage sustainable practices. 
United Kingdom 
 
Genting UK stepped up its energy efficiency efforts  
through a coordinated programme of operational  
improvements and equipment upgrades. Enhancements  
to the Building Maintenance System now cycle  
equipment during early and late periods, reducing off-
peak energy demand. The removal of gas boilers at two  
sites further lowered gas consumption.
United States 
 
Resorts World New York City continued its commitment 
to sustainable mobility with the ongoing expansion of 
its EV charging network. The property currently operates 
47 EV charging stations, providing convenient access for 
visitors driving electric vehicles. With the planned 
expansion and the development of a new parking 
garage, Resorts World New York City expects to offer 
more than 100 EV charging stations upon completion, 
signiﬁcantly enhancing charging accessibility for guests 
and staff. 
 
At Resorts World Catskills, the property’s design  
incorporates features such as an energy heat recovery  
system, a central utility plant with efficient cooling  
towers and bio-retention ponds that help manage  
stormwater runoff, contributing to reduced energy  
demand and improved environmental performance.  
Operational measures such as energy-efficient lighting,  
smart climate control systems and water-conserving  
ﬁxtures further support resource efficiency. 
 
Hilton Miami Downtown is ISO 50001:2018 certiﬁed,  
reﬂecting its commitment to energy management and  
sustainability. This certiﬁcation highlights the hotel’s  
efforts to improve energy efficiency, reduce  
environmental impact and optimise energy use. 
Bahamas 
 
Resorts World Bimini improved energy efficiency by  
upgrading to high-efficiency air-conditioning units,  
installing motion-sensor smart thermostats, adjusting  
temperature set points and adding blackout curtains in  
all guest rooms to reduce cooling demand. The resort  
also transitioned from diesel-powered trams and golf  
carts to electric vehicles, cutting diesel consumption by  
about 800 litres per month and gasoline use by about  
1,800 litres per month. These measures, supported by  
staff energy conservation awareness campaigns,  
collectively reduced fuel consumption, improved  energy 
efficiency and lowered operational emissions.
