OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE
Genting Singapore remains committed to reducing its carbon footprint by decarbonising energy generation, prioritising 
operational efficiency, expanding renewables and integrating innovative infrastructure solutions. These approaches aligned 
with the Singapore Green Plan 2030, a nationwide commitment to achieve net zero emissions by 2050 and supports the national 
target of peaking emissions by 2030.
Renewable Energy 
 
Genting Singapore continued to strengthen its renewable 
energy footprint by expanding its solar network across the 
resort. With the successful commissioning of new photovoltaic 
installations at four strategic locations – Illumination’s Minion 
Land, the Research Learning Centre, The Laurus and 
Singapore Oceanarium – its cumulative installed solar capacity 
reached 2,267 kWp across the resort. Collectively, these solar 
installations generate approximately 3.5 GWh of clean 
electricity annually, equalling roughly 770 4-room HDB ﬂats 
annual electricity consumption. The Illumination’s Minion 
Land and the Research Learning Centre are Green Mark 
Platinum Zero Energy Building certiﬁed, demonstrating the 
effectiveness of integrating renewable generation within high-
performance building designs. 
 
Green Transport 
 
Genting Singapore introduced Southeast Asia’s ﬁrst fully 
electric and driverless Robobus service in partnership with 
WeRide in June 2025, marking a signiﬁcant milestone in 
sustainable mobility innovation. 
 
Its buggy ﬂeet achieved 98% electriﬁcation (2024: 97%) and 
the remaining gasoline-powered buggies will be fully retired 
once they reach the end of their operational life, ensuring a 
complete shift to electric mobility. 
 
On the RWS8 route, Genting Singapore’s electric buses 
accounted for 13% of total trips in 2025. The introduction of 
cashless payment systems further streamlined operations, 
saving approximately 120 man-hours monthly and reducing 
resource use. 
 
The company maintained 15 EV charging stations in 2025 and 
upgraded four Tesla stations to DC fast-charging, reducing 
charging time from six hours to just 15 minutes. To support 
Singapore’s projected EV adoption growth, Genting Singapore 
plans to add nine additional stations in 2026 and convert 6% 
of existing car park lots into EV charging spaces between 2026 
and 2030. For new car parks under RWS 2.0 developments, 5 
megawatts of power capacity have been allocated speciﬁcally 
for EV charging infrastructure. 
 
Genting Singapore also introduced a dynamic booking and 
routing platform to optimise staff transportation. Through 
understanding these demand trends, the company 
introduced right-sized vehicles to its ﬂeet, such as 4- and 6-
seater options during low-demand periods to replace 
minibuses. These shifts help minimise fuel consumption and 
emissions, advancing its clean transportation goals. 
District Cooling Plant  (“DCP")  
 
DCP remains central to the resort’s sustainability strategy. By 
centralising cooling production instead of operating multiple 
standalone systems, the DCP enables large-scale optimisation 
in both energy and resource consumption. 
 
Genting Singapore advanced efficiency by ﬁne-tuning the 
Building Management System to optimise chiller 
performance, achieved a total plant efficiency of 0.63 kW/RT, 
surpassing the previous benchmark of 0.65 kW/RT in 2025. 
These improvements delivered estimated annual energy 
savings of 1.2 GWh, reducing carbon emissions and improving 
operational resilience. Planning is underway to replace two 
remaining older chillers with high-efficiency units using low-
GWP refrigerants to meet new Minimum Energy Efficiency 
Standards by 2029. 
 
Genting Singapore successful commissioned DCP 2.0 at an 
efficiency level of 0.65 kW/RT under partial loading. A key 
water efficiency feature of DCP 2.0 is the use of harvested 
rainwater and recovered air-conditioning condensate as 
cooling tower make-up water , complemented by higher 
cooling tower cycles of concentration through blowdown 
recycling. This integrated approach reduces reliance on 
NEWater by up to 30%, equivalent to annual savings of 
approximately 69,000 m³. 
 
Moving forward, two additional chillers and new heat 
exchangers will be installed under DCP 2.0, alongside an 
optimiser powered by advanced algorithm-based dynamic 
controls. 
 
With a total storage capacity of 25,000 refrigeration ton-hours  
the Thermal Energy Storage ("TES") system is designed to shift 
chilled water production to off-peak periods, thereby reducing 
peak electricity demand and optimising cooling plant 
operations. The next phases of works will focus on the 
integration and control ﬁne-tuning of TES into Genting 
Singapore’s existing cooling network. The system is projected 
to reach full integration by mid-2026, with a potential to 
achieve up to 1.2 GWh of energy savings annually.

This initiative is centred on three key priorities:

OUR FEATURE    RESORTS WORLD LAS VEGAS
At Resorts World Las Vegas, sustainability leadership is rooted in an enterprise-wide commitment 
to operational efficiency and environmentally responsible operations. This commitment drives the 
integrated resort’s efforts to implement solutions addressing climate change and supporting the 
transition to a low-carbon economy.  
 
Sustainability was prioritised in the design and construction of Resorts World Las Vegas, earning 
the resort a LEED Gold Certiﬁcation for Building Design and Construction. Since opening in June 
2021, Resorts World Las Vegas has allocated millions of dollars towards sustainability initiatives, 
including energy efficiency, sustainable transportation and water conservation and quality efforts. 
The integrated resort features 100% energy-efficient LED lighting and advanced building 
automations systems that enable continuous data collection and analysis.  
 
The property’s sustainability strategy adopts a collaborative approach to minimising GHG emissions 
and conserving natural resources. Its environmental management system aligns with ISO 50001 
(Energy Management System), ISO 9001 (Quality Management) and ISO 14001 (Environmental 
Management) standards.  
 
Resorts World Las Vegas operated on 100% renewable power , sourced directly from within Nevada. 
The resort’s overall electricity consumption decreased by nearly 3% year-on-year . With the property’s 
consumption sourced 100% from renewable power , its efforts were redirected towards reducing 
natural gas consumption and Scope 1 emissions. Through conservation programmes and efficiency 
measures, Resorts World Las Vegas achieved nearly 2% year-on-year reduction in natural gas usage. 
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS
Energy Management 
 
Energy management is central to Genting Plantations’ climate-related strategy, as energy use across estates, mills and 
downstream facilities signiﬁcantly affects GHG emissions and expose to transition risks. The company utilises a mix of 
renewable and non-renewable energy sources, with energy demand varying by site and operations. Renewable energy 
primarily comes from biomass, such as palm ﬁbres, kernel shells and empty fruit bunches. Non-renewable fuels, especially 
diesel, remain necessary in remote operations in Sabah and Indonesia due to limited grid access. Genting Plantations’ ﬁrst 
biogas power generation facility at Genting Ayer Item Oil Mill was commissioned in November 2025. Energy efficiency is 
embedded into daily operations  through  measures  such  as  energy-efficient lighting, motion sensors, improved equipment 
maintenance and employee awareness initiatives. Energy use is monitored at the facility level, with efficiency improvements 
implemented based on site-speciﬁc feasibility rather than ﬁxed reduction targets. 
 
Emission Management 
 
Genting Plantations manages GHG emissions across its estates, mills and downstream operations in line with the GHG Protocol, 
using activity data from operational records and value chain information. The company reports absolute GHG emissions as its 
primary metric, with intensity metrics monitored internally. Reduction initiatives include bio-fertilizer R&D, renewable energy 
projects, methane capture systems, sustainable peatland management, zero-burning practices and process optimisation. Belt-
press technology has avoided 26,305 tCO2e in 2025.  
 
Progress toward Genting Plantations’ carbon neutrality targets – 2030 for Malaysian operations and 2035 for Indonesian 
operations – is supported through continuous performance improvement. A formal IFRS S2 transition plan is under 
development as baseline data are reﬁned. The company does not adopt internal carbon pricing or renewable energy certiﬁcates 
at this stage.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING ENERGY
To accelerate its transition to clean power , Genting Energy is diversifying its energy portfolio with renewable and low-carbon 
solutions across its operations. These strategic investments in sustainable technologies aim to enhance energy generation 
efficiency while reducing overall GHG emissions. 
 
Jangi wind farm, India 
 
The Group’s ﬁrst renewable energy project, the 91.8 MW onshore Jangi wind farm in Gujarat, India, has been generating wind-
powered electricity with minimal environmental impact since commencing operations in 2011. 
 
The wind farm generated 181 GWh of clean energy in year 2025. Since commercial operation in September 2011, it has supplied 
approximately 2,941 GWh to India’s electricity grid, helping offset nearly 2.5 million tonnes of CO2 emissions to date, including 
about 170 thousand tCO2e emissions in 2025 alone. The electricity produced by the Jangi wind farm in 2025 is equivalent to 
the annual consumption of approximately more than 120,000 residents in India.  
 
Banten power plant, Indonesia 
 
The Banten power plant is committed to responsible operations, deploying advanced technologies and continuous 
improvement to minimise its environmental impact. Since beginning operations in 2017, it has consistently met all local 
compliance requirements without exceedance and has maintained all existing ISO certiﬁcations, including ISO 14001 
Environmental Management Systems. 
 
The plant uses leading technologies such as electrostatic precipitators which removes 99.8% of ﬁne particles and stores them 
in ash silos for safe disposals. It is also equipped with a ﬂue gas desulfurisation system that harnesses seawater as a natural  
scrubber to eliminate harmful chemicals. Together , these systems signiﬁcantly reduce airborne emissions and support long-
term environmental performance. 
 
To further enhance operational efficiency, the plant has completed the construction of a coal dome. This structure reduces 
dust emissions while preserving coal quality, improving combustion efficiency which contributes to lower overall emissions. 
As part of its broader strategy to reduce carbon footprint, the plant is integrating a 1MWp rooftop solar system to generate 
renewable auxiliary power for on-site use, directly decreasing the power plant’s GHG emissions.  
 
These combined measures not only demonstrate the power plant’s strict adherence to environmental laws and underscore 
the Group’s commitment to compliance, but also reﬂects its dedicated approach to cleaner generation and environmental 
stewardship. 
 
Dongwucha solar power plant, China 
 
As part of Genting Energy’s commitment to advancing sustainable energy solutions through innovative renewable projects, 
the 120 MWp aquaculture-complementary solar plant serves as a multi-dimensional carbon reduction model that integrates 
photovoltaics, aquaculture and port operations, in alignment with China’s dual carbon strategy. The solar power plant achieved 
full-capacity grid connection in May 2025.  
 
Since commercial operation, the solar power plant generated approximately 106GWh of clean energy, offsetting an estimated 
89,000 tonnes of carbon dioxide based on internal assessments. The electricity generated is supplied to the Dongwu Port area 
through the local grid, helping to reduce the carbon emissions intensity of surrounding industries and supporting the 
optimisation of the region’s energy structure.  
 
Zhoushan gas power plant, China 
 
Currently under construction, the Zhoushan gas power plant represents the Group’s strategic investment in cleaner and more 
energy efficient infrastructure, developed in collaboration with SDIC Power Holdings Co., Ltd in Zhejiang Province, China. The 
power plant comprises two 745 MW gas-ﬁred units equipped with state-of-the-art H-class gas turbines featuring advanced dry 
low-NOx combustion technology. These turbines are designed to co-ﬁre liqueﬁed natural gas with 10% to 50% hydrogen, 
enabling a progressive shift toward lower-carbon fuels. This hydrogen-ready capability enables the plant to signiﬁcantly lower 
its carbon intensity and GHG emission by integrating cleaner fuel mix into the power generation process.

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202568
WHY IT 
MATTERS
Climate change has made water security a critical concern 
with signiﬁcant impacts on people and the environment. 
Temperature  changes caused by climate change can 
impact water resources by placing additional demands on 
already strained water supplies. Hill operations carry an 
elevated erosion risk from heavy rain if not managed 
properly, while irregular water supply can severely affect 
crops and the agricultural communities. 
 
Long-term neglect of water management may worsen 
climate challenges, affect communities and threaten the 
sustainability of our business. Effective management of hill 
operations is crucial to mitigate erosion from heavy rains 
and ensure a stable water supply for agricultural 
communities and their crops. 
 
Embracing  sustainable,  integrated  and  holistic water 
management is crucial for the Group’s businesses. By 
taking a proactive approach to water management, 
Genting Berhad enhances its resilience to climate-related 
disruptions, ensuring the Group’s operations remain 
strong and adaptable to unforeseen changes in water 
availability. 
 
Geographically, our Group operates in nine countries, of 
which two of its eleven leisure properties are in water- 
stressed regions, namely Resorts World Sentosa in 
Singapore and Resorts World Las Vegas in Nevada, US. 
These two properties contributed collectively 40% and 43% 
respectively to the Group’s total revenue and total assets 
in 2025. 
 
Singapore is amongst the most water-stressed countries 
in the world, due to the lack of natural water resources 
such as large rivers, natural springs or glaciers as well as 
limited land to collect and store rainwater¹. The water 
demand for this country is projected to double by 2065, 
with non-domestic sector accounting for two-thirds of this 
demand. 
 
Nevada is considered a “water-stressed site” because it is 
the driest state in the United States, with very low average 
precipitation². Its population is largely concentrated in the 
southern region which receives even less rain, putting 
signiﬁcant pressure on its limited water resources, 
primarily sourced from the Colorado River through Lake 
Mead. According to the Bureau of Reclamation’s August 
2025 24-month study, Lake Mead will remain in a “Level 1 
Shortage Condition”, sitting 20 feet below the threshold 
that triggers water shortage conditions³. This makes water 
conservation a critical issue for the state. 
 
Nevada is working towards its goal of reducing water use 
to 86 gallons per person per day⁴. On 26 June 2024, the 
Nevada Irrigation District passed Water Resolution 2024-
26 declaring a water shortage emergency. This resolution 
mandates all customers to reduce their water usage by 
3%, as compared to 2024. 
 
Malaysia has introduced the Water Sector Transformation 
2040 Agenda in the Twelfth Malaysia Plan (2021 – 2025) 
and the water sector is envisioned to be an economic 
enabler and a dynamic growth engine to ensure water 
security for all. 
100% 
of GENTING 
operating properties 
have water 
management plans 
and strategies
Genting Berhad is committed to responsible water stewardship, which is part of the 
sustainability strategy in its Sustainability Policy. Our policy encourages sustainable water 
practices to reduce water consumption and improve water efficiency across all business 
units. Our operating teams are committed to establishing an efficient and effective water 
management system, focusing on conserving water resources and reducing municipal 
water usage. 
 
All our operating properties (covering 11 leisure properties, four power plants, one oil ﬁeld, 
14 palm oil mills and over 243,200 hectares of plantation and property development 
landbank) and our corporate offices maintain respective dedicated water management 
plans tailored to regional conditions and operational needs. These plans outline strategies 
for conservation, efficiency improvements and responsible sourcing.

WATER MANAGEMENT
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
Group-wide initiatives focus on: 
• diversifying water sources:  increasing the use of recycled 
water and alternative supplies to reduce reliance on 
municipal water; 
• improving water efficiency:  through technology 
upgrades, process optimisation and monitoring systems; 
and 
• reducing overall water usage:  implementing water-
saving measures across all business operations and 
promoting behavioral changes among employees. 
 
Each business division is at varying stages of adopting water-
saving measures and exploring alternative water sources to 
address water scarcity and sustainability challenges. Key 
actions include reducing municipal water use, prioritising 
recycled water and adopting innovative solutions for water 
reuse. 
 
Our teams regularly evaluate risks to both water quality and 
availability and integrate mitigation measures into 
operational planning. This approach reinforces our 
commitment to sustainable water practices across the Group. 
 
We are guided by the national and state policies and 
guidelines of the respective regions of our operations to 
ensure sustainable water use. These policies and guidelines 
include: 
• Malaysia’s Water Sector Transformation 2040 Agenda and 
its strategies. 
• Malaysia’s national water resources policy that includes the 
National Water Balance System. 
• Singapore’s national water policy, also known as the “Four 
National Taps” that focuses on sustainable water use 
through a combination of local catchment water , imported 
water , NEWater (recycled water) and desalinated water . 
• The United States of America’s The Clean Water Act, Safe 
Drinking Water Act, Water Resources Development Act and 
National Water Policy (water supply, conservation and 
management) amongst other federal and state policies 
focusing on water management and conservation. 
• New York State’s Environmental Conservation Law, Water 
Supply Act, Drought Contingency Plan and Floodplain 
Management Program and New York City Watershed 
Protection Program. 
• Las Vegas’ Colorado River Water Rights and Colorado River 
Compact, water conservation policies such as water smart 
landscaping, water-efficient appliances, rebates for turf 
removal, recycling wastewater , desalination, Lake Mead 
and Drought Contingency Plan and Nevada Irrigation 
District Water Resolution 2024-2026. 
• Indonesia’s National Water Resources Management Policy 
and Government Regulation No. 30 of 2024 regarding 
Water Resource Management. 
• China’s primary water management policy centres around 
the “Three Red Lines” concept, which includes strict 
limitations on total water usage, water use efficiency and 
pollutant load control in water function zones. 
• The United Kingdom’s EU Water Framework Directive. 
• The Bahamas’ Water and Sewerage Corporation Act. 
• India’s National Water Policy. 
 
ENGAGEMENTS WITH STAKEHOLDERS 
 
We engage with our stakeholders to gather feedbacks on 
environmental issues, including water use and water-related 
risks that could impact our operations, not just at the water-
stressed regions but across our world-wide operations. 
 
Our stakeholders include local authorities, regulatory bodies, 
environmental groups, customers, suppliers and the local 
communities. Their feedbacks ensure that our water 
management strategies remain relevant, progressive and 
responsive to evolving challenges. 
 
The Group actively engages with stakeholders to strengthen 
water conservation efforts and align with local environmental 
priorities. Engagement approach includes: 
• joint risk assessments:  working with local authorities, 
industry peers and community stakeholders to assess 
water availability risks and develop shared mitigation 
strategies; 
• participation in conservation initiatives : supporting local 
water conservation and restoration programs to protect 
critical water resources and ecosystems; 
• awareness and education : implementing community and 
industry awareness campaigns to promote sustainable 
water usage practices; and 
• collaboration solutions:  exploring opportunities for 
industrial ecology, such as leveraging water waste streams 
from other companies operating at shared sites, to 
optimise water use and reduce overall consumption. 
 
 
WATER PERFORMANCE 
 
Group water data in 2025 encompassed operations at Resorts 
World Genting, Resorts World Langkawi and Resorts World 
Kijal (Malaysia), Genting UK (United  Kingdom), Resorts World 
Sentosa (Singapore), Resorts World Las Vegas (United States), 
Banten power plant (Indonesia), Chengdaoxi block (China), 
Kasuri block (Indonesia), Genting FLNG (Indonesia) and oil 
palm plantations (Malaysia). Total water withdrawal in 2025 
amounted to 860,811 megalitres (“ML”) with total water 
discharge recorded at 851,826 ML, resulting in a net water use 
or total water consumption of 13,723 ML for the year .  
 
In addition, there were zero incidents of non-compliance 
cases on water quality, quantity permits, standards or 
regulatory requirements in 2025, underscoring the Group’s 
commitment to strong environmental management practices. 
 
Our team in Singapore has set a target to reduce municipal 
water withdrawal intensity by 30% by 2030, relative to the 
2015 baseline. We are committed to supporting this initiative 
and aligning efforts to establish a uniﬁed reduction target 
across the Group.

by operating units. Water performance data  for 2023 and 2024

Hospitality division and the Company’s corporate office.

consumption within the Group in 2025.

efforts and exploring innovative solutions to reduce usage and 
enhance water recycling.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA 
OUR COMMITMENT 
Genting Malaysia adopts a proactive approach to water 
management in line with the Dublin Principles, which 
recognise freshwater as a ﬁnite and vulnerable resource 
essential to life, development and environmental protection, 
as well as access to clean water and sanitation as a 
fundamental human right. 
 
Given Genting Malaysia’s large visitor footprint, water 
conservation is a key operational priority, helping to reduce 
costs, safeguard local water resources from overuse and 
ensure long-term availability for future needs. This 
commitment to reducing water consumption and improving 
efficiency across all facilities reﬂects the company’s broader 
dedication to responsible and sustainable resource 
management.  
 
Key initiatives included: 
• installing low-ﬂow ﬁxtures, dual-ﬂush toilets and efficient 
plumbing to reduce consumption without compromising 
guest experience; 
• implementing rainwater harvesting systems for irrigation 
and other non-potable uses; and 
• adopting greywater recycling systems to reuse treated 
wastewater for toilets and landscaping.
OUR APPROACH 
 
Genting Malaysia’s water management covers 100% of its operations at Resorts World Genting. Genting Malaysia does not 
have facilities, assets, production, or revenue in water-stressed regions and therefore does not engage with stakeholders at 
water-stressed sites. 
 
Water-related risks assessment 
Genting Malaysia has undertaken comprehensive studies on the water supply and sewage treatment systems at Genting 
Highlands to identify potential water-related risks and support ongoing management. The ﬁndings inform the company’s 
understanding of water security, operational resilience and regulatory considerations and are used to guide water 
management planning and risk mitigation measures.
Malaysia
In response to these risks, Genting Malaysia has implemented targeted initiatives to enhance water resilience, improve 
system efficiency and ensure regulatory compliance across operations.

Malaysia (cont'd)
OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont’d)
Water Withdrawal and Capture 
Genting Highlands sources raw water from surface dams 
and natural streams, with treatment carried out in full 
compliance with SPAN and DOE guidelines. Weekly water 
safety inspections, conducted in line with MOH 
requirements, ensure that all treated water remains 
potable and safe for use across the property. 
 
To support sustainable water management, Genting 
Malaysia has implemented rainwater harvesting systems, 
which allow the harvesting of around 100,000 litres of 
rainwater per year . 
 
Water Discharge 
Genting Highlands operates its own Sewage Treatment 
Plants to treat wastewater before discharge into natural 
waterways. All treatment processes comply with Malaysia’s 
DOE Standard A. 
 
To strengthen oversight, Genting Malaysia has integrated 
a real-time wastewater treatment surveillance system into 
the existing Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition 
("SCADA") system. This system enables early detection of 
issues and immediate remedial action, supporting full 
compliance with regulatory requirements while 
safeguarding surrounding ecosystems and communities. 
 
Water Saving Measures 
At Genting Highlands, the SCADA system provides real-time 
oversight of water supply and discharge. The system issues 
immediate alerts when anomalies are detected, enabling 
prompt corrective action. Phasing in digital water meters 
improves the accuracy of detecting non-revenue water 
losses, further strengthening monitoring. As a result, non-
revenue water is maintained at approximately 8% annually, 
well below the Peninsular Malaysia and F.T. Labuan average 
of 34.3%. 
 
Reusing greywater for cleaning tasks supports Sewage 
Treatment Plant usage efficiency. Water-efficient ﬁttings, 
such as ﬂow restrictors, reduce consumption and promote 
sustainable water management across the property. Water 
saving initiatives at Resorts World Genting are actively 
promoted by the Human Resource Department in 2025. 
 
Collectively, these initiatives are practical and operational 
in nature, reﬂecting efforts to improve water efficiency and 
support responsible water management at Resorts World 
Genting.

WATER MANAGEMENT

OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE
Climate change is heightening Singapore’s water security risks as dry spells intensify and national demand is expected to 
doubled by 2065, increasing the need for efficient industrial and commercial water stewardship. As an integrated resort with 
substantial water consumption, Genting Singapore recognises its responsibility to reduce demand, enhance efficiency and 
lessen reliance on energy-intensive municipal water sources. 
 
Our water conservation strategy focuses on two key priorities:
Diversifying our water sources 
To reduce our reliance on potable water , we diversiﬁed our 
water sources for our operational needs. Our approach 
includes using seawater to support our marine life, 
NEWater as a source of chilled water for our District 
Cooling Plant (“DCP“), rainwater for our resortʼs landscape 
irrigation and reclaiming DCPʼs NEWater usage.
Increasing efficiency and reducing usage 
By exploring alternative water sources and technological 
upgrades, we aim to reduce our water usage at its source.  
We actively seek innovative technologies and 
partnerships within the industry to testbed water 
conservation initiatives, ensuring prudent water usage.
2025 Water Performance 
 
In 2025, Genting Singapore recorded total water withdrawal of 2,406,596m 3, with potable water (46%) and NEWater (20%), 
amounting to 66% of its water withdrawal. Compensating its municipal water sources are rainwater (6%) used for irrigation, 
seawater (27%) for Singapore Oceanariumʼs operations and reclaimed water (1%) consisting of condensate water to 
supplement its DCP and recycled water from its cooling tower blowdown. Genting Singapore’s water withdrawal reduction 
target is set to reduce its municipal water withdrawal intensity by 30% by 2030 against its 2015 baseline. Potable water usage 
in 2025 remained stable across despite increased operations. This was achieved by addressing leaks and water inefficiencies 
identiﬁed in its water network following the discoveries from its water audit. NEWater usage increase in 2025 compared to 
2024 due to the opening of Singapore Oceanarium in July, introducing new ﬁsh habitats that requires cooling, supported by 
DCP 2.0. To ensure Genting Singapore stay within its water reduction target during RWS 2.0’s expansion, the company will 
seek new ways to diversity its water sources and increasing water usage efficiency.

WATER MANAGEMENT
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
Our Progress 
 
Genting Singapore has identiﬁed key opportunities to enhance water conservation and efficiency across Resorts World  
Sentosa. Action taken to reduce water use included: 
 
Life Support System  
 
Singapore Oceanarium’s life support systems require continuous, energy-intensive seawater cooling to maintain precise 
temperature and water quality conditions essential for marine animal welfare. To enhance efficiency, a heat recovery system 
was introduced in 2025 that circulates cooler backwashed seawater through a heat exchanger to pre-cool warmer incoming 
seawater before it enters the life support system, reducing reliance on mechanical chillers and delivering approximately 60,000 
RTh of energy savings per month.  
 
Adventure Cove Waterpark  
 
Adventure Cove Waterpark’s operations require continuous and robust water ﬁltration to maintain water quality and safety 
standards and Genting Singapore recognises that this represents a signiﬁcant driver of water and energy consumption. To 
optimise this, the company uses Defender ﬁltration systems that cleanses impurities more efficiently than conventional sand 
ﬁltration systems. This upgrade reduces water consumption from ﬁlter cleaning processes, lowers associated pumping energy 
requirements and chemical usage across waterpark operations. The Defender ﬁltration systems support improved water 
stewardship and overall resource efficiency.
Water Recirculation System 
 
All rides at Adventure Cove Waterpark are equipped with 
dedicated drainage systems that capture and channel water back 
into the park's ﬁltration infrastructure. This design minimises 
water loss from splash-out and operational discharge, 
maximising water recovery and reuse across park operations. 
 
Closed-Loop Filtration 
 
The waterpark operates a closed-loop ﬁltration system where 
used water is continuously treated and returned to circulation. 
This self-cycling approach reduces freshwater intake 
requirements by maintaining water quality through ﬁltration and 
treatment rather than discharge and replacement, supporting 
operational water efficiency while maintaining safety and quality 
standards. 
 
Waterless Car Wash 
 
Genting Singapore introduced a waterless car-wash technology 
through partnership with Shiokr that completely rethinks how 
water is used in vehicle cleaning. Instead of the 150 litres typically 
consumed in a conventional wash, each wash now requires only 
0.5 litre, amounting to a remarkable 99.7% reduction in water use. 
This shift saved an estimated 3.2 million litres of water in 2025.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 77
WATER MANAGEMENT
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
OUR FEATURE    RESORTS WORLD LAS VEGAS
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS
Genting Plantations is actively pursuing a sustainable water management strategy aimed at reducing water usage intensity 
across its operations by 2050, focusing on responsible consumption within its oil mills while safeguarding shared water 
resources and meeting workforce needs. 
 
Its operations mainly withdraw water from municipal suppliers, reducing dependence on surface and groundwater sources 
and minimising potential impacts on surrounding communities. As its operational scope evolves, Genting Plantations continues 
reﬁning water-use measurements and baselines, with oil mills and downstream facilities already recording improvements in 
water-use intensity, in line with long-term water stewardship objectives. 
 
According to the World Resources Institute Aqueduct 4.0 water risk assessment, none of Genting Plantations’ oil mills or 
reﬁneries operate in areas with high or extremely high baseline water stress. As of December 2025, no water-related risks 
were identiﬁed and there were zero incidents of non-compliance with water quality permits, standards or regulatory 
requirements across its operations.
Resorts World Las Vegas is committed to environmental sustainability, recognising 
the  importance of conserving natural resources to address current challenges and 
safeguard the future. Situated in Southern Nevada, Resorts World Las Vegas 
understands the critical vitality of water stewardship. The integrated resort 
prioritises responsible water management to ensure its long-term operational 
resilience and provide beneﬁts to the broader Las Vegas Valley. 
 
At Resorts World Las Vegas, year-over-year water consumption decreased 3%, an annual savings of nearly seven million gallons 
of water . The integrated resort strives to improve water efficiency, access and resilience through initiatives such as efficient 
irrigation systems; native, drought tolerant landscaping; low-ﬂow plumbing ﬁxtures; cooling tower management standards; 
and onsite ﬁltered water sources. 
 
Beyond direct conservation and stewardship efforts, Resorts World Las Vegas collaborates with external partners – 
governments, businesses and non-governmental organisations – to address regional and global water challenges. Such efforts 
include public-private sector innovation projects, research studies and fundraising for safe drinking water projects worldwide.

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 202578
WATER MANAGEMENT
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS (cont’d)
OUR FEATURE    GENTING ENERGY
Genting Plantations implemented the following measures to tackle water-related risk and promote responsible water usage 
within the company:
Moving forward, Genting Plantations will strengthen water use measurement and operational baselines across its operations 
to enhance monitoring and data collection. This will help improve its practices and identify areas that require immediate actions 
to reduce water use. The company will also apply measures implemented at oil mills and downstream facilities across its 
operations to optimise and reduce water-use intensity, thereby strengthening its overall water management practices.
Genting Energy maintains a strong commitment to responsible water stewardship, 
recognising water as a critical resource for sustainable power generation and resource 
extraction. This commitment is supported by a systematic approach that incorporates 
advanced water assessment tools, comprehensive monitoring of all water usage 
including demineralised, service and potable water and the implementation of targeted 
action plans. Any irregularities identiﬁed are promptly investigated, with corrective 
measures undertaken to optimise water efficiency and minimise environmental 
impacts. 
 
In the Power division, the Banten power plant operates on-site certiﬁed water station that produces high quality drinking water 
for employees. This water meets the stringent hygiene standards set by the Indonesian Ministry of Health’s Disease Prevention 
and Control Office. By eliminating the reliance on bottled water , the plant signiﬁcantly reduces single-use plastic waste and 
the indirect water footprint associated with bottled water production. As part of its ongoing water conservation efforts, the 
plant maintains strict controls over its process water management, including safely discharging 99% of treated water from its 
cooling system back to the sea in accordance with regulatory requirements and environmental best practices. 
 
In the Oil & Gas division, the Kasuri PSC team has enhanced its water management practices by introducing rainwater 
harvesting to supplement the existing groundwater sources. In 2025, the system collected and treated 3,148 kilolitres of 
rainwater , which was used for daily camp needs, including personal hygiene and operational support activities. As part of the 
camp’s ongoing renovation plan, new wastewater treatment infrastructure - including dedicated biotreatment units for laundry 
and kitchen effluent is planned for installation. These upgrades help ensure that effluents will be properly treated prior to 
discharge. This commitment to responsible water management is also evident at Genting Energy’s offshore oil facility in the 
Chengdaoxi block in China, where approximately 2.9 million cubic meters of produced water was recycled through direct 
reinjection into the reservoir . This environmentally friendly practice not only supports water reuse but also helps maintain 
reservoir pressure, thereby improving oil recovery efficiency.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 79
WASTE, POLLUTION AND MATERIALS CONSUMPTION
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
WHY IT 
MATTERS
Genting Berhad recognises that responsible waste management, alongside minimising pollution and materials 
consumption are essential for sustainable development. Waste and excessive consumption of materials not only harms 
ecosystems and public health but also places a strain on local government systems, particularly waste disposal 
infrastructure. By adopting a practical and managed approach to waste reduction, pollution control and resource 
efficiency, the Group strives to address these challenges responsibly. 
 
The government of Malaysia has launched a 10-year policy direction for solid waste management under its Circular 
Economy Blueprint for Solid Waste (2025 to 2035) which forecasts the implementation of measures such as “Extended 
Producer Responsibility”, “Pay- As-You-Throw” and “Zero Waste to Landﬁll” Certiﬁcation. Additionally, the aim is to 
support the formation of a circular economy association to help achieve the national recycling rate of 40% in 2025. 
Singapore has the Zero Waste Masterplan under the Singapore Green Plan 2030 with separate targets.
Genting Berhad is committed to responsible 
waste management as part of its broader 
sustainability strategy. As part of the Group’s 
broader sustainability efforts to minimise 
environmental impact, waste reduction, recycling 
and responsible disposal initiatives are actively 
practised across all our Group’s global operations 
to support a circular economy and reduce landﬁll 
dependency. Through continuous monitoring, 
adopting innovative measures and collaborating 
with stakeholders, we aim to minimise 
environmental impact and contribute to a cleaner , 
more sustainable future. 
 
Our Group is committed to set clear , time-bound 
objectives that go beyond regulatory 
requirements to reduce or avoid pollution, waste 
and minimise resource use. Genting Berhad  
targets to maintain the recycling waste rate at 
90% and above annually, zero environmental 
ﬁnes and penalties reported annually and 
continuously minimising resource use across 
business operations.  
 
Our business units take a proactive approach to 
waste management with Genting Singapore 
targeting to achieve a 50% reduction of  
operational waste-to-landﬁll intensity by 2030, as 
compared to their baseline year , in line with 
Singapore’s Zero Waste Masterplan. Genting 
Malaysia has set speciﬁc time targets including to 
convert on average one tonne of food waste daily 
into fertiliser , equating to 365 tonnes of food 
waste recycled annually. 
OUR APPROACH
Key Waste Management Strategies 
 
1.    Waste Prevention and Reduction 
a. Encourage waste minimisation initiatives at company level 
and engage with business units on their waste 
management initiatives. 
b. Promote resource efficiency through process optimisation, 
digitalisation and sustainable procurement practices. 
 
2.    Recycling and Circular Economy Initiatives 
a. Engage with operating teams to enhance waste 
segregation systems to maximise recycling efforts group-
wide and collaborate with suppliers and partners to 
promote the use of recyclable and biodegradable 
materials. 
a. Support initiatives to repurpose waste into new materials 
or products. 
 
3.    Responsible Disposal and Waste Diversion 
a. Prioritise waste-to-energy solutions where feasible to 
reduce landﬁll dependency. 
b. Ensure  proper  handling  and  disposal of hazardous waste 
in compliance with environmental regulations. 
c. Monitor and improve landﬁll diversion rates through 
increased recycling and composting initiatives. 
 
4.    Stakeholder Engagement and Awareness 
a. Engage  employees,  suppliers,  tenants and business 
partners in waste reduction initiatives. 
b. Conduct awareness programs and training sessions on 
sustainable waste management practices. 
 
5.    Monitoring, Compliance and Reporting 
a. Align waste management efforts with GRI 306: Waste 2020 
for transparent reporting. 
b. Track key waste metrics, including total waste generated, 
waste diverted from disposal and non-recycled waste. 
c. Ensure full compliance with local and international waste 
management regulations.

The Group  reported  total  waste  generated  of  about 1.26 
million tonnes in 2025, a 2% reduction compared with the 
previous year . Waste intensity in 2025 was 45.3 tonnes per 
million Ringgit, lowest in the three-year period. 
 
Recycled waste based on waste diverted from disposal was at 
a high of 97% and non-recycled waste was 3% of total waste 
generated in 2025. Hazardous waste was less than 0.01% in 
2025, mainly  attributable  by  the power plant operations in 
Indonesia and resort property in Malaysia. 
 
The Group targets to maintain a recycling waste rate of 90% 
and above annually, ensuring a responsible balance between 
our operational activities and the waste generated. We 
exceeded this target by achieving a 97% recycling rate in 2025. 
 
Genting  Berhad  targeted  no  environmental  ﬁnes and 
penalties  at  company  level  annually.  There were no 
environmental ﬁnes and penalties reported at both company 
and group level in 2025. 
 
Our business units demonstrated strong commitment to 
environmental stewardship through achieving various 
certiﬁable schemes and environmental management systems 
that provide assurance of operational excellence, product 
consistency and performance efficiency. 
 
About 75% of operating sites were covered by recognised 
environmental management systems, including ISO 14001 
and sustainable palm oil certiﬁcations in 2025. 
 
 
Resorts World Genting (Engineering Department), Resorts  
World Las Vegas, Banten power plant and Kasuri block have 
achieved ISO 14001:2015 certiﬁcation recognised for effective 
environmental management, encompassing building services 
management, utilities supply and plant management, as well 
as landscaping and environmental services. 
 
In addition, Resorts World Las Vegas has achieved LEED Gold 
Certiﬁcation since 2021, underscoring the resort’s 
commitment to sustainability and environmental 
responsibility. 
 
Resorts World Sentosa has been certiﬁed to the GSTC 
Destination Criteria and GSTC Industry Criteria for Hotels and 
achieved the Events Industry Council’s 2022 Sustainable 
Events Standards Platinum certiﬁcation for Venue. 
 
The operating units of Genting Plantations have secured a 
range of sustainable palm oil certiﬁcations, including the 
Roundtable on Sustainable  Palm Oil, International 
Sustainability & Carbon Certiﬁcation, Malaysian Sustainable 
Palm Oil, and Indonesian Sustainable Palm Oil, with a deﬁned 
target to certify all remaining units. 
 
As part of its ongoing efforts to uphold high environmental 
and workplace standards, Genting Plantations remains 
guided by internationally recognised standards, including ISO 
14001:2015 Environmental Management Systems and ISO 
45001:2018 Occupational Health and Safety Management 
Systems, as well as industry-based standards such as the 
Malaysian Palm Oil Board’s Code of Good Agricultural and 
Milling Practices.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA 
OUR COMMITMENT 
Genting Malaysia is committed to responsible waste management by reducing waste at source, 
minimising landﬁll disposal, improving resource efficiency and implementing practical 
measures that lower environmental impacts while supporting effective operations. 
 
Key priorities included: 
• reducing waste at source through improved operational practices, procurement choices   and stakeholder engagement; 
• reducing food and organic waste through prevention, segregation and repurposing initiatives; 
• minimising plastic waste as part of broader waste reduction efforts, with a focus on limiting single-use plastics and 
increasing recycling and repurposing rates; and 
• reducing general waste sent to landﬁll through improved segregation, monitoring and waste handling practices. 
 
Genting Malaysia collaborates with external stakeholders through local and global environmental initiatives to reduce pollution, 
waste and resource use, including partnerships that promote circularity, resource efficiency, joint sustainability efforts and the 
exploration of industrial ecology opportunities. To ensure accountability, the company has set waste-reduction targets for 
Resorts World Genting, monitors progress annually and report performance to support transparency and continuous 
improvement in line with its sustainability objectives.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont’d)
OUR APPROACH 
Genting Malaysia engages visitors, guests, suppliers and tenants to embed an inclusive and effective waste management 
approach focused on prevention, responsible consumption and sustainable practices while ensuring operational 
continuity. The company also strengthens oversight of general waste and segregation at the Batang Kali Waste 
Management Plant to reduce waste volumes sent to the Bukit Tagar Landﬁll and achieve more sustainable outcomes. 
 
2025 Key Initiatives
Resorts World Genting has strengthened its waste-reduction and resource-efficiency efforts by eliminating paper 
newspapers in guest rooms, replacing them with digital access via PressReader and reducing single-use plastics through 
reusable fabric laundry covers, reﬁllable bathroom amenity dispensers and recycled-material hangers. Genting Malaysia 
is also transitioning to more sustainable key cards, introducing wooden cards at Crockfords Hotel and Genting Grand 
Hotel, while biodegradable cornstarch cards at others. 
 
Genting UK maintains a strong commitment to sustainable waste management, ensuring full compliance with UK 
legislation while advancing broader environmental objectives. Genting UK introduced food waste bins across all locations 
in 2025 to comply with  new regulations, while also ensuring 100% of its waste is diverted from landﬁlls in partnership 
with B&W Waste Services, including site visits and employees’ training on hazardous waste management, supports their  
commitment to responsible waste disposal. 
 
Hilton Miami Downtown has implemented a range of waste management initiatives to reduce environmental impact  
and promote sustainable operations. These initiatives focus on circularity, plastic reduction and responsible disposal,  
supporting both environmental and social outcomes. 
 
Waste management infrastructure in Resorts World Bimini is currently at an early stage of development, with limited 
local capacity for recycling and a  single landﬁll serving the island. While off-island processing options are constrained, 
Genting Malaysia actively seeks collaborative  solutions to reduce environmental impact. In this context, the company 
has partnered with Virgin Voyages to responsibly transport used cooking oil to the latter’s vessels for recycling in the 
United States, ensuring proper treatment and circular reuse  rather than landﬁll disposal.
FOOD AND ORGANIC WASTE MANAGEMENT  
Organic waste, including food and garden waste, 
was segregated and composted into landscaping 
fertiliser using a MyHijau-certiﬁed composting 
machine, which meets recognised environmental 
standards. This process results in an estimated 
avoidance of 955.6 kgCO 2e per tonne of organic 
waste processed, compared to landﬁll disposal. 
Segregation at source was reinforced across all F&B 
outlets, supported by tenancy department guidance 
and team member awareness initiatives.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE
Singapore's Green Plan 2030 targets a 30% reduction in waste-to-landﬁll by 
2030 to extend Semakau Landﬁll's operational lifespan and reduce carbon 
emissions from incineration. As a major integrated resort, Genting Singapore 
recognises its responsibility to support this national objective through waste 
management practices that advance circular economy principles and deliver 
measurable impact. 
 
Our Approach 
 
Genting Singapore’s waste management strategy follows the Waste 
Management Hierarchy, prioritising prevention, reuse, recycling, recover and 
disposal. It is supported by the segregation of waste into 10 streams to 
maximise material recovery and circularity. The company engages team 
members and guests to encourage responsible consumption and disposal. 
Genting Singapore  also pilots innovative  solutions  with partners that 
transform waste into valuable resources, close material loops and explore new 
pathways for waste-to-resource conversion. These collaborations enable the 
company to test emerging technologies and scale proven solutions that 
support transition toward a circular operating model.

2025 Waste Performance 
 
Genting Singapore’s operational waste-to-
landﬁll intensity was at 0.0153 tonnes/m 2/year , 
and recycling rates were at 21% in 2025, 
compared to 2024’s performance of 0.0170 
tonnes/m2/year and 19%, respectively. 
 
Genting Singapore’s operational waste-to-
landﬁll intensity decreased in 2025 compared to 
2024 due to partial closures of Hard Rock Hotel, 
WEAVE, S.E.A. Aquarium and its Waterfront F&B 
establishments, traditionally higher waste-
generating areas. Complementing that is a 
decrease in single-use items within its hotels 
and Universal Studios Singapore back-of-house, 
thereby increasing recycling rates and reducing 
general waste volumes. 
 
Genting Singapore is making steady progress 
towards its goal of reducing operational waste-
to-landﬁll intensity by 50% by 2030 compared to 
its 2015 baseline, achieving a 42% reduction in 
2025. With the introduction of the Beverage 
Container Return Scheme by the National 
Environment Agency, Genting Singapore will 
leverage upon this opportunity to enhance its 
recyclables collection and segregation efforts, 
further boosting its recycling rates and reducing 
waste-to-landﬁll intensities.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE (cont'd)
Waste Proﬁling 
 
Genting Singapore conducted a targeted waste proﬁling 
exercise in 2025 to identify waste disposal processes and 
opportunities for source reduction. In collaboration with the 
Waste Management and Recycling Association of Singapore, 
preliminary examinations were conducted on some high-
waste volume bin centres serving hotels, select F&B 
establishments and common areas. The exercise aims to 
identify upcycling potential and circular pathways for material 
recovery to close the waste loop via connecting with relevant 
industry partners.   
 
Upstream Food Waste Management  
 
Genting Singapore is working towards reducing food waste 
generated at its source. Building on its 2024 pilot at at Hotel 
Ora’s All Day Dining restaurant, LOMA, upstream food waste 
tracking expanded across additional F&B outlets in 2025. The 
food waste management software enables kitchen and 
stewarding teams to log waste by source, food type and 
service line, providing granular data on preparation waste, 
plate waste and buffet surplus. Analysis of this data 
throughout 2025 had provided insights to reduce food waste, 
particularly from buffet and preparation waste. However , 
given guest visitation rates ﬂuctuate with the seasons, the 
company requires more time and data to implement further 
targeted interventions, enabling more precise waste 
reduction strategies. 
Food Waste Processing 
 
Genting Singapore enhanced its onsite food waste treatment 
capacity by complementing the food waste grinder with a new 
onsite food waste digestor . Since the grinder’s installation, 
50% increase in volume of recycled food waste compared to 
2024 and this improvement is expected to grow further with 
the addition of the new digestor . 
 
The digestor converts food waste into treated greywater 
suitable for discharge into the sewerage system, reducing the 
volume of organic waste requiring off-site disposal. With a 
daily processing capacity of up to 2 tonnes, the system can 
manage higher waste volumes than the existing grinder , 
ensuring operational resilience while accommodating both 
current operational loads and future development 
requirements. To ensure effective food waste segregation and 
processing, targeted training sessions were conducted for 
internal teams and F&B tenants at WEAVE as part of their 
onboarding programmes. While signiﬁcant efforts have been 
made to implement food waste recycling infrastructure and 
segregation systems across the integrated resort, public area 
food waste segregation remains a key operational challenge. 
In addition to ongoing education for internal teams and 
tenants, Genting Singapore is actively evaluating behavioural 
nudges, smart infrastructure and guest-centric design 
solutions to improve segregation outcomes in shared spaces.
OUR FEATURE    RESORTS WORLD LAS VEGAS
Waste diversion rate was nearly  40%  in 2025
Waste management is central to resource conservation at 
Resorts World Las Vegas. The integrated resort adopts a 
comprehensive strategy – investing in both technologies and 
best practices – to minimise waste across the property. To 
achieve its waste reduction objectives, all materials generated 
onsite are meticulously sorted by trained personnel. This 
sorting ensures that recovered materials are accurately 
categorised into appropriate streams, such as recyclables and 
compostables. 
 
The resort collaborates with specialised recycling partners and 
waste management professionals to handle materials 
unsuitable for conventional disposal methods. 
 
Overall, the property processes and diverts over two dozen 
distinct material streams. Targeted initiatives focus on 
addressing high-impact waste categories, including single-use 
food scraps. Through a partnership with a local livestock 
farming operation, Resorts World Las Vegas diverts 100% of 
its recovered food scraps to be converted into animal feed, a 
preferred environmentally-friendly solution recognised by the 
US Environmental Protection Agency. The integrated resort is 
also collaborating with non-proﬁts and industry partners to 
explore innovative, data-driven solutions in its kitchens and 
restaurants. 
 
Through a collaborative initiative with a pioneer in the 
renewable and alternative fuel markets, the integrated resort 
transforms waste cooking oil from its kitchens and restaurants 
into biodiesel. Third-party certiﬁcation ensures that every 
gallon of biodiesel produced undergoes strict quality control 
analysis. This process not only reduces the carbon footprint 
but also promotes the use of renewable energy sources. 
 
Resorts World Las Vegas diverted nearly 40% of its waste 
stream in 2025. Without any large-scale renovations nor 
remodelling projects in the last calendar year – from which a 
higher percentage of the waste stream can be diverted – the 
integrated resort witnessed a slightly lower diversion rate 
year-over-year . In total, more than 2,800 tonnes were diverted 
from the landﬁll in 2025.

Genting Plantations prioritises effective waste management as part of its environmental stewardship, recognising that poor 
waste  handling can lead to pollution, higher operating costs, equipment damage product contamination and regulatory non-
compliance.  Genting Plantations has developed a holistic approach that embraces the principles of a circular economy. By 
adhering to applicable  regulations, our Plantations division ensures the safe and sustainable handling of various waste types, 
such as domestic, agricultural,  biomass and industrial by-products, addressing both environmental and social concerns.

WASTE, POLLUTION AND MATERIALS CONSUMPTION
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS (cont’d)
Wastewater 
 
Managing liquid by-products is essential to protecting environmental quality across Genting Plantations’ operations, with Palm 
Oil Mill Effluent and Palm Oil Reﬁnery Effluent treated through dedicated systems to minimise impacts on surrounding land 
and water .  
 
Genting Plantations has established a roadmap for phased installation of similar systems across all mills. The company is also 
working towards lowering Biochemical Oxygen Demand and Chemical Oxygen Demand levels by 2050 to improve water quality 
and further reduce emissions.   
 
To minimise environmental risks, stringent procedures and protocols are applied to ensure responsible management of all 
liquid by-products.
Scheduled Waste Management 
 
Scheduled waste management is a critical part of Genting Plantations’ environmental compliance practices. All scheduled 
waste is managed in accordance with regulatory requirements and its internal Scheduled Waste Standard Operating Procedure, 
which governs identiﬁcation, handling, storage and disposal. This includes the engagement of licensed contractors approved 
by the relevant authorities to ensure safe and compliant waste disposal. 
 
Scheduled waste is generated across estates, oil mills, downstream manufacturing facilities and the AgTech Division and is 
tracked to support ongoing compliance with environmental regulations. In 2025, total scheduled waste  disposed amounted 
to 625 metric tonnes. 
 
Going forward, the company will continue to strengthen the management of hazardous and non-hazardous waste across all 
operations, while improving the performance and efficiency of wastewater treatment systems. The company remains focused 
on meeting applicable effluent discharge standards and upholding consistent environmental compliance. 
 
Biomass By-Products, Production and Use 
 
Biomass utilisation remains a key pillar of Genting Plantations’ waste management strategy, contributing to both recycling 
and energy recovery across operations. By integrating biomass by-products into daily practices, the company reduces overall 
waste generation and its commitment to responsible resource management. 
 
In the context of oil palm cultivation and milling, biomass recycling offers a practical and sustainable avenue for optimising 
material use. Common by-products such as empty fruit bunches, palm kernel shell and ﬁbre are repurposed as mulch, soil 
enhancers or as biofuel for in-house energy generation. During the year under review, Genting Plantations recycled about 
1,133,335 metric tonnes of biomass.

across oil mills in Malaysia to 
mitigate GHG emissions.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 87
WASTE, POLLUTION AND MATERIALS CONSUMPTION
DRIVING ENVIRONMENTAL STEWARDSHIP
OUR FEATURE    GENTING ENERGY
Genting Energy maintains a robust waste management programme that ensures compliance with all relevant regulatory 
requirements while safeguarding human health and the environment. This programme is guided by key performance metrics 
that monitor waste generation and drive continuous improvement in environmental performance across the company’s 
operations.  
 
In 2025, Genting Energy generated 81,447 tonnes of industrial waste, primarily consisting of ﬂy ash, bottom ash, glass wool, 
used oils and expired chemicals, with the majority originating from the Banten power plant in Indonesia. The Chengdaoxi 
offshore oil facility in China generated 186 tonnes of waste, an increase from the 146 tonnes recorded in 2024. This increase 
was mainly attributed to a platform-wide repainting campaign, which involved sandblasting and recoating works as part of 
corrosion protection maintenance undertaken for the ﬁrst time since the facility’s commissioning.   
 
Genting Energy continues to strengthen its solid waste management practices by identifying major waste streams to reduce 
waste generation and maximise recycling. Hazardous waste is stored and managed by government-authorised vendors for 
safe disposal or repurposing, while non-hazardous waste is reused or recycled wherever possible. In 2025, Genting Energy 
diverted 71,988 tonnes of waste from direct disposal through its recycling efforts, reﬂecting continued progress in improving 
resource efficiency.  
 
The Banten power plant continues to demonstrate strong environmental performance, earning a “Blue” rating under 
Indonesia’s PROPER programme for the seventh consecutive year . Overseen by the Ministry of Environment and Forestry, the 
PROPER programme recognises consistent commitment for pollution control and effective environmental management 
practices.

WHY IT 
MATTERS
Genting Berhad acknowledges that customers are essential to the success of its leisure business, which remains the 
Group’s primary revenue driver . The Group’s resort properties attract millions of customers annually and are managed 
by Genting Malaysia, Genting Singapore and Resorts World Las Vegas, offering a comprehensive range of entertainment 
and hospitality service. By placing customer satisfaction at the forefront, the Group strengthens revenue streams, fosters 
brand loyalty, builds competitive advantage and supports sustainable growth. Continuous improvement of guest 
experience will strengthen the Group’s market leadership in the leisure industry and secure long-term success.
Genting Berhad upholds a strong commitment to customer health, safety and security through the Group’s stringent site 
safety measures, responsible gaming practices and compliance with all applicable safety regulations. 
 
Customer well-being remains a core element to the Group’s leisure strategies, which are implemented across the resort 
properties and facility attractions managed by Genting Malaysia, Genting Singapore and Resorts World Las Vegas. These 
strategies aim to enhance customer experience, promote responsible gaming, support sustainable procurement and drive 
positive economic impact.

THEME PARK SAFETY 
At the heart of its world-class entertainment offerings, safety is 
paramount. Every ride and attraction is built and maintained with the 
utmost care. All relevant machinery at its theme park has a valid 
Certiﬁcate of Fitness issued under the Occupational Safety and 
Health (Plant Requiring Certiﬁcate of Fitness) Regulation 2024. 
 
Key safety features at theme park include: 
• compliance with Department of Occupational Safety and Health 
("DOSH") guidelines on the safety management of amusement 
park devices and the mandatory Certiﬁcate of Fitness for relevant 
rides; 
• regular inspection by TUV Nord to ensure adherence to 
international standards such as EN 13814 and ASTM F2291; 
• 730 CCTV cameras provide constant surveillance for added peace 
of mind; and 
• preventive maintenance based on the manufacturer’s 
recommendations ensures safety for all riders.
FOOD SAFETY 
Genting Malaysia’s food safety measures 
include: 
• Hazard Analysis and Critical Control Point 
(“HACCP”)  Food  Safety  Management 
System  implemented  across  all  food 
outlets; 
• ISO 9001 and ISO 22000 certiﬁcations for 
its central kitchen and food and beverage 
outlets; and 
• all food outlets and restaurants are subject 
to monthly food safety inspections. 
 
Genting Malaysia achieved a 100% 
compliance rate in 2025, across all food 
outlets and restaurants through these 
monthly inspections.
FIRE SAFETY  
Genting Malaysia ﬁre safety strategy includes: 
• adhering to the Fire Services Act 1988 to meet all ﬁre safety standards; 
• enforcing a No Smoking Policy, with designated areas to minimise risks; and 
• complying with Arahan Pentadbiran BAB 8 to protect both people and property. 
 
2,100 cases attended and 3,762 employees attended ﬁre training in 2025.
OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont'd)
ROAD SAFETY 
Genting Malaysia’s commitment to road safety is grounded in 
national regulations and global best practices. Key measures include: 
• adhering to the Malaysian Road Transport Act 1987 and other local 
traffic regulations; 
• conducting independent Road Safety Audits by experts to ensure 
continuous improvement; and 
• performing regular safety audits in line with the Public Works 
Department and Malaysian Institute of Road Safety Research 
("MIROS") Guidebook. 
 
Implementing  ongoing  road  improvement  projects  aimed  at 
reducing risks for all road users. In 2025, Genting Malaysia carried 
out  comprehensive road infrastructure initiatives, including road 
furniture maintenance, road resurfacing, monthly drone inspections 
and ground improvement works within our resort and surrounding 
areas across our Malaysian operations. These proactive measures 
contributed to a lower number of accidents per 10,000 vehicles of 
0.32 in 2025, compared to 0.39 in 2024.
SLOPE SAFETY 
Genting Malaysia implemented key initiatives 
to safeguard the slopes and roads across 
Genting Highlands, including:  
• slope hazard and risk assessment; 
• rainfall monitoring; and 
• introduction of the Slope Stability 
Management Programme for long-term 
safety. 
 
Collaboration with MIROS reﬂects continuous 
improvement of the  Genting Highlands 
Access Road by Genting Malaysia.
A HOLISTIC APPROACH TO SAFETY 
 
Genting Malaysia prioritises the well-being of its guests, employees and visitors by adopting a proactive and thorough approach 
to safety across all areas of its business.

CUSTOMER HEALTH, SAFETY AND SECURITY
SAFEGUARDING COMMUNITY WELFARE
OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE
Attractions Safety and Accessibility 
 
Attractions safety forms a core pillar of Genting Singapore's 
commitment to sustainable and resilient operations. All rides 
across Universal Studios Singapore and Adventure Cove 
Waterpark undergo stringent safety assurance measures, 
including stress tests, system diagnostics and comprehensive 
risk assessments before opening to the public. Team 
members also undergo structured training in emergency 
response, ride operations and guest management, 
reinforcing a culture of care and operational excellence. 
 
Safety Design at Universal Studios Singapore 
 
Every new attraction, including recent additions such as 
Illumination's Minion Land is designed to meet General 
Performance Speciﬁcations (“GPS”). GPS incorporates all 
ASTM International amusement ride safety requirements and 
enhances them with additional criteria in key areas such as 
system redundancy, long term reliability, predictive 
diagnostics and comprehensive acceptance testing. These 
measures extend beyond ASTM's baseline framework, 
supporting its commitment to attractions that are not only 
safe but engineered for operational robustness and 
consistently high-quality guest experiences. 
 
Accessibility at Universal Studio Singapore 
 
Accessibility remains a key priority at Universal Studios 
Singapore, where the park is designed to welcome guests of 
all disabilities. Wheelchair friendly access routes and 
dedicated ride-in-wheelchair experiences such as Despicable 
Me Minion Mayhem, Shrek 4D Adventure and WaterWorld 
ensure that guests with mobility needs can enjoy the full 
range of attractions. Guests with physical disabilities may also 
enjoy the attractions, provided they meet the speciﬁc rider 
requirements applicable to each ride. To further support 
guests with physical, medical, or cognitive conditions, the 
park offers the Guest Assistance Pass (“GAP”), an accessibility 
support system that provides prioritised attraction boarding 
for the guest and one accompanying caregiver . This allows 
them to wait in a more comfortable environment instead of 
remaining in the physical queue. Assessed upon veriﬁcation 
of supporting documents, GAP is issued by the Guest Services 
team based on individual needs. GAP enhances inclusivity 
and ensures a safe, digniﬁed and enjoyable experience for all 
visitors. 
Heat Stress Management 
 
Safety at its parks extends beyond attractions to holistic guest  
wellbeing and climate resilience. With rising temperatures,  
both parks adopt enhanced heat-stress risk management  
aligned with national guidelines, including continuous Wet-
Bulb Globe Temperature monitoring, dynamic roster  
adjustments for outdoor crew and proactive guest cooling  
measures. Material choices such as ETFE rooﬁng in  Universal 
Studios Singapore reduce surface temperatures  and energy 
consumption while improving visitor comfort. 
 
Food Safety 
 
Genting Singapore’s Quality Assurance team conducts 
monthly inspections to ensure compliance with food safety 
and hygiene procedures, while its Food Safety Policy is 
regularly reviewed and updated to remain relevant.  
 
Additionally, the company performs random external 
laboratory testing of food samples to meet stringent 
microbiological standards. These rigorous measures ensure 
the resort deliver safe, exceptional and unforgettable dining 
experiences to every guest. 
 
Genting Singapore operates under a Food Safety  
Management System designed with reference to 
internationally recognised standards such as ISO 22000:2018 
and HACCP . It safeguards the highest levels of food safety and 
quality across all operations. 
 
All F&B team members undergo the Food Safety Level 1 
Course conducted in-house. Every food handler is thoroughly 
trained and certiﬁed to uphold strict hygiene standards 
across all premises.  
 
Automated monitoring devices have been installed in walk-
in chillers and freezers to track temperatures and trigger 
immediate alerts to chefs and maintenance teams whenever 
deviations occur , ensuring optimal storage conditions and 
protecting ingredient freshness. 
 
The digital I-Auditor system was transitioned for all F&B 
outlet inspections in 2025 to reduce paper waste, streamline 
processes and strengthen accountability through a 
centralised platform for tracking corrective actions, 
supporting operational excellence and the Group’s 
sustainability goals.

RESPONSIBLE GAMING 
 
At Genting Berhad, we acknowledge the potential negative 
impacts of gambling on individuals and communities. As a 
leader in the gaming and hospitality industry, we recognise 
our duty to minimise the negative impact on gambling and 
promote ethical, sustainable business practices. 
 
We are dedicated to implementing comprehensive 
responsible gambling measures across all our gaming 
operations worldwide. We prioritise customer well-being by 
fostering a safe and responsible gaming environment. 
 
Our leisure-based companies, namely Genting Malaysia, 
Genting Singapore and Resorts World Las Vegas operate 
under strict regulatory frameworks and implement 
comprehensive responsible gaming programmes to minimise 
the risks associated with gambling. Our casinos and 
integrated resorts provide access to resources that help 
customers make informed decisions about their gaming 
activities. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Our Group’s responsible gaming measures include: 
• compliance with regulations : adhering to all applicable 
responsible gaming laws and regulations of the 
jurisdictions where they operate; 
• responsible marketing: committing to ethical marketing 
and advertising, aiming to prevent children and other 
vulnerable people from experiencing harm or exploitation 
caused by gambling; 
• employee training: providing comprehensive training 
aligned with industry best practices to employees, arming 
them with knowledge and tools to effectively promote 
responsible gaming; 
• self-exclusion programme: offering customers the option 
to voluntarily exclude themselves from gaming activities 
through a structured self-exclusion programme; 
• collaborative partnerships:  partnering with local 
regulatory councils and non - governmental organisations 
to improve gambling safety efforts. For example, Resorts 
World Bimini’s collaboration with Florida Council on 
Compulsive Gambling and Genting Singapore’s partnership 
with National Addictions Management Service; and 
• support resources:  establishing a Responsible Gaming 
Resource Centre at Resorts World Catskills to help patrons 
and employees with gambling problems.
Genting Malaysia strictly complies with all applicable laws and regulations. Its active collaboration with government agencies, 
responsible gambling organisations and the wider community helps mitigate the social impacts of gambling. Engagement in 
industry forums and partnerships, including collaboration with the Malaysia Gambling Rehab Centre, promotes responsible 
gaming practices and drives collective progress. 
 
ADDRESSING SALIENT HUMAN RIGHTS ISSUES IN RESPONSIBLE GAMBLING 
 
Human rights considerations in the gambling industry encompass a wide range of issues, including protecting vulnerable 
individuals and ensuring fair gaming practices. Genting Malaysia recognises the critical importance of addressing these matters 
to uphold a responsible and equitable gaming environment. 
 
The company actively engages with regulatory authorities, industry partners and advocacy organisations to address these 
concerns. Through this collaborative approach, Genting Malaysia implements robust measures that promote fairness, 
transparency and accountability across all gaming operations, reinforcing its commitment to responsible gaming and ethical 
business practices.
Resorts World Las Vegas complies with the American Gaming Association Code of Conduct for Responsible Gaming and has 
pledged to its team members, patrons and the community to make responsible gambling an integral part of its daily operations.  
 
• Team members at the resort are trained on responsible gaming during new hire orientation and periodic refresher training. 
• The resort will make reasonable efforts to honour a patron’s written request to be self-limited from gambling activities and 
reserves the right to exclude a patron from gambling. 
• The resort will make diligent efforts to prevent individuals under 21 years of age from participating in any gambling or 
sports betting, loitering in the gambling area of the casino, or from gaining access to online, mobile or in-room gambling 
opportunities.

OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont’d)
Resorts World Genting continues to uphold the Malaysian Gaming Industry Responsible Code of Conduct, working closely 
with its Corporate, Legal and Compliance departments. Partnerships with the Malaysian Mental Health Association 
("MMHA") and The Mind strengthen joint education campaigns focused on problem gambling prevention and awareness.  
 
Resorts World Genting has established a Responsible Gaming (“RG”) Committee comprising senior management 
representatives. The Committee meets monthly to review and enhance the RG framework, policies and strategies, 
ensuring alignment with industry standards and continuous improvement. 
 
Its RG Policy prioritises proactive education, provides intervention support for at-risk individuals and ensures a safe and 
transparent gaming environment. In 2025, the Responsible Gaming Policy and procedures were reviewed and enhanced 
under the guidance of the ESG Committee. 
 
Key updates included: 
 
• enhanced screening procedures to identify potential high-risk casino guests, including ex-employees; 
• improved online self-exclusion application process; 
• strengthened communication protocols between Casino Operations, Marketing and RG Working Teams, coordinated 
through monthly RG Committee meetings; and 
• integration of #PlayResponsibly hashtags and QR code tools across Trend-D visuals and RG promotional materials.
Malaysia
Human Rights Issues in the Context of Gambling
Problem Gambling and 
Addiction
• Provide access to adequate support and resources for individuals struggling with 
problem gambling or addiction. 
• Implement measures to prevent and address the negative impact of gambling on 
mental health and well-being. 
Underage Gambling • Enforce strict measures to prevent vulnerable groups including minors from 
participating in gambling activities. 
• Safeguard the rights and well-being of underage individuals. 
Customer Protection and Fair 
Play
• Ensure fair and transparent practices in the gambling industry to protect consumers 
from fraud or exploitation. 
• Treat individuals equitably and provide accurate information on the odds and risks 
associated with gambling. 
Privacy and Data Protection • Safeguard individuals’ privacy by implementing robust data protection measures. 
• Secure all sensitive information collected during gambling activities. 
Access to Responsible Gaming 
Resources
• Provide easy access to responsible gambling resources and information. 
• Empower individuals to make informed choices about their gambling activities. 
Cultural Sensitivity • Respect cultural diversity by avoiding practices that may offend or disrespect cultural 
or religious beliefs regarding gambling.
In Malaysia, these human rights considerations are managed through a structured framework aligned with local regulatory 
requirements, supported by deﬁned policies, training programmes and performance targets to monitor effectiveness and 
drive continuous improvement. Across the Genting Malaysia’s overseas operations, responsible gaming and human rights 
considerations are addressed through initiatives tailored to local regulatory contexts and operating environments, supporting 
the consistent application of Genting Malaysia’s responsible gaming principles.
