PREVENTION & AWARENESS
  RG TRAINING 
• Employee Education: New hires receive RG Awareness 
Training to ensure they understand responsible 
gaming practices. 
• Annual Refresher Courses: These courses reinforce 
employees’ knowledge and keep them up to date on RG 
policies. 
• RG Specialists: Dedicated RG specialists are available to 
assist guests in distress, providing timely support. 
• Training Coverage: In 2025, RG training coverage 
among casino-related employees reached 100%, 
against a target of 100%. 
• Responsible Gaming Ambassador Certiﬁcation: The 
MMHA conducted a Masterclass, further strengthening 
internal RG expertise.
ADVERTISING & PROMOTIONS 
• Responsible Marketing and Advertising: We take proactive 
actions to ensure promotions are not directed at vulnerable 
groups, mitigating the potential negative impact of 
gambling. 
• Self-Exclusion Programme: Guests in the Self-Exclusion 
Programme do not receive promotional materials, 
advertisements or offers. 
• Post-Self-Exclusion Advertising: Guests returning from the 
Self-Exclusion Programme do not receive advertisements 
for 3 months to ensure a smooth transition back. 
• Cultural Sensitivity: Our advertising and promotional 
guidelines respect cultural sensitivities and promote 
responsible gambling.
HEALTHY GAMING HABITS
  PASSAGE OF TIME 
• Regular reminders encourage guests to take breaks during play. 
• EGM screens and time displays help guests stay aware of gaming session durations.

Malaysia
United Kingdom 
 
Genting UK is committed to responsible gaming, ensuring that all advertising, marketing and promotional materials fully 
comply with applicable regulations, including from UK Gambling Commission and under the UK Consumer Rights Act. All 
materials are reviewed and approved by the Compliance Team prior to release. 
 
Employee training remains central to its approach, with all staff completing safer gambling training annually. Automated 
alerts help maintain training compliance, achieving near-full completion across the business, except for employees on long-
term leave. 
 
Genting UK also provides a dedicated Safer Gambling webpage, offering tools and resources to help players manage their 
gaming responsibly. This includes: 
• Self-assessment - Allows players to evaluate their gaming habits; 
• Time-Outs -  Temporarily restrict access to all Genting sites for a chosen period. Accounts cannot be reactivated until 
the Time-Out period ends; and 
• SENSE National Self-Exclusion -  A free service that enables individuals to self-exclude from all land-based casinos 
licensed in Great Britain for a minimum of six months. 
 
Genting UK supported Safer Gambling Week and actively engaged with industry bodies and initiatives, reinforcing its 
commitment to player protection and promoting responsible gaming practices in 2025.

Genting Malaysia’s New York operations ensure guest protection and strictly adhere to its Responsible Gaming Plan,

Resorts World New York City is an active member of the American Gaming Association’s Responsible Gambling 
Committee, contributing to initiatives that support and implement responsible gambling codes and guidelines. 
 
To achieve its target of 100% participation in problem gambling training, Resorts World New York City is transitioning its 
training to online training to enhance accessibility and completion rates across all properties. 
 
Genting Malaysia has reaffirmed its commitment to responsible gaming by participating as a Community Partner in the 
2025 “Too Young to Bet” Campaign, a new initiative under the annual Gift Responsibly Campaign to raise awareness of 
the risks associated with underage gambling. The campaign is organised by the National Council on Problem Gambling 
and supported by the North American Association of State and Provincial Lotteries, reﬂecting Resorts World’s ongoing 
dedication to responsible and socially conscious gaming practices. 
 
Through this initiative, Genting Malaysia supports awareness-raising on the risks of underage gambling, including 
educational messaging, parental awareness guidance and youth gambling statistics disseminated through digital and 
community-facing channels. Campaign activities included promoting responsible gaming messages, engaging families 
and community members on youth protection and participating in post-campaign evaluation surveys. These efforts 
reinforce Genting Malaysia’s commitment to responsible gaming, community education and collaboration with recognised 
organisations to help prevent gambling-related harm.
Bahamas

efforts by participating in Problem Gambling Awareness Month (March 2025) and Responsible Gaming Education Month 
(September 2025). During these months, Resorts World Bimini pushed responsible gaming messages through pop-ups 
on the casino webpage and on-machine reminders, such as “Play Smart from the Start” and “Not 18 yet? No Bet.” 
 
Resorts World Bimini supports guests and team members through its partnership with the Florida Council on Compulsive 
Gambling, offering conﬁdential helpline services, text and chat services and referrals to treatment when necessary. 
Online responsible gaming training raises employee awareness.

1. Education & Awareness – Deliver ongoing RG education to promote safer gambling attitudes and informed decision-

OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE (Cont’d)
Accreditation by RG Check 
 
Resorts World Sentosa is proud that its RG Programme has 
achieved and maintained the highest worldwide RG Check 
accreditation score for ten consecutive years (2015 to 2025), 
demonstrating Resorts World Sentosa’s leadership in RG 
policy, management and programmes. 
 
RG Check, developed by the Responsible Gambling Council, 
is the most comprehensive accreditation programme 
globally. Its RG Programme meets 47 criteria across eight 
standards, including policies, training and player protection 
measures. Annual reviews ensure continuous compliance and 
improvement. 
 
Governance and Oversight 
 
The governance framework ensures accountability at every 
level. Resorts World Sentosa’s RG Committee, comprising 
senior management, reports to the Board through the 
Compliance Committee. 
 
This collaborative approach ensures that the RG Programme 
remains robust, adaptable, effective and aligned with 
evolving standards while maintaining the highest 
performance and governance standards. 
 
RG Outreach Efforts 
 
In support of the annual National RG Awareness Week, 
Resorts World Sentosa partnered with the Ministry of Social 
and Family Development to host four roadshows in 2025 
within its casino. 
 
Beyond the national initiative, Resorts World Sentosa 
organised an additional four roadshows throughout the year 
to further promote RG awareness among patrons. These 
events engaged a total of 1,928 patrons, with 598 patrons 
enrolling in the Manage Game Play (“MGP”) Programme 
during the sessions. 
 
Resorts World Sentosa remains committed to proactively 
promoting the MGP Programme, which empowers patrons 
to make informed decisions about their gambling habits and 
adopt healthy gambling practices. To date, the programme 
has achieved over 3,400 enrolments.
Manage Game Play Programme 
 
The MGP Programme empowers patrons to make informed 
gambling decisions by providing real-time information on 
their gambling activity across all Electronic Gaming Machines. 
 
Key Features: 
• Personalised Limits: Patrons can set preferred time and/or 
spend limits for their gambling sessions. 
• Real-Time Notiﬁcations: SMS alerts are sent at selected 
intervals to help patrons stay within their self-determined 
limits. 
• RG Ambassador Support: Patrons may opt for a “shoulder 
tap” reminder from a trained RG Ambassador if they 
exceed 200% of their set limit. RG Ambassadors provide 
discreet reminders and offer assistance or RG resources 
when needed. 
 
Over 3,400 patrons have enrolled in the MGP Programme and 
more than 95% remain active participants, demonstrating 
strong engagement. Surveys indicate that patrons ﬁnd the 
programme easy to use and effective in promoting breaks or 
cessation of play upon receiving notiﬁcations. 
 
This programme reﬂects our commitment to player 
protection and RG practices, ensuring that gambling remains 
a safe and enjoyable experience. 
 
Responsible Gambling Ambassador ("RGA")  
 
The RGA served as a vital resource for player education, 
outreach and support. RGAs are specially trained to: 
• assist patrons with information and guidance on RG 
practices; 
• promote awareness of RG tools and programmes available 
at Resorts World Sentosa; 
• provide practical tips for safe gambling and encourage 
informed decision-making; and 
• offer access to player safeguard options, including self-
exclusion and MGP Programme enrolment. 
 
Patrons can easily identify RGAs by their official badges and 
approach them for  assistance  at any  time. As of 2025, 
Resorts World Sentosa has more than 800 RGAs reinforcing 
its commitment to maintaining a safe and supportive 
gambling environment.

CUSTOMER HEALTH, SAFETY AND SECURITY
SAFEGUARDING COMMUNITY WELFARE
OUR FEATURE    GENTING SINGAPORE (cont’d)
Responsible Advertising 
 
Resorts World Sentosa is committed to socially responsible 
advertising and promotions. All casino advertisements and 
promotions undergo a screening process to ensure that they 
adhere to Resorts World Sentosa’s responsible advertising 
policy. Resorts World Sentosa maintains a stringent 
compliance framework to ensure that all marketing 
materials:  
• Do not mislead or contain any expressed or implied 
inducement to gamble. 
• Avoid targeting vulnerable groups, including: 
o Singapore Citizens and Permanent Residents who are 
non-premium players and hold day-entry levies. 
o Individuals with gambling problems or under casino 
exclusion orders. 
o Minors. 
 
To further support informed decision-making, every new 
member receives an RG kit upon casino membership 
enrolment. The kit includes: 
• information on RG programmes; 
• educational materials on signs of problem gambling; and 
• details of help services and support channels available. 
 
This approach reinforces its commitment to ethical marketing 
practices, player protection and community well-being, 
aligning with its broader sustainability objectives. 
 
Player Education and Safeguards 
 
Resorts World Sentosa provides comprehensive resources 
throughout the casino to maximise player reach: 
• RG Brochures – Brochures in multiple languages on 
gambling risks and safeguards. 
• Self-Service Membership Kiosks – Members can check 
gambling activities and access RG resources easily. 
• Self-Exclusion Programme – Resorts World Sentosa has an 
in-house self-exclusion programme offering variable ban 
length options. 
• Access to Money – No ATMs in the casino and restriction 
on credit card transactions for Singapore Citizens and 
Permanent Residents. 
• Venue and Game Features – Ensure gambling activities are 
not visible from outside the casino, keeping them 
concealed from families and those under 21 years old 
visiting the resort. 
• RG features are integrated in electric gaming machines, 
time displays and cool-off rooms. 
 
Casino Entry Checks 
 
Resorts World Sentosa’s casino entry system is designed to 
uphold strict compliance and RG standards by ensuring that 
only eligible patrons gain access. All individuals entering the 
casino must present a valid government-issued photo 
identiﬁcation for veriﬁcation. 
 
To strengthen accuracy and prevent unauthorised entry, 
advanced technologies such as Optical Character Recognition 
and Facial Recognition are deployed. These systems perform 
accurate identity veriﬁcation and impersonation checks, 
serving as an effective deterrent against attempts to use 
another person’s identiﬁcation documents to gain entry. 
 
This comprehensive screening process prevents entry by 
minors, excluded individuals and patrons subjected to visit 
limits, reinforcing its commitment to a safe and responsible 
gambling environment. 
 
RG Training 
 
Resorts World Sentosa’s RG training curriculum is regularly 
reviewed and endorsed by the RG Independent Advisory 
Panel to ensure its relevance and effectiveness. All team 
members receive RG training and are required to read and 
acknowledge the company’s policy, which outlines its RG 
objectives. 
 
Casino employees must complete and pass the RG 
Awareness training before undertaking any casino-related 
duties. They are also required to attend annual refresher 
training, which is tracked and reported to senior 
management. 
 
Employees appointed as RGA undergo additional specialised 
training conducted by senior counsellors from the National 
Addictions Management Service and enhanced programmes 
organised by the Ministry of Social and Family Development. 
This advanced training equips RGA with the skills to identify, 
respond to and assist patrons exhibiting signs of distress 
potentially linked to problem gambling. RGA are also 
required to complete annual refresher training to maintain 
competency.

WHY IT 
MATTERS
As an investment holding company whose portfolio companies have a wide geographical footprint in multiple industries, 
Genting Berhad recognises that its investments have far-reaching impacts on a broad range of stakeholders. This 
understanding guides the Group’s approach to managing social responsibilities across the operations. 
 
Safeguarding the rights, interests and well-being of local communities enables the Group to build trust and foster long-
term relationships, thereby securing its licence to operate, which is essential for sustainable growth. The Group’s 
ongoing community investments further reaffirm its commitment to being a responsible corporate citizen. 
Genting Berhad’s Sustainability Policy sets out the rationale for , and approach to, the Group’s 
investments in communities. The Group is committed to contributing positively to societal 
development through structured philanthropic and CSR programmes. Its community 
investments focus on health, education, environmental stewardship and community outreach, 
and are aligned with its business strategy to foster meaningful connections that create shared 
value for the communities it serves, while supporting long-term business sustainability. 
 
Employee participation is encouraged through structured engagement mechanisms, including 
deﬁned programmes, partnerships and collaborations with charitable organisations that 
promote active involvement. These group-wide commitments are further supported by speciﬁc 
targets established by the operating teams of the Group’s portfolio companies. 
 
The Group continued to undertake CSR activities that have a positive impact on the lives of 
local communities, some of which are outlined below.
OUR APPROACH
Genting Dementia Care  
Genting Berhad established the Genting Dementia Centre in 
Kuala Lumpur to provide daycare services for individuals 
living with dementia. In addition to providing direct care, the 
centre serves as a knowledge-sharing platform, offering 
webinars and training programmes for caregivers, family 
members and professionals involved in dementia care. 
 
The centre is a collaboration between the Genting Group and 
the University of Malaya’s Faculty of Medicine. The Group 
supports the care centre’s daily operations through the 
contribution of staff and volunteers. 
 
University of Malaya – Tan Sri Lim Goh Tong 
Endowment Fund 
 
 In 2009, Genting Berhad established the University of Malaya 
– Tan Sri Lim Goh Tong Endowment Fund with a capital of 
RM1 million. The endowment fund generates dividends to 
sustainably ﬁnance scholarships, international exchange 
programmes, study trips and other educational programmes 
for student from the university’s Faculty of Business and  
Economics. 
 
Genting Charity Bazaar  
The Genting Charity Bazaar , held in June 2025, brought 
together  more  than  350  employees  and  affiliates  who 
managed  stalls,  handled  logistics  and  supported  sales 
activities throughout the event. The bazaar raised total  net 
proceeds of over RM400,000, which were donated to support 
various charitable organisations and communities in need. 
 
 
Weekend Food Kitchen Initiative  
The Weekend Food Kitchen initiative supported food-insecure 
communities by providing freshly prepared meals while 
reducing avoidable food waste. Employees of the Genting 
Group volunteered on allocated Saturdays in 2025 to assist 
with meal preparation and distribution. The Group helped to 
extend the programme’s reach and ensure consistent 
support for underserved individuals.

Climate Action 
 
Genting Singapore advances environmental sustainability 
and stewardship through the Ocean Advocates programme, 
a volunteer-driven initiative led by its Conservation and 
Science team. By uniting and mobilising individuals 
passionate about ocean protection, the programme drives 
coastal and underwater clean-ups in Singapore. Data 
collected during each cleanup is compiled and submitted to 
global monitoring databases managed by Ocean 
Conservancy and PADI's Dive Against Debris, contributing to 
broader efforts to understand and mitigate marine pollution.  
 
In 2025, Singapore Oceanarium's Ocean Advocates 
programme conducted six clean-ups in partnership with 
seven organisations, including Friends of Marine Park, Youth 
Ocean Advocates, KAIA Initiative and National University of 
Singapore Society. These efforts mobilised over 180 
volunteers who collectively removed 846 kg of marine debris 
from local waters. Activities included two dive clean-ups at 
Seringat Kias and Lazarus Island and four coastal clean-ups 
at Marina East Breakwater , Punggol Coast, Coney Island and 
Bendera Bay on St. John's Island.  
 
By connecting community action with scientiﬁc data and 
conservation outcomes, Ocean Advocates not only improves 
marine habitats but also inspires a growing community of 
individuals committed to protecting ocean health and 
advancing climate action. 
Supporting Local 
 
Genting Singapore recognises local organisations as essential 
partners in building vibrant, resilient communities and 
driving sustainable economic growth. By strategically 
investing in local businesses, social enterprises and 
community organisations, the company catalyses economic 
opportunity while strengthening the social fabric that 
underpins long-term prosperity.  
 
Through sustained collaboration, capacity building and 
market access, Genting Singapore enables these 
organisations to scale their impact, create meaningful 
employment and reinforce Singapore's vision for inclusive 
economic development.  
 
In 2025, Genting Singapore featured products from JOURNEY 
by TOUCH Community Services, representing the creative 
talents of persons with intellectual disabilities and ART:DIS 
Studio, which transforms artworks by artists with disabilities 
into quality lifestyle products. These partnerships supported 
crafters and generated over SGD6,500 in sales while 
providing guests with meaningful, locally crafted souvenirs. 
 
Beyond individual collaborations, Genting Singapore invests 
in Singapore's artistic future by funding emerging arts groups 
and partnering with cultural institutions, creating platforms 
through which local creativity inspires meaningful dialogue 
and enriches the nation's cultural landscape.

COMMUNITY INVESTMENTS
SAFEGUARDING COMMUNITY WELFARE
Resorts World Las Vegas continues to focus on areas that 
were determined by its team members that are important to 
them as well. These overarching categories that were 
selected by its team members are: Women, Children, 
Animals, Education, Military, Seniors, Homelessness and 
Health. The team works with organisations that ﬁt into one 
or more of these categories as they are universal and cover a 
wide range of charities. 
 
Focused on what Matters in our Community 
 
Some of the organisations that Resorts World Las Vegas 
partners with are the Las Vegas Metropolitan Police 
Department, Crestwood Elementary School, American Red 
Cross, Just One Project, Catholic Charities, Nevada Society for 
Prevention to Cruelty to Animals, Women, Infants and 
Children, US Vets and Opportunity Village and many more. 
 
Serving Those Who Serve 
 
Las Vegas Metropolitan Police Department: Resorts World Las 
Vegas hosts the Good Ticket Programme, through which the 
company organises 12 events annually on its property to 
recognise and honour the unsung heroes of the Metro Police 
Force. Recipients are presented with certiﬁcates for rooms, 
food and beverage, and show tickets as a token of 
appreciation from Resorts World Las Vegas and the 
community. The company also hosts an annual Trunk or Treat 
event in October , during which the team decorates and 
distributes candy in a safe space for children in the 
community.  
 
Educational Support 
 
Resorts World Las Vegas continues its partnership for a third 
year with Crestwood Elementary School, a Title I school 
where most students are in need of assistance and come 
from low-income families. The company has remodelled the 
teachers’ lounge and enhanced the outdoor garden learning 
space over the past two years and is currently working on 
updating the school library. 
 
Each year , the company also hosts a Teacher Appreciation 
event to recognise all faculty and staff, a Halloween event to 
provide goodie bags for all 600 students and a back-to-school 
drive to collect items for faculty to share with students, as 
well as book, holiday toy, and food drives. 25 gift baskets and 
toys were provided in 2025 to children who attended the 
school. 
 
Giving in Many Ways 
 
Red Cross Blood Drive: Resorts World Las Vegas hosts four 
events each year to collect blood from team members and 
guests, helping to support those in need within the local 
community. To date the company has hosted 515 donors, 
collected 467 units of blood, including 139 new donors in 
2025. 
 
Las Vegas Urban League - Women, Infants and Children: 
Resorts World Las Vegas partners with this organisation each 
year to provide toys to children as part of an adopt a family 
programme. In 2025, the company collected, provided and 
distributed to 30 families in need through the generous 
contributions of its team members. 
 
Parades: Resorts World Las Vegas participated in four  
parades within the city in 2025, namely the Martin Luther 
King Jr . Day Parade, Helldorado Parade, PRIDE Parade and 
Veterans Parade. Resorts World Las Vegas hosted a ﬂoat and 
walkers in each parade and provided entertainment, music 
and handouts to those in the audience. 
 
Community with a Purpose 
 
Resorts World Las Vegas remains committed to supporting  
the community and the team members who live and work 
within it. The company continues to seek new volunteer 
opportunities, while representing the organisation in the 
community every day.

Genting Plantations corporate social responsibility activities covers education, healthcare, religious and social and sports in  
Malaysia and Indonesia. 
Driving Sustainable Practices  
Genting Plantations carries out awareness programmes to educate its workers, especially those working in the natural 
environment on sustainable practices. These programmes include managing human-wildlife conﬂicts, raising awareness on 
the urgency of preventing hunting and poaching of endangered wildlife as well as preventing forest ﬁres and avoiding the use 
of ﬁres to clear land, in line with its Zero Burning Policy. Genting Plantations also works with local farmers to adopt sustainable 
agricultural methods, which enhances economic and food security, while reducing negative efforts on the environment. 
Providing Essential Infrastructure  
Genting Plantations emphasises on developing and 
maintaining essential infrastructure, especially in locations 
where connectivity remains limited to improve living 
conditions for nearby communities and contributing to wider 
social and economic progress. 
 
In 2025, its work remained focused on the development of 
infrastructure such as roads, bridges and other key facilities, 
which have delivered positive outcomes across community 
engagement, business and stakeholder impacts. 
 
Empowering Local Communities  
Genting Plantations supports economic growth in local 
communities by creating job opportunities and sourcing 
materials and services from local entrepreneurs where 
possible. The majority of its workforce comprises individuals 
from the surrounding areas.
Educational Support  
Genting Plantations continues to support the education of 
employees’ children and surrounding communities through 
a range of initiatives aimed at improving access to quality 
education and contributing to the development of future 
generations. Key initiatives include: 
• Local schools – provision of educational support to over 
119 schools located within and around its plantation areas 
in Malaysia and Indonesia. Contributions include ﬁnancial 
assistance, upgrades to school facilities and the supply of 
learning materials, all aimed at enhancing the quality of 
education for children in these communities; 
• Sponsorships – provision of educational sponsorships 
through the Tan Sri (Dr .) Lim Goh Tong Endowment Fund, 
to support students’ access to higher education; and  
• Educational facilities – establishment and management of 
schools and crèches within its plantation areas to provide 
accessible education and childcare that support early 
childhood development and ease the responsibilities of 
working parents.

HUMAN RIGHTS AND LABOUR STANDARDS
ENHANCING WORKPLACE PRACTICES
WHY IT 
MATTERS
Genting Berhad upholds human rights as a core tenet throughout its operations, treating all individuals with dignity, 
respect and equality regardless of background. This conviction drives Genting Berhad’s efforts to build an inclusive 
work environment that empowers employees to reach their full potential professionally and personally. 
 
Genting Berhad builds resilience and future readiness by addressing human rights issues and adhering to robust labour 
standards, fulﬁlling ethical obligations as a responsible corporate citizen. This commitment to fair practices and 
investment in people creates an environment where everyone feels valued, cultivating a positive workplace culture that 
enables long-term success and shared prosperity. 
Genting Berhad’s approach to human rights is guided by its 
core values and dedication to complying with local and 
international laws. The Company protects employee rights by 
ensuring safe and healthy working conditions, fair treatment 
and living wages for all employees. 
 
Genting Berhad’s commitment to upholding labour rights 
encompasses the following: 
Genting Berhad's global operations are governed by a 
comprehensive set of local and international regulations. At 
the local level, Genting Berhad is governed by the Federal 
Constitution, Employment Act 1955,  Anti-Trafficking  in 
Persons and Anti-Smuggling of Migrants Act 2007 and 
Occupational Safety and Health Act 1994. 
 
At the international level, the Genting Berhad’s compliance 
framework is aligned with the International Bill of Human 
Rights, International Labour Organisation (“ILO”) Declaration 
on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work, ILO 
Conventions: Indigenous & Tribal Peoples (no. 169), United 
Nations (“UN”) Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous 
Peoples and the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and 
Development Guidelines. 
Furthermore,  Genting  Berhad  adheres  to  labour laws 
preventing child and forced labour through the Basic 
Conditions of Employment Act, Labour Relations Act, Skills 
Development Act and Occupational Safety and Health Act. 
 
The governance oversight is provided at Board level, led by the 
Executive Chairman, with clear management accountability at 
the Company’s Executive Committee, led by the Chief Executive.  
 
The day-to-day implementation and monitoring responsibilities 
are integrated across key functions, with clear functional 
ownership and oversight. These include Human Resources 
(workforce rights, fair wages, non-discrimination, recruitment 
practices and grievance handling); Procurement and Supply 
Chain through heads of departments (supplier due diligence, 
labour standards compliance and corrective action plans); Risk 
Management (risk assessment and monitoring) and 
Sustainability (coordination and reporting disclosures). 
 
Genting Berhad’s expectations are set out in its Human Rights 
Policy and Code of Ethics, which are publicly available on its 
corporate website. These expectations are communicated to, 
and apply to its employees, business partners and suppliers 
across the value chain. 
 
As part of its risk assessment processes, Genting Berhad 
conducts regular reviews and proactively assesses human 
rights impacts on an ongoing basis. Identiﬁed risks are 
managed through documented mitigation measures, 
stakeholder engagement and integration into business 
operations and decision-making processes.  
 
Accessible grievance and whistleblowing mechanisms are 
available to both internal and external stakeholders, with 
safeguards to ensure conﬁdentiality, anonymity and protection 
against retaliation. These mechanisms support the transparent 
identiﬁcation, escalation and remediation of potential 
concerns.

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025110
HUMAN RIGHTS AND LABOUR STANDARDS
ENHANCING WORKPLACE PRACTICES
Genting Malaysia upholds human rights across all operations by aligning its practices with local and international standards, 
including the International Bill of Human Rights and the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights. 
 
The company embeds dignity, respect, non-discrimination and fair treatment into contracts, operations and third-party 
engagements, ensuring a safe and inclusive environment for employees, guests, partners and communities. 
 
The company integrates human rights protections throughout the value chain. The company clearly communicates its 
expectations to all stakeholders, including suppliers and contractors, who must meet the same standards as the company. 
These conditions include the requirement to establish internal grievance mechanisms with anonymous reporting channels, to 
address workplace concerns. 
Number of substantiated complaints concerning human rights violations
Genting Berhad 2023 2024 2025
Company Level 0 0 0
Group Level 0 0 0
OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA
ACCESS TO REMEDY
Genting Malaysia operates a formal, conﬁdential grievance mechanism accessible to individuals and communities. This 
system: 
• enables concerns to be raised safely; 
• provides a structured process for investigation and resolution; and 
• ensures appropriate remedies for any adverse human rights impacts linked to its operations. 
INCLUSIVE BEHAVIOUR AND TRAINING
Genting Malaysia actively promotes inclusive behaviour through training and policy enforcement: 
• Mandatory training programmes supporting diversity and inclusion; 
• Genting UK mandatory eLearning on Inclusive Leadership and Equity, Diversity and Inclusion; and 
• Genting Malaysia policies, including the Equal Opportunities Policy, Respect at Work Charter and Anti-Bullying and 
Harassment Policy, which deﬁne expected conduct and support a respectful workplace.
Genting Berhad has implemented measures to identify, assess and manage labour-related risks across its operations and 
supply chain, underpinned by a zero-tolerance approach to child labour , forced labour and human trafficking. The 
implementation is supported through strict recruitment age and identity veriﬁcation procedures, compliance with applicable 
labour laws, and the application of ethical employment and supplier standards across the workforce and value chain. 
 
To promote fair and decent working conditions, Genting Berhad supports responsible working hours, complies with statutory 
minimum wage requirements, and promotes fair and living wages in line with applicable labour laws and industry expectations. 
Mitigation measures including overtime controls, workload management and ﬂexible work arrangements, are implemented 
where appropriate to reduce the risk of excessive working hours. 
 
The Company respects freedom of association and the right to collective bargaining, as practised across its operations. 
Established mechanisms enable employee representatives and legally recognised labour unions to engage in regular and 
constructive dialogue with management, supporting the transparent identiﬁcation, escalation and resolution of workers’ 
concerns. 
 
These commitments are embedded within the Human Rights Policy and Supplier Code of Conduct, and are supported by audits, 
training programmes, supplier due diligence processes and conﬁdential grievance mechanisms. Genting Berhad reported zero 
substantiated cases of child labour , forced labour or major labour‑rights non‑compliance, reﬂecting effective oversight and 
implementation in 2025. 
 
OUR PERFORMANCE 
 
There were zero substantiated complaints concerning human rights violations recorded at the Company and Group level in 
2025.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 113
HUMAN RIGHTS AND LABOUR STANDARDS
Genting Plantations’ approach is to manage human rights in a structured and continuous manner across its operations and 
value chain. This includes adhering to applicable local, national and international standards while respecting the laws and 
constitutional rights of the regions where the company operates. Its Sustainability department oversees this effort by 
identifying relevant human rights risks, implementing appropriate controls and engaging stakeholders on community and 
labour rights to address issues and clarify expectations across the supply chain. 
 
Respecting the Rights of Indigenous and Local Communities 
 
Genting Plantations upholds both customary and statutory land tenure rights of indigenous peoples and local communities. 
Its approach aligns with the UN Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples, ILO Convention on Indigenous and Tribal 
Peoples (No. 169) and the application of Free, Prior and Informed Consent (“FPIC”). 
 
The company does not proceed with development or expansion unless FPIC has been secured from affected communities. 
FPIC is implemented through a structured process involving direct engagement with landowners, community members and 
appointed representatives using transparent communication channels. Engagement activities include  participatory mapping, 
Social and Environmental Impact Assessments (“SEIA”), High Conservation Value Assessments (“HCVA”), High Carbon Stock 
Assessments (“HCSA”) and land tenure studies, conducted in line with the RSPO FPIC Consent Guide and recognised best 
practices. Independent third-party observers are invited to observe the FPIC process and outcomes are validated through 
community meetings.

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025114
HUMAN RIGHTS AND LABOUR STANDARDS
Where land disputes arise, Genting Plantations will hold 
dialogues and seek out resolutions with affected parties. 
Compensation for loss of beneﬁts or relinquishment of rights 
is negotiated on a voluntary basis, with agreements ﬁnalised 
and endorsed by relevant local authorities. Disputes that 
remain unresolved are managed through established conﬂict 
resolution procedures that are aligned with social principles. 
Affected parties may also submit their grievances to RSPO. 
 
Genting Plantations conducts Social Impact Assessments 
(“SIA”) once every ﬁve years in accordance with the Malaysian 
Sustainable Palm Oil Guideline requirements (2025) to 
maintain engagement with affected communities and 
address identiﬁed community concerns. 
 
Genting Plantations integrates community impact 
considerations into its property development activities from 
planning through execution. Site selection assessments 
evaluate location suitability, including access to essential 
infrastructure and services. Engagement with local 
authorities and community representatives helps identify 
and manage social risks and opportunities associated with 
new projects. Where feasible, developments were 
undertaken within existing built-up areas and applied 
compact design approaches to manage land use and limit 
pressure on surrounding communities. 
 
Respecting and Upholding Employee Rights 
 
Genting Plantations prioritises the protection of employee 
rights across its operations by ensuring its employees 
operate in a safe and healthy working conditions, receive fair 
treatment and fair living wage. 
 
Its operations observe the following principles strictly: 
• Rights to Safe and Healthy Workplace 
• Rights to Decent and Fair Living Wages as well as Beneﬁts 
• Rights to Fair Working Hours 
• Rights to Fair Grievance Mechanisms 
• Rights to Freedom of Association and Collective 
Bargaining 
• No Discrimination 
• No Forced Labour in all forms 
• No Child Labour in all forms
No Forced, Human Trafficking and Debt Bondage Labour 
 
Genting Plantations prohibits all forms of forced labour, 
exploitation, human trafficking, harassment, abuse, contract 
substitution, debt bondage and the retention of identiﬁcation 
documents and has measures in place to safeguard 
employees' rights. 
 
The company provides secure storage for personal 
documents, manages all foreign-worker recruitment directly 
and at its own cost as well as ensures contracts are issued in 
English and workers’ native language. The company also 
assesses labour-related risks across its operations and supply 
chain, maintaining oversight of higher risk activities managed 
by third-party contractors, even though its operations are not 
located in areas identiﬁed as high risk. 
 
Certain measures implemented to minimise risk of forced 
labour within its operations, included: 
• strict Supplier Code of Conduct; 
• due diligence and audits; 
• ethical recruitment practices; 
• training and awareness; 
• grievance mechanisms; 
• collaboration with NGOs and industry initiatives; and 
• zero tolerance policy. 
 
Preventing Child Labour and Protecting Young Workers 
 
Genting Plantations prohibits all forms of child labour and 
does not permit young workers to be exposed to hazardous 
working environments. The company enforces a zero-
tolerance policy on child labour, which is communicated to 
employees, contractors and suppliers. 
 
The company partners with Humana Child Aid Society Sabah 
to support and push for education for plantation workers’ 
children and monitor school attendance to ensure they are 
not involved in labour work. 
 
Genting Plantations also mitigates child labour risks by 
conducting age veriﬁcation checks, compliance audits and 
engagement with workers’ families and local communities.

HUMAN RIGHTS AND LABOUR STANDARDS
Security Personnel Training in Human Rights  
 
Genting Plantations requires all auxiliary police to receive 
training on the company’s human rights policies with speciﬁc 
emphasis on their application in security-related situations. 
Its auxiliary police follow a strict code of conduct, which 
includes: 
• respect for human rights; 
• conﬁdentiality; 
• non-discrimination; 
• professional conduct; and 
• de-escalation. 
 
The Code of Etiquette also applies to third-party security 
providers. 
 
Auxiliary police duties include protecting company assets, 
employees and visitors, responding to security incidents, 
monitoring adherence to security protocols and supporting 
investigations into security breaches. 
 
Workers’ Rights to Freedom of Association and Collective 
Bargaining 
 
Genting Plantations manages risks related to freedom of 
association and collective bargaining through measures 
outlined in its Code of Conduct and Supplier Code, supported 
by periodic, risk-based assessments conducted as part of 
ongoing human rights due diligence. These assessments 
informed remediation actions, internal controls and 
escalation procedures, complemented by continuous 
monitoring and structured stakeholder engagement. 
 
To apply these requirements in practice, Genting Plantations 
provides training for employees and suppliers, including 
multilingual sessions for migrant workers, facilitates worker 
representation through inclusive committees and applies 
deﬁned protocols for collective bargaining where unions are 
active. Supplier audits verify compliance with labour 
standards and ethical recruitment practices, with corrective 
actions taken where needed. An anonymous grievance 
mechanism and transparent employment contracts further 
safeguard workers’ rights to organise and bargain 
collectively.
Supporting Workers with Fair Wages and Beneﬁts  
 
Genting Plantations provides fair compensation, essential 
support and reasonable working hours to meet employees’ 
basic living needs. Its practices are aligned with the RSPO 
Guidance on implementing a Decent Living Wage, ensuring 
workers and their families can maintain a respectable 
standard of living. Wages meet or exceed legally prescribed 
minimum requirements and this expectation is applied to 
suppliers to promote fair pay practices across the supply 
chain. In Malaysia, payslips are issued in English and the 
worker’s native language to ensure transparency and clarity, 
and all operating mills and derivatives comply with legal 
requirements on overtime pay and working hours. 
 
To apply these commitments consistently, Genting 
Plantations follows a wage-setting approach based on 
internal wage policies. These policies align with international 
and local labour standards, including those issued by the ILO 
and the legal frameworks of the countries where Genting 
Plantations operates. Key elements of this approach 
included: 
• monitoring local wages regulations; 
• internal policy compliance; 
• assessment of wage levels; 
• feedback and grievance mechanisms; 
• review and corrective action; and 
• working hours and overtime.

DIVERSITY, EQUAL OPPORTUNITY AND INCLUSION
WHY IT 
MATTERS
Genting Berhad recognises that workforce diversity, equal opportunity and inclusion are vital elements in fuelling the 
organisation’s productivity, innovation and cohesion. By encouraging a diverse and inclusive workplace, Genting Berhad 
ensures a dynamic blend of voices, experiences and perspectives among team members, stimulating innovative ideas 
and solutions. This diversity of thought enables the business to excel across various situations and challenges while 
enhancing its ability to understand and serve an equally diverse customer base. 
 
Achieving a fair workplace for long-term sustainability goes beyond compliance with legal requirements. It requires 
cultivating a culture where fairness and respect are embedded as core values, fostering higher employee satisfaction 
and productivity. Genting Berhad’s emphasis on equal opportunity and inclusion is a strategic approach that strengthens 
the company’s success and sustainability. Creating a work environment where every individual feels valued, respected 
and empowered contributes to the overall growth and performance of the Group, ensuring that employees can reach 
their full potential and drive collective success. 
OUR APPROACH 
 
Genting Berhad is committed to fostering an inclusive and non-discriminatory work culture on the basis of race, religion, 
gender , age, sexual orientation, disability, nationality or any other status protected by local law across all our operations. This 
commitment includes the following initiatives:
• selecting Board members and senior management by 
requiring individuals of high calibre with extensive 
knowledge, experience, expertise and skills; 
• outlining non-discrimination practices in our Code of 
Conduct and Ethics and the Human Rights section of this 
report, prohibiting all forms of workplace discrimination 
and ensuring every individual has an equal right and 
voice to contribute; 
• providing fair treatment and equitable opportunities to 
all employees regardless of their background across all 
stages of the human resources process, including 
recruitment, selection, retention, training and 
development, appraisal, promotion, succession planning 
and retirement; 
• embracing  inclusivity  and  diversity  by  ensuring all 
employees feel valued and heard and encouraging open 
dialogue where employees can express concerns or 
provide feedback; 
• ensuring fair payment through regular pay audits to 
guarantee  equal  compensation  for  equal work 
regardless of gender , race or other personal characteristics; 
• conducting  bias-free  hiring  and  evaluation using 
structured interviews, consistent evaluation criteria and 
diverse hiring panels to minimise unconscious bias in 
recruitment and evaluations; 
• maintaining an accessible grievance process to address 
concerns related to unfair treatment, harassment or 
discrimination, enabling timely and effective resolution; and 
• providing equal access to professional development and 
growth opportunities for all employees to support a fair 
and empowering workplace. 
These initiatives foster a collaborative environment and reinforce Genting Berhad’s reputation as a responsible and sought- 
after employer . By embedding fairness into our organisational culture, we enhance employee engagement, strengthen 
teamwork, boost productivity and improve our ability to attract and retain a diverse and talented workforce. 
 
The Group supports the community through the employment of under-privileged groups, including employees with disabilities, 
as well as initiatives to address youth unemployment, such as apprenticeships and graduate placement. Internship programme 
are organised by the Company and its subsidiaries to provide youths with valuable exposure to the inner workings of business 
operations and professional interactions.

Workforce Analysis and Planning 
 
Genting Berhad conducts regular reviews of organisational 
structure and job design to ensure roles are aligned with 
required competencies and business priorities. This approach 
enables greater ﬂexibility in role assignments, promotes 
optimal deployment of talent and supports workforce 
adaptability in a dynamic operating environment. 
 
Targeted Skill Assessment 
The Group will conduct targeted skill assessments, and 
structured manager feedback to evaluate existing 
competencies across the organisation. These assessments 
provide insights into current skill levels and inform 
development priorities at both individual and organisational 
levels. This proactive approach enables timely interventions 
through reskilling, upskilling and talent development 
initiatives. 
 
Job and Key Performance Matrix 
Following a comprehensive structure review exercise 
conducted in collaboration with respective departments, job 
matrix and key performance matrix have been developed 
according to job levels and functional requirements. These 
matrix provide clear expectations for performance, support 
consistent evaluation and serve as a foundation for career 
progression, capability development and succession 
planning. 
 
Talent Attraction and Acquisition   
 
The Group implements skill-based hiring practices by 
prioritising relevant skills and competencies alongside 
candidates’ experience and educational background. 
Recruitment decisions also take into consideration of 
candidates’ job preferences, to ensure alignment between 
individual capabilities and organisational needs.  
 
Strategic and Inclusive Recruitment 
Recruitment efforts focus on attracting candidates with the 
right mix of technical skills, competencies, and adaptability, 
while ensuring fair and transparent hiring practices. By 
aligning recruitment strategies with organisational priorities 
to ensure that new hires are well positioned to contribute 
meaningfully to business performance.

Values Aligned Hiring  
The Group emphasises value alignment in its hiring practices. 
Candidates are assessed on their alignment with the Group’s

employee engagement, and support long-term retention. Key 
employment values have been identiﬁed and incorporated 
into the Group’s employee development plans. This ensures 
that values-based behaviours are reinforced throughout the 
employee lifecycle, from onboarding and performance 
management to learning and career development. 
 
Talent Development and Succession Planning 
 
Genting Berhad recognises that continuous talent 
development and effective succession planning are critical to 
sustaining business performance and leadership continuity. 
The Group adopts a structured and forward-looking 
approach to developing employee capabilities while 
preparing a robust pipeline of future leaders. 
 
Upskilling and Reskilling 
The Group invests in upskilling and reskilling initiatives 
through a range of training programmes, including online 
learning courses, workshops and professional certiﬁcations. 
These initiatives enable employees to strengthen existing 
competencies and acquire new skills, ensuring they remain 
engaged, adaptable and responsive to evolving business 
requirements and technological advancements. 
 
Culture of Continuous Learning 
Genting Berhad promotes self-directed development by 
providing employees with access to learning tools and 
resources, as well as dedicated time for self-learning. This 
empowers employees to take ownership of their personal 
and professional growth while supporting continuous 
capability enhancement across the organisation. 
 
The Group supports job rotation and cross training 
opportunities to encourage employees to gain exposure to 
different roles and functions. This approach broadens skill 
sets, builds cross-functional understanding and equips 
employees with diverse perspectives, contributing to the 
development of a more versatile and agile workforce. 
 
Performance and Skill-Based Management 
Clear performance metrics are established in alignment with 
business objectives to drive career development and is 
guided by skill-based promotion practices. 
 
Career and development plans have been incorporated into 
the performance management system to empower 
employees to proactively manage their development, align 
personal growth goals with organisational needs and track 
progress systematically. 
 
The Group regularly evaluates the effectiveness of its talent 
development programmes to support leadership readiness 
and long-term business continuity. 
 
Succession Planning and Leadership Continuity 
Genting Berhad enhances leadership continuity through the 
continuous review and reﬁnement of its succession planning 
frameworks and processes. This structured approach ensures 
that succession planning remains relevant, forward-looking, 
and aligned with evolving business strategies and 
organisational priorities. 
 
High-potential talent is identiﬁed and developed through 
targeted development initiatives, including leadership 
development programmes, performance management 
processes and talent reviews. This approach to succession 
planning supports effective leadership transitions and 
reinforces the Group’s long-term sustainability and business 
continuity objectives. 
 
Talent Engagement and Mobility 
 
The Group adopts an integrated approach that empowers 
employees, optimises talent deployment and supports 
continuous learning and career progression to drive 
innovation, strengthening organisational capability, and 
ensuring long-term business sustainability. 
 
Talent Development and Continuous Learning 
Genting Berhad invests in structured learning and capability-
building initiatives, including technical training, leadership 
development programmes, online learning platforms, 
workshops and professional certiﬁcations. These programmes 
are designed to enhance job-related competencies, prepare 
employees for evolving roles and support future-ready skill 
development. 
 
In addition, the Group promotes self-directed learning by 
providing employees with access to learning resources and 
tools, enabling them to take ownership of their professional 
growth. Job rotation, cross-training and project-based 
assignments are also encouraged to broaden employees’ 
skillsets, improve cross-functional understanding and 
develop a more versatile and agile workforce. 
 
Employee Feedback and Engagement 
Regular feedback mechanisms are established to strengthen 
employee engagement and support continuous improvement. 
These include feedback solicited through performance 
review, stay interview, exit interview, structured discussions 
on skill development and career aspirations. Insights 
gathered from these channels inform talent and 
development strategies and enable alignment between 
individual goals and organisational priorities.

OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY AND HEALTH
WHY IT 
MATTERS
A safe and healthy workplace is fundamental to protecting our people, sustaining our operations and upholding our 
responsibilities as a Group. By fostering a proactive safety culture and embedding risk prevention into how we work, 
we safeguard our people, strengthen business continuity and enhance overall productivity as well as strengthen trust 
with our stakeholders. 
 
Our commitment to safety goes beyond compliance. Our goal is to operate in a “Zero Harm” environment—where risks 
are systematically identiﬁed and controlled, accidents are prevented, and safety is a shared responsibility across all 
levels of the organisation. 
 
Embedding safety and health practices into our operations demonstrates good governance, ensures regulatory 
compliance and supports long-term business resilience. Occupational safety and health ("OSH") risks for potential and 
new projects are evaluated during social due diligence, focusing on OSH compliance and safe working environment. 
These ﬁndings guide our team’s project planning, decision making and implementation of risk mitigation measures. 
403-1, 403-2, 403-4, 403-5, 403-6, 403-7, 403-9GRI
Policies & Procedures 
Developing, communicating 
and enforcing clear OSH 
policies and standard 
operating procedures to 
ensure consistent safety 
practices across all operations.
Active Participation & Oversight 
An Occupational OSH Committee is established, 
comprising representatives from both management 
and employees. The Committee meets on a 
quarterly basis to review safety performance, 
address identiﬁed risks and recommend 
improvement actions. Employee representatives 
also serve as Floor Wardens or First Aiders to provide 
on-the-ground safety support.
Risk Assessment & Hazard 
Identiﬁcation 
Systematically evaluating 
workplaces, processes and 
equipment to identify 
potential occupational hazards 
and implement preventive 
measures.
    OUR APPROACH 
 
At Genting Berhad, workplace safety and health is a shared responsibility, and we are committed to maintaining the highest 
standards through a structured and proactive approach. Guided by strict OSH policies, we work closely with our operational 
teams to ensure all activities align with industry best practices, aiming to minimise the risk of incidents or accidents.  
 
We are mindful of the prevailing health-related issues as such the COVID-19 pandemic, HIV/AIDS, tuberculosis, dengue and 
malaria, which may affect our employees and other stakeholders, including customers, business partners, suppliers and 
visitors. In addition, Genting Berhad addresses global health challenges, including non‑communicable diseases, through 
employee wellness and mental health programmes, alongside healthcare support, dementia care initiatives and health‑focused 
community investments across the communities in which it operates. 
 
Our comprehensive OSH approach includes:
Governance of Health and Safety 
 
Oversight of OSH is embedded within the Group’s governance structure. At Genting Berhad, our Board of Directors, led by the 
Executive Chairman, provides strategic oversight on health and safety matters, ensuring OSH is integrated into business 
strategy and decision-making process. Our directors are regularly brief by the Executive Committee on any key sustainability 
development that could impact the Company. The Executive Committee comprises senior management team and is headed 
by the Chief Executive Officer of Genting Berhad.

Safety Training & Awareness 
Equipping employees with the knowledge and 
skills to work safely, respond effectively to 
emergencies, and maintaining a safety-ﬁrst 
mindset.
Continuous Monitoring & Improvement 
Regularly reviewing OSH practices to ensure compliance, 
competency and effectiveness, and driving continuous 
improvement in line with emerging risks and industry 
standards.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 123
OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY AND HEALTH
Employee Wellness Programme 
As part of our ongoing commitment to building a safe and 
supportive work environment, Genting Berhad expanded its 
focus on holistic employee wellbeing in 2025. This includes 
mental, emotional and psychological health, in addition to 
physical safety. 
 
Genting Berhad launched the Genting Wellness and Wellbeing 
Programme, an initiative designed to strengthen physical and 
psychological resilience and promote a positive workplace 
health and safety culture in 2025. 
 
As part of this programme: 
• employees can access a range of health-related features 
through the BookDoc mobile application, supporting them 
in monitoring their wellbeing; 
• all employees are encouraged to complete the Depression, 
Anxiety and Stress Scale (“DASS-21”) psychological 
assessment, enabling them to better understand their 
levels of stress, anxiety and depression and to seek support 
when needed; 
• insights from the assessment guided the rollout of Mental 
Health Awareness Workshops, equipping employees  with  
knowledge  on  recognising mental-health symptoms, 
practising self-care and providing peer support to 
colleagues who may be experiencing emotional distress; 
and 
• continuous health-promotion activities including mental 
health awareness sessions, stress awareness sessions and 
ﬁtness activities which will be carried out to encourage 
healthy lifestyles. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
We also participated in the PERKESO Activ@Work Challenge 
to encourage healthy lifestyles through daily physical activity 
and healthier lifestyle habits. By creating a safe and 
supportive environment, we aim for sustainable growth and 
success for both our organisation and employees. 
 
These initiatives complement our broader OSH efforts and 
reinforce our commitment to fostering a workplace 
environment where employees feel safe, supported and 
empowered to prioritise their health and wellbeing. 
  
Health and Safety Training 
Genting Berhad provided regular OSH-related training to 
strengthen safety awareness and competency. In 2025, 161 
employees participated in various safety-related training 
programmes, including Basic Office Ergonomic, Initial 
Ergonomic Risk Assessment and Safety of Chemical Handling 
Training. Through these programmes, employees are 
equipped with the knowledge and skills required to identify 
workplace hazards, apply preventive safety measures and 
respond appropriately to potential risks, contributing to a 
safer and more resilient workplace.  
 
Our OSH management system fully complies with the OSH Act 
2022, including Section 15, which requires employers to 
ensure a safe working environment and Section 24, which 
obliges employees to prioritise their own safety and that of 
others. The system applies to all employees, activities and 
workplaces, reinforced by robust processes to maintain 
quality, competency and active participation at every level. 
  
By embedding these practices into our daily operations, we 
foster a “Zero Harm” culture that safeguards lives, enhances 
engagement, and strengthens operational resilience - 
ensuring that safety is not just a policy, but a way of working.

Mental Health Workshop 
 
Genting Berhad continued to prioritise employee mental well-being as a key component of our social sustainability agenda in 
2025. 65% of our workforce participated in the DASS-21 Survey, demonstrating a strong level of engagement and awareness 
around mental health matters.  
 
The survey ﬁndings provided valuable insights into employees’ mental and emotional well-being and served as a foundation 
for targeted interventions. Based on these ﬁndings, a series of mental health workshops were conducted and made accessible 
to all employees. The workshops aimed to equip employees with practical self-help strategies to better recognise, manage 
and cope with mental health challenges in both their professional and personal lives.  
 
Feedback received from participants was highly encouraging. Employees reported increased awareness, improved coping 
mechanisms and a greater sense of psychological safety at work. These outcomes reﬂect positive progress in nurturing 
healthier habits, strengthening employee engagement and fostering a more supportive and inclusive workplace culture.  
 
Mental Health Training Conducted
Looking Ahead 
 
Looking ahead to 2026, Genting Berhad remains optimistic and committed to advancing a more holistic, engaging and 
sustainable employee wellness journey. We will continue to strengthen our mental health initiatives while placing equal 
emphasis on physical health and nutrition, recognising their interconnected role in overall well-being. 
 
We look forward to continuing our collaboration with relevant partners and stakeholders to empower employees to prioritise 
their well-being, build resilience and thrive in a healthy, supportive and high-performing workplace.

Genting Malaysia Occupational Safety And Health Policy Statement
Genting Malaysia is committed to:
providing a safe and healthy workplace for all employees;
complying with relevant legislation and regulations and other OSH requirements;  
promoting a culture of safety through training, awareness and engagement;
identifying and mitigating workplace hazards; and
continually improving OSH performance through:
• resource optimisation;
• waste recycling and minimisation;
• good practicable means to reduce risk and ill health;
• eﬃcient reduction in accident rate;
• human resource development in training and competency skills; and

OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY AND HEALTH
OUR FEATURE    GENTING MALAYSIA (cont’d)
Malaysia 
 
Genting Malaysia maintains a structured OSH framework 
overseen by dedicated OSH Committee. The Committee 
provides governance, monitors performance and ensures 
the consistent application of safety policies across all 
operations. 
 
In 2025, Genting Malaysia further strengthened its OSH 
practices through a combination of targeted initiatives, 
including: 
• workplace safety audits; 
• employee safety training; 
• hazard identiﬁcation and risk assessments;  
• the organisation of OSH Week 2025; and 
• environmental, health and safety quarterly meeting 
attended by employees and employer representatives. 
 
Genting Malaysia is certiﬁed to ISO45001:2018, reﬂecting 
its commitment to a systematic and internationally 
recognised approach to occupational safety and health. 
OSH expectations are communicated to supply chain 
partners to promote consistent safety standards 
throughout the value chain. 
 
Work-related hazards are identiﬁed and managed through 
multiple channels, including: 
• regular inspections and risk assessments; 
• safety observation programmes; 
• incident and near-miss reporting; and 
• feedback gathered during safety brieﬁngs and OSH 
committee meetings. 
 
Insights from these processes strengthen controls and 
continuously improve the OSH management system. 
 
Employee well-being forms an integral part of Genting 
Malaysia’s safety approach. Employees have access to 
more than 35 panel clinics, health programmes and 
medical beneﬁts. In addition, the company conducted a 
Wellness and Wellbeing Programme to promote a healthier 
and more supportive workplace. Compliance with the 
Employment Act 1955 ensures that working hours, 
overtime and double-shift arrangements remain within 
legal limits, safeguarding employee welfare. 
CONTRACTORS SAFETY 
 
Genting Malaysia applies the same safety standards to 
contractors as to its employees, reinforcing a consistent 
and disciplined safety culture across all worksites. 
 
Key requirements include: 
• site registration with the Department of Occupational 
Safety and Health ("DOSH"); 
• mandatory participation in regular health and safety 
meetings; and 
• competency requirements, including valid CIDB 
registration. 
 
Safety violations are addressed promptly, with repeat 
offences subject to ﬁnes. Contractors are required to 
report all accidents and near-miss incidents to enable 
timely investigation and corrective action. This approach 
helps ensure a safe, compliant and well-managed working 
environment for all stakeholders.

United Kingdom 
 
Genting UK maintains a structured approach to health and safety 
through ongoing policy development, regular site-wide risk 
assessments and employee training on food safety, ﬁre safety and 
accident prevention. Consistent standards are reinforced through 
mandatory health and safety audits for maintenance providers and 
supply chain partners. Employee wellbeing is supported through 
targeted initiatives, including free annual night worker health 
assessments and access to occupational health services. Working 
hours are managed in line with the Working Time Directive, which 
limits average working week to 48 hours unless an employee has 
formally opted out.
Bahamas 
 
Resorts World Bimini prioritises team member 
safety, health and wellbeing through structured 
training, accessible healthcare and preventive 
initiatives, including annual First Aid, 
Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Automated 
External Deﬁbrillator training to strengthen 
emergency preparedness. The resort provides on-
site preventive health services such as free blood 
pressure and glucose screening through its 
Wellness Fair and enrols employees in the 
Bahamas National Health Insurance programme 
for primary healthcare access. 
 
To address limited dental services on the island, 
Resorts World Bimini fully funds a mobile dental 
van offering twice-yearly check-ups and 
treatment. Working hours are managed in 
compliance with the Bahamas Employment Act, 
ensuring no employee exceeds the daily or weekly 
limits without overtime compensation.
United States 
 
Genting Malaysia’s US operations promote a strong safety culture 
through comprehensive Occupational Safety and Health 
Administration (“OSHA”) training, regular Safety Committee 
meetings and specialised certiﬁcation for departments handling 
heavy-duty equipment. Workplace incidents are tracked using 
OSHA 300 Logs to support risk assessments and hazard-reduction 
efforts. Safety responsibilities are embedded across functions, 
supported by weekly site inspections and annual training led by 
Security, Facilities, Risk Management and Human Resources.
Genting Singapore established essential safety management systems, processes and a safety culture in alignment with the 
Workplace Safety and Health Act of 2006, guided by its Workplace Safety and Health (“WSH”) Policy and Framework. This WSH 
framework is underpinned by strong leadership from senior management and active engagement from all team members, 
ensuring shared accountability and safety awareness across the organisation. 
 
Genting Singapore maintained the ISO 45001 certiﬁcation accreditation for the resort in 2025. Coupled with the prestigious 
bizSAFE STAR status achieved at the highest level of the bizSAFE Programme, these accomplishments are a testament of its 
robust implementation of safety across the organisation.
ENHANCING WORKPLACE PRACTICES

=== PAGE 130 ===

GENTING BERHAD   |  SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025128
 
 
Its approach to workplace health and safety is driven by the following three key strategies:  
 
Effective Safety Governance: 
Resorts World Sentosa Board of Directors, supported by the Safety Steering Committee and Safety Working Committee, 
oversees the implementation of the Safety Management System for Genting Singapore. Its Safety Management 
Programme ensures that practical measures are implemented to identify, assess and mitigate hazards, prevent unsafe 
practices and reduce risks to the lowest levels possible. Under this programme, Genting Singapore ensures that its 
Business Units adhere to the ISO 45001-2018 Occupational Health and Safety Management Systems standard, which 
includes provisions for emergency preparedness addressing emerging workplace risks such as terrorism threats, disease 
outbreaks and mental health challenges. The company also conducts ad-hoc inspections to ensure that its contractors 
comply with established safety standards while providing their services. 
 
Fostering a Safety Culture: 
Genting Singapore encourages active participation from all team members in safety initiatives, including training, 
campaigns and incidents as well as near-miss reporting. To support these efforts, relevant departments have their own 
Safety Committee that engages team members to promote safe practices relevant to their roles and responsibilities. 
These committees conduct quarterly safety meetings and communication sessions with their respective heads of 
departments to ensure open dialogue and continuous safety awareness. They also conduct investigations into safety 
incidents and near-misses and ensure that risks are communicated and addressed effectively. 
 
Prevention Through Proactive Risk Management and Preparedness: 
Genting Singapore’s operations are required to adopt the Workplace Safety and Health (“WSH“) policy and framework to 
meet the local WSH legislative requirements. Adoption of Hierarchy of Controls to eliminate hazards systematically, 
ensuring both contractors and team members are actively engaged in this process. External contractors undergo 
mandatory safety induction, routine checks, and safety reviews to maintain high safety standards. Resorts World Sentosa 
adopts a risk-based approach that aligns with WSH (Risk Management) Regulations and Genting Singaporeʼs Enterprise 
Risk Management framework. This approach supports the Safety Management Systems by prioritising activities posing 
higher risks, ensuring they are addressed ﬁrst, followed by lower-risk activities. By adopting a risk-based approach, 
business units are empowered to assess their risks and identify areas for continuous improvement effectively. 
 
OUR PROGRESS 
Genting Singapore strengthened its workplace health and safety governance in 2025 by enhancing reporting practices 
to align  with GRI 403 and the Ministry of Manpower’s requirements. The company adopted a revised framework that 
expands its recordable injury count to include all cases including Outpatient or Hospitalisation Leave, Light Duty, Death 
and Occupational  Disease, offering more transparency and completeness. Genting Singapore remains committed to 
continually improving its  safety management systems and ensuring a safe and supportive work environment for all team 
members.

OUR FEATURE    RESORTS WORLD LAS VEGAS
OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY AND HEALTH
Resorts World Las Vegas is ﬁrmly committed to protecting the health and safety of its team members, contractors and the  
community. This commitment is reﬂected in the company’s Core Responsibilities which emphasise that safety is a shared  
responsibility across the organisation. The team works closely with federal, state, and local regulatory agencies to ensure full  
compliance with all applicable regulations. 
 
Resorts World Las Vegas’ Safety/Wellness Committee played a central role in advancing these efforts throughout 2025 by  
coordinating safety initiatives and partnering with the Communications team to deliver monthly safety and wellness updates 
to all  team members. The committee also collaborated with the Beneﬁts team to provide a range of onsite services including 
Access  Dental, Medicine on the Move, ﬂu shot clinics, Empower sessions and support from Beneﬁt Brokers. Additionally, an 
annual  beneﬁts fair was held during 2025 open enrollment period to help team members access to essential resources and 
information.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT 2025  |  GENTING BERHAD 129
OCCUPATIONAL SAFETY AND HEALTH
OUR FEATURE    GENTING PLANTATIONS
Genting Plantations is committed to minimising fatalities and high-consequence work related injuries, while targeting to 
achieve zero fatalities. Genting Plantations has a comprehensive Occupational Safety and Health Management System 
(“OSHMS”), which integrates safety protocols and procedures into operational controls. The OSHMS governs hazard 
identiﬁcation and risk assessments, safety training and inductions, emergency preparedness and toolbox brieﬁngs. It also 
covers workplace inspections and audits, incident reporting and investigation, Safety and Health Committee engagement, 
Personal Protective Equipment controls and health surveillance for higher-risk activities. 
 
DOSH conducted Occupational Safety and Health Act compliance audits at selected Genting Plantations operating units in 
Malaysia. These audits assessed compliance with applicable occupational safety and health requirements. Observations and 
recommendations arising from the audits were addressed through corrective actions in line with the OSHMS. 
 
Chemical Health Risk Assessments were conducted at selected operating units under the Property division, including the 
Genting Indahpura and Genting Pura Kencana sales offices in 2025. Conducted by a competent assessor , the assessment 
focused on the use of pesticides and fertilisers in landscaping and maintenance activities. Findings from the assessments 
informed the implementation of appropriate risk control measures in line with the OSHMS, including chemical handling 
procedures, training and monitoring arrangements where applicable. 
 
Occupational Safety and Health Performance 
Genting Plantations monitors its occupational safety and health performance based on work-related injury and incident data 
recorded in Malaysia and Indonesia. Its safety performance indicators include work-related injuries, fatalities and recovery 
time, while accident rates are calculated based on the total number of recorded cases and total hours worked, as per GRI 
reporting standard. 
 
Genting Plantations recorded ﬁve work-related fatalities in 2025, comprising one case in Malaysia and four cases in Indonesia. 
These incidents resulted in a combined fatality accident rate of 0.02 and a combined recordable work-related injury rate of 
6.18 across operations in both countries. The fatalities were caused by incidents involving falling objects, fall-related punctured 
trauma, drowning, fall-related laceration injuries and caught-in or between crushing hazards. The company has enhanced its 
safety protocols and reinforced safety reminders to further protect its workforce. 
 
The company will continue to strive for a “Zero Harm” workplace environment by complying with the OSHMS and enhancing 
its safety measures, procedures and protocols based on lessons learned. Its efforts will include advancing alignment with 
national OSH standards, lowering workplace incidents and fostering self-regulating practices. Going forward, Genting 
Plantations will reﬁne its OSHMS and enhance employee awareness of hazard and risk management.
OUR FEATURE    GENTING ENERGY
Genting Energy is ﬁrmly committed to safeguarding the health and safety of its employees, contractors and communities. This 
commitment is reﬂected in its stringent compliance with all applicable regulatory requirements governing the oil, gas and 
power sectors across its operations.  
 
The Kasuri block received ISO 45001:2018 certiﬁcation for its OHSMS, reﬂecting alignment with international standards and 
reinforcing systematic hazard management, regulatory compliance and continuous improvement of safety performance.  
 
The Banten power plant continues to uphold high occupational health and safety standards. In addition to maintaining its ISO 
45001:2018 certiﬁcation, the plant received a Certiﬁcate of Appreciation from the Indonesian Ministry of Manpower for 
achieving a 95.18% audit score at the Advanced level under the OSHMS assessment.  
 
Across all operations in 2025, Genting Energy recorded zero workplace injuries and zero work-related fatalities across all 
operations, maintaining a zero lost time incident rate. To further strengthen Genting Energy’s safety culture, 134 employees 
and 10,039 contractors participated in structured health and safety training programs during the year .
